# Audit of the papers and the formalization

Papers, with the versions audited:

- **ECToBlob**: J. A. Rhodes, E. S. Allman, C. Ané, H. Baños, *Statistical inference of the Tree of Blobs of a
  phylogenetic network from quartet concordance factors*,
  [bioRxiv 10.64898/2026.05.28.728501 v1](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.64898/2026.05.28.728501v1).
- **TOB-QMC**: J. Dai, Y. Han, E. K. Molloy, *Quartet-based species tree methods enable fast and consistent tree of
  blobs reconstruction under the network multispecies coalescent*,
  [bioRxiv 10.1101/2025.11.05.686850 v5](https://www.biorxiv.org/content/10.1101/2025.11.05.686850v5), with its supplement
  (internally dated February 26, 2026; its link to v5 is unconfirmed, and its numbering differs from v5's in places).
- **TINNiK**: E. S. Allman, H. Baños, J. D. Mitchell, J. A. Rhodes, *TINNiK: inference of the tree of blobs of a species
  network under the coalescent model*, [Algorithms Mol. Biol. 19 (2024), 23](https://doi.org/10.1186/s13015-024-00266-2).
- **Allman et al. 2023**: E. S. Allman, H. Baños, J. D. Mitchell, J. A. Rhodes, *The tree of blobs of a species network:
  identifiability under the coalescent*, [J. Math. Biol. 86 (2023), 10](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00285-022-01838-9), read in the
  PMC author manuscript NIHMS1884071 ([PMC10062380](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10062380/)) and the
  [arXiv TeX](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.03466). It is a primary paper here, and the three above also cite it.
- **Gusfield et al. 2007**: D. Gusfield, V. Bansal, V. Bafna, Y. S. Song, *A decomposition theory for phylogenetic networks and
  incompatible characters*, [J. Comput. Biol. 14 (2007), 1247–1272](https://doi.org/10.1089/cmb.2006.0137), read in the PMC author
  manuscript ([PMC2581772](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC2581772/)), and, for its symbols, an [author's copy](https://people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~yss/Pub/decomposition.pdf) on Yun Song's page.

Author TeX exists for Allman et al. 2023 only (arXiv). The others were audited against PDF text, publisher XML (which embeds TeX for TINNiK's equations), or the plain text of a PMC page. The journal's text of Allman et al. 2023 and of Gusfield et al. 2007 wasn't reachable: findings on them are about the author manuscripts, and Section 3 says where that matters. Line numbers are locators in those texts, as [`docs/CONFIGURATION.md`](CONFIGURATION.md) defines for each paper. The labels of the route check (`ecto:…`, `tob2025:…`, `tinnik:…`, `a:…`, `g:…`, `ext:…`) are this project's, except the `a:` labels of numbered results, which are the arXiv TeX's. Section, result and equation numbers are the papers'.

Status of the formalization:

- **Proved, by the papers' arguments** except for the departures of Section 7: every numbered result of ECToBlob
  (Theorems 3.1, 4.1, 8.2; Lemmas 3.5, 6.1, 8.1; Corollaries 3.6, 4.2, 4.3; Proposition 5.1; equations (3.2), (3.3); Remark 3.2), of TOB-QMC
  (Lemmas 1, 2, 3, 4; equations (1)–(3); Theorems 1, 2; Corollary 1; the ASTRAL corollary; the correctness of 3-fix-1-alter; the
  consistency claim of §4.3; supplement Lemmas 4, 8), of TINNiK (Propositions 5, 6; Appendix C; Theorem 4), of Allman et al. 2023 (Lemmas 1, 2, 3; Theorems 1, 2, 3, 4; Corollaries 1, 2; Proposition 1 and its converse) and of Gusfield et al. 2007 (Lemmas 1–5; Theorems 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 11; Corollaries 1, 2, 3; and the refutation of all four Full-Decomposition Optimality Conjectures).
- **Two numbered results of Gusfield et al. 2007 aren't proved as printed.** Theorem 4 is false as printed, by explicit networks (E42, E43), and its true form is proved, in both directions. Theorem 10 as printed has a notation slip, and it stays unproved and unrefuted in that literal form; it is proved with the rooted count, and for single crossovers with distinct columns (E50, E51). Lemma 2 of Allman et al. 2023 needs a word that it lacks, which all its uses satisfy (E55).
- **Also proved, from what the papers say in passing:** ECToBlob's observation in §3 on MQS and displayed trees, its Remarks 3.3, 5.2 and 8.3, and Remark 8.4 for binary networks, with a counterexample on a non-binary one (E35); the T3 option of TINNiK and TOB-QMC; TOB-QMC's bipartition search combined with 3-fix-1-alter; neighbor joining in TINNiK's Step 4; ASTRAL's dynamic program, exact on its search space; counts of operations behind the running-time claims of three of the papers; from ECToBlob's §10, the line test without the no-anomaly assumption, a random `T₀` and data splitting, and combining edge p-values; the remarks of Allman et al. 2023 listed in the [README of `src/AllmanEtAl2023/`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/README.md), including the examples of its Figures 3 and 9; and from Gusfield et al. 2007, the sentences at lines 135, 156, 180, 329, 403, 411, 418, 420, 554, 559 and 608 (lines 156, 180, 403 and 554 in the corrected forms of E46–E49), Footnote 2, and the numbers of Figures 5–10. Section 9 lists what remains.
- **Proved cited results**: Rhodes 2020 Theorem 2; Huber et al. 2025 Theorem 5.1,
  forward direction; Ané et al. 2024 exchangeability and a strict, quantitative form of the class-1 half of its Proposition 12; Mitchell et al. 2019 Theorem 3.1 for this model; NANUQ's Theorem 36, corrected (with its Proposition 9 proved for binary level-1 networks); that neighbor joining is exact on tree metrics (Saitou, Nei 1987; Studier, Keppler 1988); Buneman's four-point theorem; the optimality of ASTRAL's dynamic program (Mirarab et al. 2014); and, for Gusfield et al. 2007, the Perfect Phylogeny Theorem (Gusfield 1991), the Splits Equivalence Theorem (Semple, Steel 2003), that incompatible sites lie on one blob and the structure of reduced galled trees (Gusfield et al. 2004), the haplotype bound (Myers, Griffiths 2003), the Hudson–Kaplan bound, and the bound by ordered SPR, as Gusfield et al. state it (Song, Hein 2005). Section 2 says which of these sources were read.
- **Unconditional.** No result of the literature is assumed. ECToBlob's Assumption 1 and TOB-QMC's standard assumptions, 6a and T-quartet nonanomaly stay hypotheses of the theorems that state them, as in the papers.
- **Statements**: the corrections are listed in Section 4 and at the end of Section 6. Those to the numbered results of the first three papers were agreed with the user who requested this formalization. E35, E36, E37, the correction of NANUQ's Theorem 36, and E42–E66 came later, from reading and proving the later papers and what they say in passing, and are reported here for that user to read.
- `lake build` passes with no warnings, and there is no `sorry`, `axiom`, `native_decide` or `implemented_by` in [`src/`](../src). [`scripts/Audit.lean`](../scripts/Audit.lean) checks all 19,669 declarations of the libraries, including private and generated ones and those of the library [`lean4-phylogenetics`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics), against the standard axioms [`propext`](https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Init/Core.html#propext), [`Classical.choice`](https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Init/Prelude.html#Classical.choice), [`Quot.sound`](https://leanprover-community.github.io/mathlib4_docs/Init/Prelude.html#Quot.sound).
- [`src/MainResults.lean`](../src/MainResults.lean) states 39 main theorems, of all five papers, and proves them from the libraries. [`scripts/check_kernels.sh`](../scripts/check_kernels.sh) checks them under Lean's kernel and NanoDa, through Comparator's normal mode, and under con-ron, and all three accept them.
- No person has reviewed the proofs. Independent agents reviewed the statements and compared the proofs with the papers
  ([`CREDITS.md`](CREDITS.md)).

## 1. Summary

- **Errors.** ECToBlob's (3.3) overcounts (E2), and its Remark 8.4, worded as what the argument "indicates", fails on a non-binary network, by an explicit tree and `T₀` (E35). Its Remark 3.3 cites for "no anomalous quartets" a paper whose definition is non-strict, under which (3.1) and the conclusion of Theorem 3.1 fail on a null set of parameters (E36); the paper's own use is strict. The claims of TOB-QMC's supplement that its Lemma 7 and Theorem 1 hold at
  arbitrary level are false at level 2 (E25), its Lemma 2's converse is false (E23), and so is its claim that positive edge
  lengths exclude the centroid (E28). TINNiK has a misprint in the count of quartets sharing three taxa (E29), and its cut model is an open set on which the MLE need not exist at counts with a zero (E31); neither affects a result. Gusfield et al. 2007: Theorem 4 is false as printed, because the definition of "efficient" omits the node where a blob is entered (E42) and because it doesn't exclude the site-free arcs that line 97 allows (E43); it holds once these are repaired, up to two conventions (E44). The first repair is in print: Gusfield's 2014 book *ReCombinatorics* counts that node, in a stricter definition, and states the theorem with it, though in the snippets we could read it doesn't say that the 2007 definition fails (E42). Also false as printed: the characterization of the most tree-like network at line 373 (E45), line 554 on galled networks, probably `M` for `M + S` as at line 552 (E49), line 560 on visible nodes, which needs distinct columns (E50), the step at line 403 on one `M` (E48), and line 180's wording of "reduced" (E47). None of these touches Theorem 1 or the refutation of the conjectures. Allman et al. 2023: a side remark in the proof of Proposition 1 is false for some `ε` (E57). No numbered result of that paper fails, reading a subnetwork as connected; read literally, Lemma 2 does (E55).
- **Gaps.** The proof of ECToBlob's Theorem 8.2 has the most serious ones: the quartet witnessing a false edge may have a
  trivial 4-blob, where the T1 null holds (E12); this needed a new 1,100-line argument, [`ECToBlob.falseEdge_witness`](../src/ECToBlob/FalseEdge.lean#L1044), and
  changes no statement. Others: E1, E3, E4, E6, E7, E13–E17 (ECToBlob); E18–E22, E24, E39 (TOB-QMC); E30, E32, E33, E40 (TINNiK); E52, E53 (Gusfield et al. 2007); E56, E58–E62 (Allman et al. 2023). All are repaired, except that Theorem 8.2 stays on binary gene trees, so the displayed tree model on non-binary networks is not covered (E37). Only E22 changes a statement among these, the margin of supplement Lemma 4.
- **Missing hypotheses.** ECToBlob's Theorem 8.2 needs generic parameters for the literal general model (E10, agreed) and, for its Cauchy and CBC variants, positive alternatives (E11, agreed; E11 holds for Lean's continuous extension of the Cauchy transform at `p = 1`, where the printed formula is undefined). Lemma 2 of Allman et al. 2023 needs `M` connected (E55). Gusfield et al. 2007: see E42–E51. Section 4 has all of them.
- **Implicit conventions, notation slips and misprints.** E44, conventions of Gusfield et al. 2007; E43 and E46, a case line 97 allows and the statements must exclude; E51, its `R(M + S)` for the rooted count; E54 and E66, misprints in the two PMC manuscripts; E57, E63, E64, E65, smaller points in Allman et al. 2023, where E64 is an unstated qualification, "internal", on edges between trivial blobs; E34, the older ones.
- **Redundant hypotheses.** Section 5. The most notable: the disjointness of `A₁, A₂` and of `B₁, B₂` in Theorem 4.1 of
  ECToBlob; all the refinement hypotheses in TOB-QMC's Lemma 4 except [`TOBQMC.RefinesBlob`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L48); `0 ≤ δ` in TINNiK's Theorem 4; the binary hypothesis in Allman et al.'s Theorem 2 and Lemma 3.
- **Use of cited results.** TINNiK's Theorem 2 is applied beyond its printed hypothesis (b) (E30), TOB-QMC cites the statement
  of TINNiK's Theorem 4 for what is a step of its proof (E21), and ECToBlob's Proposition 5.1 adapts a theorem by "checking
  the argument", which turns out right (E5). Gusfield et al. 2007 restate the definition of a reduced galled tree too weakly (E47), and apply Theorem 4 where it doesn't hold (line 418, E53). Section 2 lists gaps in the proofs of cited results. Among results only cited, Theorem 36 of NANUQ is false as printed, at the level of the distance, and its proof picks the wrong level; Studier and Keppler's Lemma fails at polytomies, though their theorem holds.

## 2. Results from prior work and how the papers use them

Allman et al. 2023 is both a primary paper of this audit and what TINNiK, TOB-QMC and ECToBlob cite. Its own results, by its own proofs, are in [`src/AllmanEtAl2023/`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023), and its findings are in Section 3. The rows below on it are the forms the later papers use.

The rows from Gusfield 1991 to Song and Hein, except Semple and Steel's, are stated on the objects of [`src/Gusfield2007/`](../src/Gusfield2007), so [`src/External/`](../src/External) and that library import each other at the level of modules, with no cycle.

### What was read

| Source | Cited by | Read? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gusfield 1991 | Gusfield et al. 2007 | Not read: the publisher refused the download, and no open copy was found. The proofs are the standard argument, by a laminar family |
| Semple, Steel 2003, *Phylogenetics* | Gusfield et al. 2007, Allman et al. 2023 | Not read: a printed book, and no copy was looked for. The proofs are standard, and may differ from the book's |
| Song, Hein 2005 | Gusfield et al. 2007 | Not read: closed, with no open copy found. So whether its trees are binary, and how it defines an SPR, couldn't be compared. [`R_le_sprLength`](../src/External/SongHein2005/Bound.lean#L1315) has no binary hypothesis, so it covers Figure 10's trees either way. The proof is the standard construction of a recombination graph from marginal trees |
| Gusfield, Eddhu, Langley 2004 | Gusfield et al. 2007 | Read, in the authors' copy and not the journal's text |
| Myers, Griffiths 2003 | Gusfield et al. 2007 | Read: the journal's PDF, pp. 376–377, 379 |
| Hudson, Kaplan 1985 | Gusfield et al. 2007 | Read: the journal's PDF (a scan), p. 148, pp. 152–153, Appendix 2 |
| Huson, Klöpper 2007 | Gusfield et al. 2007 | Not read: closed. [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsNonDegenerate`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L45) comes from the authors' errata, and [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsGalled`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L41) from Definition 1 of Huson et al. 2009, which continues it and may be worded differently. So its Conjecture 1 wasn't read either |
| Bandelt et al. 1995 | Gusfield et al. 2007 | Read: pp. 745–748. They give the decomposition at cut nodes of the median network, and the proof here takes another route (E53, Section 7) |
| Baños 2019 | Allman et al. 2023 | Not read. The claim is proved here, by a mixture of two trees |
| Gusfield 2014, *ReCombinatorics* | Later than Gusfield et al. 2007, and not cited by a proof here | Only short snippets from Open Library's public search inside, and the front matter on the author's page. It bears on E42, E45 and E52 |
| Rhodes 2020 | TINNiK; Allman et al. 2023, for Theorem 4 | Read for TINNiK. For Allman et al.'s Theorem 4 the library's Steel theorem is used, so neither it nor Semple and Steel 2005 was read for that citation |

The other citations of the two new papers are for definitions or context, and no proof uses them: in Allman et al. 2023, Steel 2016, Solís-Lemus and Ané 2016, Xu and Ané 2021, Allman et al. 2011; in Gusfield et al. 2007, Gusfield 1997, Felsenstein 2004 and the works on programs (beagle, multicross.pl, Hudson's MS), whose numbers this formalization gets otherwise (Section 7).

### Assumed

Nothing. ECToBlob's adaptation of Allman et al.'s Theorem 1 is also proved: [`ECToBlob.adaptedAllmanTheorem1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section5.lean#L151), for its measure (`δ₀ + μ` on hybrid lengths) on networks whose blob vertices have degree at most 3. It goes beyond the source's proof, and the paper only sketches it ("checking the argument"). The witness lies on the face where all hybrid lengths are `0`, since under the degree condition the two cut arcs of the reduction can be taken to be tree arcs ([`AllmanEtAl2023.exists_reduction_of_blobDegree`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Reduction.lean#L951)). Assumption 1 of ECToBlob is the paper's own hypothesis of Theorem 8.2, not a cited result, and appears as [`ECToBlob.Assumption1Forward`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L205) and [`ECToBlob.Assumption1Converse`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L211).

[`External.Rhodes2020.exists_isExactBuilder`](../src/External/Rhodes2020/Theorem2.lean#L889) gives another |
| `G_S(M) = G(M + S)`, and that two blobs with an arc in common are equal | Gusfield et al., throughout | [`Gusfield2007.conflictGraph_eq_incompatGraph_addRow`](../src/Gusfield2007/Basic.lean#L71), [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsBlob.eq_of_inter_nonempty`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L35) |
| Parsimony score of a tree and re-rooting it at a row | Gusfield et al. Corollary 3, line 411 | [`Gusfield2007.MutationTree.parsimonyScore_row_le`](../src/Gusfield2007/Parsimony.lean#L335), [`Gusfield2007.parsimonyScore_eq`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L66) |

### Gaps in the proofs of cited results

Proving the cited results by their sources' arguments exposed these. All the results hold, except NANUQ's Theorem 36 as printed, and Studier and Keppler's Lemma at polytomies. The gaps of Allman et al. 2023 are E55–E66, since it is a primary paper.

- **Gusfield, Eddhu, Langley 2004.** The "general fact" in the proof of its Theorem 2.2, that two sites in conflict are together on a recombination cycle, asserts with no proof that an arc into a recombination node can be deleted so that the leaves keep their labels. The proof here doesn't need it: if every recombination node copies a parent on `{i, j}`, only three pairs occur; otherwise tracing the states up gives two cycles sharing an arc ([`GusfieldEtAl2004.exists_isBlob_of_incompatible`](../src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004/SameBlob.lean#L123)). It gives one blob, which is what 2007 cites, and not the source's stronger one cycle.
- **Myers, Griffiths 2003.** Their equation (4) counts all segregating sites. The form with distinct columns, which Gusfield et al. state at line 559, comes from their remark that the bound applies to any subset of sites. Their treatment is for single crossovers; line 559's "the same proof establishes" the bound for multiple crossovers is right ([`MyersGriffiths2003.haplotypeBound_le_R`](../src/External/MyersGriffiths2003/Bound.lean#L72)).
- **NANUQ (Allman, Baños, Rhodes 2019), Theorem 36.** False as printed at the level of the distance: line 2023 of our text says binary networks have `p_i ≠ 1/3`, but Definition 8 calls the centroid tree-like, and a binary level-1 network with a `3₂`-cycle has CF `(1/3, 1/3, 1/3)` at positive lengths ([`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.ThreeTwo.centroid_N₃`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/ThreeTwoCycle.lean#L1022)). There the star test rejects with probability tending to `β` only, so for no levels does the distance reach the theoretical one with probability tending to 1 ([`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.not_tendsto_of_centroid`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/NANUQ.lean#L1138), [`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.ThreeTwo.not_theorem36AsPrinted_N₃`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/ThreeTwoCycle.lean#L1087)). The formal statement [`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.Theorem36AsPrinted`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/NANUQ.lean#L1482) has the printed hypothesis and quantifiers, at `ρ = 0`, and concludes at the distance, which is the reduction its proof makes at the start, not the printed conclusion on the network inferred. On `N₃` that conclusion fails too, by an argument by hand, not in Lean: on the star class the distance is constant, and its circular decomposition has no split `ab|cd`. The theorem holds with no centroid CF, which holds off a null set ([`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.theorem36_generic`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/Proposition9.lean#L1635)). Its proof says "we choose `α_m` to be the minimum of the `α'_m`", which must be the maximum, since a test rejects when the p-value is below the level ([`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.exists_levels`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/NANUQ.lean#L667)); and `α'_m` is not a number, since the law of the test depends on the estimated `φ̂`. Steps 4 and 5, to the splits graph, are not formalized.
- **Studier and Keppler 1988, neighbor joining.** Their Lemma, read in a scan of the paper as saying that `S_ij` of neighbours is strictly least in its row and column, fails at a polytomy: on a 4-star with unit weights all `S_ij` are equal. Their theorem holds: [`Phylogenetics.NJ.exists_QC_lt_of_not_nbr`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/NeighborJoining.lean#L785), by a different comparison (Section 7).
- **Ané et al. 2024, Definition 2.** It calls a class-1 quartet anomalous when `P(T₁|N) < 1/3`, so that a CF at `1/3` is not anomalous, while TINNiK and TOB-QMC count it as anomalous. Consequences in E36.
- **Mirarab et al. 2014, ASTRAL.** As extracted, the recursion shows only `A' ∈ X`, while the prose requires `A - A' ∈ X` too; Lean requires both. This may be an artifact of extraction. The sources were read through a summarizing fetch, and the supplement with the proof of their Theorem 1 was not accessible, so [`Phylogenetics.IsTraceback.isAstralOptimal`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/AstralDP.lean#L1103) is not compared with that proof.

### Does each paper use each cited result correctly?

| Cited result | Where | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Huber et al. Theorem 5.1 | TOB-QMC Lemma 2 | Correct. The source treats binary semi-directed networks; the forward direction needs neither |
| Ané et al. exchangeability | TOB-QMC Lemma 3, ECToBlob §3, §5 | Correct |
| Allman et al. Theorem 1 | TINNiK Theorem 4 | Correct; it also excludes the centroid, since strictly cut and non-cut CFs have two different entries, so the separate analyticity argument is unnecessary (Section 11) |
| Allman et al. Theorem 2 | TINNiK Theorem 4 | Applied loosely: E30 |
| Rhodes 2020 Theorem 2 | TINNiK Theorem 4 | Correct. Pendant edges have weight at least 2 as well |
| Mitchell et al. Theorem 3.1 | TINNiK Prop. 5 | Correct for fixed φ; the "not too close to the boundary" remark is not needed (E32) |
| TINNiK Theorem 4 | TOB-QMC §4.3 | Applied loosely: E21 |
| Allman et al. Theorem 1, adapted | ECToBlob Prop. 5.1 | Correct. The paper only says "checking the argument"; the adaptation is proved here (E5) |
| Ané et al. Definition 2 and Example 1 | ECToBlob Remark 3.3 | Needs the strict reading: E36 |
| ASTRAL, its search space | TOB-QMC lines 518–519 | "defined by the bipartitions induced by the input gene trees" is exact for ASTRAL-I's default only; ASTRAL-II adds to them, which [`TOBQMC.isTraceback_refines_treeOfBlobs_of_superset`](../src/TOBQMC/Astral.lean#L219) covers. That ASTRAL-III always keeps every input bipartition was not confirmed |
| Perfect Phylogeny Theorem | Gusfield et al. line 264 | Applied loosely: it gives neither labelled internal edges nor the absence of nodes of degree two (E53) |
| Splits Equivalence Theorem | Gusfield et al. line 344 | Correct as a theorem; the splits it is applied to needn't agree (E42) |
| Gusfield et al. 2004, one blob | Gusfield et al. lines 182, 375 | Correct: the source's general fact gives more |
| Gusfield et al. 2004, reduced galled trees | Gusfield et al. line 180 | The definition is paraphrased too weakly, and the claim fails with the printed wording (E47). Correct with the source's |
| Haplotype bound | Gusfield et al. lines 559, 576–584 | The bound is correct; the equality case drawn from its proof needs distinct columns (E50) |
| Hudson–Kaplan bound | Gusfield et al. lines 594, 643 | Correct |
| Song, Hein 2005 | Gusfield et al. line 654 | The statement as Gusfield et al. give it is proved; the source wasn't read |
| Bandelt et al. 1995 | Gusfield et al. line 411 | A fair pointer: pp. 745–748 give the decomposition at cut nodes of the median network. Neither paper writes the link from cut nodes to the components of `G(M)` (E53) |
| Huson, Klöpper 2007 | Gusfield et al. line 554 | The sentence on them is false as printed, and true with `M + S` (E49). Its source wasn't read |
| Huson, Klöpper 2007, Conjecture 1 | Gusfield et al. line 590 | Not checked: the statement of that conjecture wasn't read, so this project can't confirm that the refutation applies to it (Section 9) |
| Baños 2019 | Allman et al. after Proposition 1 | The claim is true; the source wasn't read |
| Semple and Steel 2005, Rhodes 2020 | Allman et al. Theorem 4 | Correct: the conclusion is the library's [`Phylogenetics.mem_of_forall_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Splits.lean#L649) |

## 3. Errors and gaps in the papers

### ECToBlob

**E1. Remark 3.2.** "Any multifurcation in a potential maximizer can be arbitrarily resolved to increase the MQS score" is
loose when some probabilities vanish: an arbitrary resolution need not increase the score. Some resolution with positive
probability does, since the three probabilities on a quartet sum to 1. [`ECToBlob.remark3_2`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L569) proves the remark.

**E2. Equation (3.3) is false as printed.** Summing over representative tuples counts a quartet once for each choice of
representatives of the other blocks, so a quartet in distinct blocks is overcounted by the product of the sizes of the
unused blocks. With blocks of sizes `(2,1,1,1,1)` and all cross-block CFs `1/3`, the left side is `3` and the right side `10/3` on a binary `T`
([`scripts/SourceChecks.lean`](../scripts/SourceChecks.lean)); a multifurcating species tree with one positive-length cherry realizes these CFs. This refutes
the identity, not Theorem 3.1. Repair, along the paper's route: weight each local term by the product of its four block sizes,
divide by `∏ᵢ |Xᵢ|`, and use the weighted local objective for the splicing. Lean: [`ECToBlob.equation3_3`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L86), [`ECToBlob.localWeight`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L187),
[`ECToBlob.theorem3_1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L488). The identity also needs the `Xᵢ` to cover `X`, as the paper's do. The side claims at lines 497–500 and 504–507, and lines 501–504 (a splice of maximizers maximizes `f_m`), are false for the printed, unweighted `f̃_m`: with block sizes `(4,1,1,1,1)` and abstract weights, the maximizers of `f_m` and of the unweighted `f̃_m` can differ (not shown realized by a network). All hold for the weighted objective. Lines 461–463 also use, unstated, that the quartets a tree displays determine its splits ([`Phylogenetics.subset_of_forall_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Splits.lean#L748)), and lines 504–507 that a side of a maximizer outside `T_B` refines one multifurcation ([`Phylogenetics.exists_mem_multifurcations_isBlockUnion`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Detach.lean#L230)).

**E3. Corollary 3.6, proof.** "The probability that `f̂` and `f` have different sets of optima → 0" is false when `f` has
ties: `f(a)=f(b)=0`, `fₙ(a)=1/(n+1)`, `fₙ(b)=0` converges uniformly with different argmax sets for every `n`, and ties do
occur here. The result holds: the empirical optima lie among the expected ones, by the gap to non-maximizers, which is what
the corollary says. Lean: [`ECToBlob.corollary3_6`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L1483).

**E4. Theorem 4.1, proof.** "The minimality of `|E|` implies that the funnel of `a₁` can only attach to `G̃₃` on the
`ã₂`-side" is elliptical: it needs the comparability of the sets `E(z, …)` of cut edges for different replacements. Going from
one funnel to all of them at once needs that funnels on opposite sides of an edge share no node, which is true and unstated. The "two
3-blobs" and "chain of 2-blobs" remarks are used only in part: the chain on `G̃₃` gives the comparability ([`ECToBlob.quartetCuts_subset_or_subset`](../src/ECToBlob/Section4Funnels.lean#L1204)), and funnels on opposite sides share no node ([`ECToBlob.Induces.attach_funnels`](../src/ECToBlob/Section4Funnels.lean#L301)).
Also unstated: that the `ã₂`-side is the component off the arc of `E` nearest `ã₂`, and that `b̃₁`, `b̃₂` lie in one component of `G̃₃ \ E`. The result holds.

**E5. Proposition 5.1.** Its proof rests on an adaptation of Allman et al.'s Theorem 1 stated by "checking the argument", and on atoms at hybrid length 0 that the source's measure doesn't have. The sketch is filled in here, and the claim holds, including on the face where all hybrid lengths vanish ([`ECToBlob.adaptedAllmanTheorem1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section5.lean#L151), [`ECToBlob.proposition5_1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section5.lean#L184)). The two other facts are proved for every network: [`Phylogenetics.NMSC.cut_alternatives`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Exchangeability.lean#L550), [`Phylogenetics.NMSC.trivial_uniform`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Exchangeability.lean#L771).

**E6. Lemma 6.1, proof.** `Bon(P) = km` omits the clipping at one in the paper's definition: `{3/4, 3/4}` gives `Bon = 1`, not
`3/2`. The inequality needed still holds. Lean: [`ECToBlob.bonP_le_mul`](../src/ECToBlob/Section6.lean#L144).

**E7. §6 and Lemma 6.1: Cauchy endpoints.** The Cauchy combination is undefined at `p = 0`, an allowed input by `P ⊂ [0,1)`, and CBC
can feed `Bon = 1` into it. Lean's total tangent is not a reading of the undefined expression, so the Lean definitions
extend `f` continuously at the endpoints. Simultaneous inputs `0` and `1` have no continuous extension, but this case does not
occur on the lemma's domain: `Bon = 1` forces all inputs positive. Lean: [`Phylogenetics.cauchyP`](../src/Basic/Testing/Combining.lean#L40), [`ECToBlob.cauchyP_pair_le_max`](../src/ECToBlob/Section6.lean#L182). E11 and the value of CBC in E15 rest on this choice.

**E8. Lemma 6.1, part 1: the domain of the bound.** The lemma gives no domain for `B`, and evaluates it only at `(min P, max P)`, so `η ≤ D`. There the printed `B_{Cauchy,k}(η, D)`, with `f` extended at `0` as in E7, has all the claimed properties, and this is all that Theorem 8.2 uses. Off that domain it does not: it is not non-decreasing in `k` when `η > D` (at `η = 0.9`, `D = 0.1` it is `0.9` at `k = 1` and `0.5` at `k = 2`), and it fails `∼ kη` at `D = 0`. Lean states the lemma on `0 ≤ η ≤ D < 1` with the printed bound ([`ECToBlob.lemma6_1_part1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section6.lean#L539), [`ECToBlob.cauchyBound`](../src/ECToBlob/Section6.lean#L274)).

**E9. Algorithm 7.1, Step 10.** The late rule needs a convention when no iteration is rejected. Lean chooses the first iteration, which agrees
with the rule's intent ([`Phylogenetics.selectedIteration`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L97)).

**E10. Theorem 8.2: missing genericity.** The theorem quantifies over any model meeting Assumption 1, whose converse clauses
hold only generically, while the proof invokes "our assumption of generic numerical parameters". For the literal general model
the statement is false: a one-parameter family of laws on a rooted 4-cycle network (Section 4) coincides at one parameter with the law of a
resolved tree, and no sequence of tests can converge to both. This is not a counterexample under the NMSC. Lean quantifies over
parameters meeting both clauses: [`ECToBlob.theorem8_2`](../src/ECToBlob/Section8.lean#L725), and [`ECToBlob.theorem8_2_generic`](../src/ECToBlob/Section8.lean#L762) for the generic form. Corrected as the user
agreed, 2026-10-27.

**E11. Theorem 8.2: boundary laws.** Assumption 1 allows both alternatives of a quartet with a cut-edge split to have probability zero, since (5.2) is a condition on `p₁ > p₂ = p₃` with no positivity, though the source's T1 null is `p₁ ≥ p₂ = p₃ > 0`. Counts on such a quartet are `(n,0,0)` almost surely, so its T1 statistic is `0` and its p-value is exactly `1` for every `n` ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.t1PValue_degenerate`](../src/Basic/Testing/Limits.lean#L332)). The proof does not cover this: Lemma 6.1 assumes `P ⊂ [0,1)`, and "individual p-values `≤ 1-δ`" fails. The printed Cauchy formula is undefined at `p = 1`, so what the theorem says here depends on how `f` is extended. With Lean's continuous extension (E7), `Cauchy` of positive p-values containing `1` is `1`, and the theorem fails for Cauchy and CBC, as follows. With a finite value of `f(1)`, as in the authors' R code (`tan((0.5-1)π)` is finite in floating point), Lemma 6.1 holds at `D = 1`, the printed proof goes through, and the output on the example below is correct.

Counterexample: the rooted 5-sunlet with arcs `r→v_b, r→v_c, v_b→v_a, v_c→v_d, v_a→h, v_d→h, h→e, v_a→a, v_b→b, v_c→c, v_d→d`, whose tree of blobs is the five-leaf star, under the Displayed Tree model with weights `γ` on `((a,e),b,(c,d))` and `1-γ` on `((a,b),c,(d,e))`. On `abcd`, the
network has the cut split `ab|cd` and the CF is `(1,0,0)`; the other four quartets have CFs `(γ, 1-γ, 0)` up to order, and no cut split or trivial 4-blob. Both directions of Assumption 1 hold, for all `γ ∈ (0,1)`. Take `T₀` with cherries `ab`, `cd`, a resolution of the star. The star test keeps `T₀`, since these CFs are not uniform; `T₀` displays `ab|cd`, so its overall
Cauchy p-value is `1 ≥ α_n`; so for all `α, β` both selection rules return `T₀`, which is not the star. Bon and BBC are not affected, since clipping loses the `1`. A mixture with weights `δ, 1-δ` of a point mass at `T₀` and the uniform law on all 15 binary trees shows that `-log β_n = o(n)` for any
sequence working on trees, so the failure does not depend on the choice of sequences. An independent reader recomputed the law, the network's predicates and Assumption 1, and simulated the Lean definitions on this example and on a second law with the same CF on `abcd` at `n = 500, 5000, 50000`: Cauchy and CBC returned `T₀` in every replicate, and Bon and BBC returned the star at `n ≥ 5000`.

The NMSC has positive CFs ([`Phylogenetics.nmscCF_pos`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Basic.lean#L96)), so the paper's main model loses nothing. Lean adds the hypothesis `hpos` to [`ECToBlob.theorem8_2`](../src/ECToBlob/Section8.lean#L725), for these two variants only and on T1-strict quartets only. So `hpos` is what Lean's extension needs; a finite `f(1)` would need no such hypothesis. The user agreed to this correction on 2026-10-31.

**E12. Theorem 8.2, proof: the witness may be a trivial 4-blob.** "By Corollaries 4.2 and 4.3, `S^a(e)` is empty exactly when `e` is a cut-edge" fails for non-binary
networks: the quartet that those corollaries find may have a trivial 4-blob, on which the T1 null hypothesis holds (`CF = (1/3,1/3,1/3)` is on every T1 null).
So those corollaries do not show that a false edge has a T1-rejecting quartet. The statement holds. Repair: an edge surviving Step 3 has a quartet with no trivial 4-blob in its
multipartition, and from it [`ECToBlob.falseEdge_witness`](../src/ECToBlob/FalseEdge.lean#L1044) produces a quartet in the multipartition of `T₀` with neither a cut-edge split on the wanted bipartition nor a
trivial 4-blob, for all three quartet types at every stage. The formal proof starts from the paper's witness ([`ECToBlob.Deterministic.exists_not_inducedCutSplit_edgeQuartets`](../src/ECToBlob/Deterministic.lean#L376)); the trivial 4-blob is the case that needs the repair ([`ECToBlob.Deterministic.Run.exists_not_t1Null_of_witness`](../src/ECToBlob/Deterministic.lean#L399)). Logically Corollary 4.2 is then redundant (Section 11).

**E13. Theorem 8.2, proof: T1 at the centroid.** "Arguing as for the star test" is not enough: T1 p-values are not asymptotically uniform at the centroid (the paper's own
line 1017 notes this), so Lemma 8.1 does not apply there. Repaired by tightness ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.t1PValue_centroid_tight`](../src/Basic/Testing/Limits.lean#L370)) and a union bound.

**E14. Theorem 8.2, proof: the threshold.** `B(η_n, 1-δ) ∼ β_n` does not give `< β_n`. Repaired with a constant: `η_n = c₀ · level` ([`ECToBlob.Deterministic.exists_combineBounds`](../src/ECToBlob/Deterministic.lean#L660)). Only
`b_n = o(n)` is used, not `b_n ∼ log n`, and the asymptotic of `α_n` is unused. "An individual p-value is below `η_n` exactly when the test statistic is above `b_n`" holds for the star test, whose test law is fixed, and "the T1 tests are handled similarly" does not carry it over:
the T1 law depends on an estimated `μ`. Lean bounds the T1 p-value by the `χ²₂` tail at the statistic: with `Z̄ = Z̄₀ + μ`, for `μ ≥ 0` the extra term `½(1 - sgn Z̄)Z̄²` is at most `Z̄₀²`, so the variable is at most `Z² + Z̄₀²` ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.t1Tail_le_chiSqTwoTail`](../src/Basic/Testing/Tails.lean#L81)).

**E15. Theorem 8.2, proof: bounds on the combined p-values.** "In `(α_n, 1-δ)`" is false on the upper side for Bon, BBC and CBC, even with positive alternatives: `Bon({3/4, 3/4}) = 1`, and then `BBC` is `1`, and so is `CBC` under the extension of E7. Individual p-values `≤ 1-δ` is false when alternatives vanish (E11). Only the
lower bounds are needed, and the upper individual bounds only for Cauchy and CBC. Lean uses [`ECToBlob.minP_le_combineP`](../src/ECToBlob/Section6.lean#L234) and [`ECToBlob.combineP_le_two_mul`](../src/ECToBlob/Section6.lean#L249) for Bon and BBC, and Lemma 6.1 for Cauchy and CBC.

**E16. Theorem 8.2, proof: intermediate trees.** The bounds `ε/(2s)` are per edge, but for type `mul` an edge's quartets depend on the random current tree `T_k`, so the union must
run over all states the algorithm could visit. Likewise "our prior choice of `M`" does not cover the sets that the overall `p_k` uses. The formal proof keeps the paper's test-by-test bounds with one test for each pair of a subset `T ⊆ T₀` and an edge of `T`, and one overall test for each `T`, and their number `K` replaces `s` ([`ECToBlob.eventually_t1_all`](../src/ECToBlob/Section8.lean#L569)).

**E17. Theorem 8.2, proof: Step 3.** "`T_{k₁}` is obtained from `T₀` by contracting precisely those edges that resolve multifurcations" is false as written: on a 4-cycle, `ab|cd` resolves a multifurcation of the tree of blobs and survives Step 3, since its quartet is not uniform. The formal
proof shows what is needed: `T_B` is kept and each kept edge has a non-uniform quartet ([`ECToBlob.Deterministic.Run.treeOfBlobs_subset_afterStar`](../src/ECToBlob/Deterministic.lean#L288), [`ECToBlob.Deterministic.Run.exists_not_uniform`](../src/ECToBlob/Deterministic.lean#L305)).

### TOB-QMC, main text (v5)

**E18. Lemma 3, proof.** The result holds; the steps below are repaired in [`TOBQMC.lemma3_of_properties`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L619), [`TOBQMC.lemma3`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L678), and in the proof of exchangeability.

- Lines 449–451: "uniquely maximized by `T_N`" is wrong: every refinement of `T_N` ties ([`Phylogenetics.quartetPart_of_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Detach.lean#L587)); (2) only compares with non-refinements. The strictness of (2) needs that a non-refinement differs from `T_N` on some T-quartet, so that displayed quartets determine splits ([`Phylogenetics.subset_of_forall_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Splits.lean#L748)).
- Lines 467–471: "only after the four sampled lineages enter this blob can they coalesce" is false for the block containing the root: the three other lineages can coalesce inside the blob and then leave upward. The conclusion holds for a different reason: a pair that coalesces first decides the quartet whichever taxon is above, and if all three leave uncoalesced the three quartet trees are equally likely. [`Phylogenetics.NMSC.exchangeability`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Exchangeability.lean#L412) treats both cases.
- Lines 472–486: the reasons (i), (ii) justify `QS^v(new) = QS^v - QS^{v(b)} + QS^{v(a)}`, not the printed equality with `QS^v(T*)`; quartets without `b` are unaffected, and those with `b` read as the same with `a`, because `b` is now sister to `a`. These last facts are unstated, and the case `QS^{v(a)} > QS^{v(b)}` cannot occur at a maximizer.
- Line 485, "Repeating": needs three unstated facts: detaching `b` leaves the partial scores of the block's other taxa unchanged, so `a` stays best; the block side grows step by step; and earlier block sides
  survive later operations ([`TOBQMC.vertexTaxonPart_detachReattach_of_ne`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L331), [`Phylogenetics.insert_mem_detachReattach`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Detach.lean#L536), [`Phylogenetics.mem_detachReattach_of_not_mem`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Detach.lean#L549)). Also, `a` must be chosen afresh for each block, on the current tree.
- Lines 489–492: the reattached tree is a tree only because of property (2), since a side of the refined tree is either a lift or lies inside a block, which is unstated ([`Phylogenetics.reattach_isUnrootedTree`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Splice.lean#L723)). That a refinement gains needs that displayed quartets determine splits. "Amplified" has no single factor: the gain on `X` is the sum over representative tuples of the gains, divided by `∏|B|` ([`TOBQMC.prod_card_mul_vertexPart_sub`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L493)).
- Lines 492–495: property (2) is what forces every remaining side to be a union of blocks ([`TOBQMC.displays_filter_isBlockUnion`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L578)), which is not noted.
- Lines 497–498: compatibility of the local trees at different vertices is asserted ([`Phylogenetics.IsVertexPartition.compatible_of_ne`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Splice.lean#L78)), and that the result is binary needs a proof ([`Phylogenetics.isBinaryTree_of_forall_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Detach.lean#L177)).

**E19. Lemma 4, proof.** Of ⇒ (lines 551–556): It does not treat two or more taxa below `v_h`. They lie in one block, a fact stated only in the proof of Corollary 1, so distinct blocks exclude this case. Of ⇐ (line 546): it stops at "these conditions are sufficient" without saying why the cycle with its attachments has no 2|2 cut edge. A cut edge isn't on the cycle, so two taxa on its far side would attach through it, contradicting distinct blocks ([`TOBQMC.Level1.not_hasWedgeCutSplit`](../src/TOBQMC/Wedge.lean#L1844)).
Lean: [`TOBQMC.Level1.blockWalk_of_below_reticulation`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1Blobs.lean#L902). The result holds.

**E20. Corollary 1, proof (lines 567–568), and Theorem 2, proof (lines 583–584).** "(otherwise `B_N` is not binary)" gives the wrong reason for each side having at least two blocks: a side with one block is a cut side, so the edge would be in the tree of blobs. Binary `B` is not needed. In Theorem 2, "By the setting" each block lies in a single block of the quadripartition also needs `e ∉ T_N`, for the same reason ([`TOBQMC.Level1.exists_not_isTreelike`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L635)). That an edge of `B_N` not in `T_N` refines one vertex of `T_N`, from a non-trivial blob (lines 530–531, 563–564), is also taken "by the setting": a lowest bridge with taxa of both sides below it gives the vertex, and a trivial blob has too few children for four blocks ([`TOBQMC.exists_blob_of_false_positive`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L507)). The result holds.

**E21. Consistency (lines 597–600).** The paper cites TINNiK's Theorem 4 as stating that the tests are correct on all four-taxon subsets. Its statement is about the algorithm's output, and this fact is a step of its proof, for generic parameters at `ρ = 0`, so it doesn't apply as cited under v5's assumptions. The fact does hold under them, for reasons the paper does not give: non-treelike subsets by 6a; subsets the tree of blobs resolves by nonanomaly and exchangeability; and for a treelike subset the tree of blobs does not resolve, a highest separating arc is not a bridge, and its source is a fork where the rest of the process is symmetric, so the split's CF is strictly largest ([`TOBQMC.Level1.nmscCF_lt_of_not_displays`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1CF.lean#L174), from [`Phylogenetics.NMSC.nmscCF_lt_of_fork`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/TreeCF.lean#L451)). Such a subset need not induce a tree: a reticulation above the fork can stay in its induced subgraph, with three lineages through it. So at level 1, T-quartet nonanomaly implies condition 6b, and [`TOBQMC.Level1.cfDetectable`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1CF.lean#L197) gives CF-detectability under v5's assumptions. The step from Theorem 1's almost-sure "for all large `k`" to probability tending to one is unstated. Lean: [`TOBQMC.consistency`](../src/TOBQMC/Consistency.lean#L131), [`TINNiK.tests_correct`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L538), and the private `tendsto_optimaRefine` of [`src/TOBQMC/Consistency.lean`](../src/TOBQMC/Consistency.lean).

**E22. Margin (lines 503–505, and supplement Lemma 4, Eq. (1)).** "`π` is the minimum absolute difference between the qCFs of T-quartets" is always zero: by exchangeability every T-quartet has equal alternatives, so `π(Y) = 0`, and the supplement's "`π_min > 0`" is false for every network whose tree of blobs is not a star. The condition "absolute error `< 0`" is impossible, so Lemma 4 as printed is vacuous. Repair: use the positive differences between the displayed quartet and each alternative, minimized over T-quartets, with the same radius. Lean: [`TOBQMC.Margin`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L691), [`TOBQMC.exists_margin`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L857).

### TOB-QMC, supplement

**E23. Lemma 2: false converse.** A treelike induced quartet can restrict a star on the full tree of blobs: the network with arcs `s→e, s→r, r→u, u→v, v→h, r→w, w→h, u→a, v→b, w→c, h→d` has
a five-leaf star as tree of blobs, while its restriction to `{e,a,b,c}` displays `ab|ce` ([`scripts/SunletCheck.lean`](../scripts/SunletCheck.lean)). The proof of the converse in fact reproves the contrapositive of the forward direction. Main v5 keeps only the forward direction, as does Lean ([`TOBQMC.lemma2`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L34)). Agreed with the user.

**E24. Lemma 4, proof.** Strict coordinate errors are weakened to non-strict bounds, after which strict positivity is claimed at the boundary radius; keeping them strict repairs this at the same radius. Equations (6)–(7) must cancel agreeing quartet
terms before identifying the sums. The final display has `2|Y_tree|δ` for `2|Y_star|δ`. `B*` is "some tree that maximizes `QS_E`", and then `QS_E^star(B*) ≥ QS_E^star(T)` is taken from Lemma 5, which gives this for the constructed tree; it holds for any maximizer, since the constructed tree attains both maxima, but that needs saying. Lean: [`TOBQMC.supplementLemma4`](../src/TOBQMC/WQC.lean#L707).

**E25. Lemma 7 and Theorem 1 at arbitrary level: false.** The supplement's claims extend to networks of arbitrary level that a disjoint cover finds a non-treelike quartet around every false edge. On the binary level-2 network with arcs `(0,2),(1,3),(1,4),(3,6),(2,8),(6,9),(6,10),(9,12),(2,13),(13,7),(3,14),(14,5),(13,14),(0,15),(15,1),(9,16),(16,11),(15,16)`,
leaves `4,5,7,8,10,11,12`, the 3-fix-1-alter cover with blocks `{10},{7},{4,5,8},{11,12}` and anchors `10,7,8,12` has every quartet treelike, though the edge is false. Main v5 restricts Theorem 2 to level 1, so this is consistent with it. This concerns covers, not an oracle on all quartets around an edge (Section 10).

**E26. Lemma 8: missing hypothesis.** A trivial blob need not contain a reticulation: the singleton blob at the parent of `a, b` in `((a,b),(c,d))`. The intended statement requires a nontrivial blob, as agreed with the user. Only used through level-1 arguments in Lean: [`TOBQMC.Level1.exists_reticulation_or_forall_isBridge`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1Blobs.lean#L353).

**E27. §1.2.** The initial `α = ρ/(1-ρ)` conflicts with the later `ρ = 1/(α+1)` and the stated endpoints; the consistent form is `α = (1-ρ)/ρ`. Lean: `Phylogenetics.concentration_relation`, [`Phylogenetics.urnStep_eq_concentration`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Inheritance.lean#L103).

**E28. "We can safely assume" no centroid (supplement line 300).** Positive tree edge lengths do not exclude `P = (1/3,1/3,1/3)`: the `3₂`-cycle at `t = t₁ = t₂ = 0.02`, `γ = 1/2`, `t₃ ≈ 0.12830`, `ρ = 0` has the centroid CF, as TINNiK itself says in its discussion before Theorem 4. This does not affect v5, whose Definition 1 has strict inequalities and so excludes it on T-quartets. It matters in the supplement, whose Definition 1 is non-strict and relies on this sentence to exclude the centroid: there a true edge with such a quartet would be contracted by the star test.

### TINNiK

**E29. B-inference, Step 3(a)(i).** The number of quartets sharing exactly three taxa with a fixed one is `4(N-4)`, not `4(N-1)`. The order is unchanged. Lean: [`TINNiK.card_sharing_three`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L38).

**E30. Theorem 2 of the source, as applied.** Hypothesis (b) says that a `t` is a B-quartet "on the induced 4-taxon network", but the Inference Rule also has to fire from a `t` inferred as a B-quartet of the full network, which may be treelike when induced.
This needs a stronger rule than the printed one. It holds by the same proof, because the separating cut edge also resolves `t` on `N`. Lean: [`AllmanEtAl2023.rule_core`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Basic.lean#L614), from which [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem2`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem2.lean#L36) and [`AllmanEtAl2023.closure_sound`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Basic.lean#L732) follow.

**E31. Appendix C.1.** A maximum need not exist on the open cut model: counts `(1,0,0)` have supremum not attained ([`scripts/BoundaryCheck.lean`](../scripts/BoundaryCheck.lean)). Lean estimates on the closed simplex, with strictly positive generating parameters, as agreed with the user. [`TINNiK.cutLineMLE_maximizes`](../src/TINNiK/Statistics/AppendixC.lean#L72).

**E32. Propositions 5 and 6, proofs.** "Assuming `θ₀` is not too close to the boundary … dependent on the sample size" is informal and not needed for fixed `φ₀`. The result holds as stated. In Proposition 6, differentiating under the integral has no justification given: it holds for `x > 0`, by domination on a neighbourhood of `x`, and fails at `x = 0`, where the derivative of the integrand vanishes and `G₁` has a positive right derivative. This does no harm, since `{0}` is null. The density `g = 2 Σ d/dx Gᵢ` also needs `Gᵢ(x) → 0` as `x → 0` ([`ProbabilityTheory.measure_polarStrip_eq_lintegral_of_hasDerivAt`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Probability/GaussianPlane.lean#L739)). Every display of the proposition checks out.

**E33. Theorem 4, proof.** Under the alternative, "the test distribution converges to the `χ²₁`" gives no comparison at cutoffs `m d/2` growing with `m`, and "without loss of generality" the MLE on the vertical line hides that the fitted line is random and `d` depends on it. Repairs: the test distribution is pointwise at most that of `Z²`, and the least limit over the three lines gives one `d` ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.cutTest_alternative`](../src/Basic/Testing/Tails.lean#L672), [`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.cutTail_le_chiSqOneTail`](../src/Basic/Testing/Tails.lean#L147)). Under the null, the paper's argument works and Lean follows it, with two corrections to "`μ₀` goes to `∞` and the distribution converges to the `χ²₁`". First, it should be `|μ₀| → ∞`, as Appendix C.3 writes it, since anomalous strictly cut CFs have `μ₀ → -∞`; the two signs behave alike, since `z̄ ↦ -z̄` swaps the last two terms of `Λ_n`. Second, the limit is governed by `cos α₀ |μ₀|`, the distance from `(0, μ₀)` to the other two lines, and `|μ₀|` alone is not enough: `α₀ → π/2` as `φ₀ → 3/2`, and at fixed `n` there `|μ₀| → ∞` while `cos α₀ μ₀ → -√n`, so the law tends to that of `min(Z², (Z-√n)², (Z+√n)²)`. This does no harm at a positive CF, where `φ̂ → φ₀ < 3/2`. Lean proves the limit uniformly in the argument and the parameter ([`Phylogenetics.Trinomial.abs_cutTail_sub_chiSqOneTail_lt`](../src/Basic/Testing/Trinomial/Tests.lean#L168)), which is what the plug-in needs ([`Phylogenetics.Trinomial.eventually_abs_cutTail_estimate_sub_lt`](../src/Basic/Testing/Trinomial/Tests.lean#L286)); so the p-value is asymptotically uniform ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.cutPValue_asymptoticallyUniform`](../src/Basic/Testing/Limits.lean#L416)) and `P(p < α_m) → 0` for any `α_m → 0` ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.cutTest_null`](../src/Basic/Testing/Limits.lean#L445)). With no 4-blob among the induced networks, "the maximum of the `α'_m`" is over an empty family; any sequence tending to `0` serves, and Lean adds `P(χ²₁ > m)` ([`TINNiK.exists_levels`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L614)). The centroid step evaluates at hybridization parameters `0` or `1`, on the boundary of the parameter space, so "analyticity" must hold across it: the CFs are analytic on all the coordinates ([`AllmanEtAl2023.isGenericNull_of_analyticOnNhd`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/GenericCF.lean#L166)). The result holds.

### ECToBlob's remarks and discussion, and smaller points elsewhere

These came from formalizing what the papers say in passing.

**E35. ECToBlob Remark 8.4: fails on a non-binary network.** The remark says the argument "indicates" that with `T₀` not a resolution the output keeps the cut edges of `T₀` and contracts the others, with no restriction on the network. For binary networks this holds ([`ECToBlob.Coarsening.remark8_4`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark84.lean#L1617), with `hpos` of Theorem 8.2 for Cauchy and CBC), and on many non-binary pairs too ([`ECToBlob.Coarsening.remark8_4_of_noTrivial`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark84.lean#L1586)). It fails on the tree `((0,1),2,3,4)` with one internal edge, with `T₀` the binary tree with cherries `02`, `34` and middle taxon `1`, the false edge `34|012` stays, since `34|01` is a cut split and `34|02`, `34|12` are trivial 4-blobs, all on the T1 null, and `34|01` is not uniform, so neither test contracts it ([`ECToBlob.Coarsening.Counterexample.not_remark8_4`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark84.lean#L2363), for all options, at the sequences of Theorem 8.2, for a law on nine gene trees meeting Assumption 1 in the form of [`ECToBlob.theorem8_2`](../src/ECToBlob/Section8.lean#L725); and [`ECToBlob.Coarsening.Counterexample.not_remark8_4_nmsc`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark84.lean#L2415) for the NMSC itself, at every valid `θ` and `ρ ∈ [0,1]`). The limit on any network and any `T₀` is [`ECToBlob.Coarsening.limitTree`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark84.lean#L822), and [`ECToBlob.Coarsening.limitTree_eq_inter_iff`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark84.lean#L1205) is the exact criterion for the remark on a pair `(N, T₀)`. The cut edges of `T₀` stay in all cases, and every edge of the limit is compatible with the tree of blobs. The remark's wording is hedged ("indicates", "should").

**E36. ECToBlob Remark 3.3 and Proposition 5.1: "no anomalous quartets" must be strict.** Lines 402–404 cite Ané et al. for: the NMSC satisfies (3.1) when the network has no anomalous quartets and cut edges have positive lengths. The remark quotes no definition, and the paper's own use is strict (line 722). Their Definition 2 gives only `1/3 ≤ p_ab|cd`. At a `3₂`-cycle with hybrid arcs of length 0, arcs `s₁, s₂` on the other side of the cycle, whose sum is their `s₀`, and cut edge `t = log(1 + (1-ρ)γ(1-γ)(1-e^{-(s₁+s₂)}))`, all three CFs equal `1/3`: nothing is anomalous in that sense, (3.1) fails, and all three binary trees tie, so the conclusion of Theorem 3.1 fails too ([`ECToBlob.Remark33Counterexample.remark3_3_strict`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33Counterexample.lean#L691), for any `0 ≤ ρ < 1`). The parameters with a tie form a null set. Under TINNiK's definition, or TOB-QMC's, the claim holds. Lean's [`IsTQuartetNonanomalous`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L246) and [`NoAnomalousQuartets`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L182) are strict.

**E37. ECToBlob Theorem 8.2: "any model" and the displayed tree model.** With the convention of lines 289–293 that a star contributes `(1/3, 1/3, 1/3)`, a trivial 4-blob under the displayed tree model gives counts `(n/3, n/3, n/3)` on every sample and p-values exactly `1` ([`ECToBlob.pValues_of_hasTrivialFourBlob`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L719)). So line 1065, "each individual p-value is uniformly distributed", is false there ([`ECToBlob.not_asymptoticallyUniform_starPValue`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L732)), and Lemma 8.1 doesn't apply. [`ECToBlob.theorem8_2`](../src/ECToBlob/Section8.lean#L725) takes binary gene trees, so it does not reach this model on non-binary networks. Our reasoning, not in Lean: for Bon and BBC the conclusion should still hold, since the repaired proof uses only the lower bound on p-values; for Cauchy and CBC the model already fails through E11.

**E38. ECToBlob §10: "the T1 test has greater power".** The null of T1 is nested in that of T3 ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.isT3Null_of_isT1Null`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L1292)), and the T3 statistic is at most the T1 statistic ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.t3Stat_le_t1Stat`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L539)). The p-values compare the other way at the maximum likelihood topology, since there the statistics agree and the T3 reference law is lower: [`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.t3PValue_lt_t1PValue`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L1315), and [`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.exists_t3PValue_lt_t1PValue`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L1324) at counts `(3,2,1)`. At a fixed level, if T1 rejects `s` and T3 doesn't, then `s` isn't the maximum likelihood topology. The sentence holds in the sense that at a CF on another half-line T1 rejects and T3 doesn't ([`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.t1_rejects_where_t3_cannot`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L1361)).

**E39. TOB-QMC: scaling claims and Algorithm 2.** The result still holds under each of these.
- Line 603 credits "the well-known binary lifting algorithm" with LCA queries in `O(1)`. Binary lifting takes `O(log n)` per query; `O(1)` needs an Euler tour with a sparse table, and both have the `O(n log n)` precomputation. So the totals stand.
- Algorithm 2 (supplement lines 654–676): the tuple `(a₁,a₂,b₁,b₂)` is never reassigned from `q_min` on a repeat, so each pass rebuilds the same `N` and the repeat is vacuous; at `n = 4` the set `N` is empty, so line 10 has no minimizer and line 4 never exits; the test at line 5 isn't counted in `i`; and `i` overshoots the limit by up to `2(n-4)`. Lean: [`TOBQMC.SearchOut.counts`](../src/TOBQMC/BipartitionSearch.lean#L559), with at most `(n-3) + 2·limit + 2(n-4)` subsets per edge ([`TOBQMC.card_threeFixOneAlter_union_testedSets_le`](../src/TOBQMC/BipartitionSearch.lean#L578)).
- Lines 130–132 have "fast and default ... `2n²` and `n²/4`, respectively", swapped against the supplement (lines 851–855).
- Supplement Lemma 8's proof, beyond E26: "reticulation nodes in `v`" should be `B`; "binary" isn't used; the two disjoint paths degenerate at `u₀ = r'`; "`u₀` is an ancestor of `u₁`" holds only for a particular reticulation on the cycle.

**E40. TINNiK: B-quartet inference, "this pair will be compared again later".** That sentence alone does not cover a third B-quartet of the rule appearing after the pair's comparison. The sentence before it supplies the comparison ("any time a new one is discovered it is compared to all quartets that share three taxa with it"), and the other of the pair is among its [`TINNiK.otherSets`](../src/TINNiK/Counts.lean#L57), so the algorithm is right. Lean: [`TINNiK.mem_bQuartetInference_iff`](../src/TINNiK/Counts.lean#L682) proves its output is the closure [`BInferred`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L118).

**E41. ECToBlob §3 and §7: what the observations need.**
- Lines 363–369 say that for `γ`s near 0 and 1, MQS infers displayed trees, on a set of positive measure, and that ties occur between. All three hold for the NMSC at every `ρ` ([`ECToBlob.Section3Observations.not_isNull_selectsDisplayed`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3Observations.lean#L809), [`ECToBlob.Section3Observations.exists_tie_of_displayedTrees`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3Observations.lean#L962), and [`ECToBlob.Section3Observations.fourCycle_not_isNull`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3Observations.lean#L1142), [`ECToBlob.Section3Observations.fourCycle_exists_tie`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3Observations.lean#L1132) on a 4-cycle). "Select a displayed tree" needs that tree binary: optimal trees are binary, so otherwise the statement is about its resolutions. "Sufficiently close" needs positive length on each internal edge of the displayed tree, which holds on binary networks and when all lengths are positive; it fails on `N₁` of Figure 1 with both hybrid arcs of length 0, where all CFs are `1/3` ([`ECToBlob.Section3Observations.isMQSOptimal_treeAC_of_length_zero`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3Observations.lean#L1270)).
- Lines 922–935 on time. "The `k`th edge contracted might be incident to as many as `4 + k` other internal edges" holds as an upper bound ([`ECToBlob.Counts.card_incidentSides_le`](../src/ECToBlob/Counts.lean#L409)) when `k` counts all edges contracted so far, Step 3's included, and the `4 + k` includes pendant edges; a step may contract several tied edges. It is not attained for `k > (s - 8)/2`. The printed sum `∑_{k=0}^{s-3}` has one term too many. And the `O(s⁵)` for `bi` and `quad` assumes p-values are stored: as written the algorithm recomputes at each step, which is `O(s⁶)` for every type. Storing them is sound ([`ECToBlob.Counts.edgeT1P_eq_of_qType_ne_mul`](../src/ECToBlob/Counts.lean#L495)). The bounds are [`ECToBlob.Counts.ectoblobOperations_le`](../src/ECToBlob/Counts.lean#L827), [`ECToBlob.Counts.ectoblobOperationsMul_le`](../src/ECToBlob/Counts.lean#L848), [`ECToBlob.Counts.ectoblobOperationsRecomputing_le`](../src/ECToBlob/Counts.lean#L868).

### Gusfield, Bansal, Bafna, Song 2007

"Line N" here is a line of the plain text of the PMC author manuscript, one line per paragraph ([`docs/CONFIGURATION.md`](CONFIGURATION.md)). That plain text loses the symbols the page renders as images. An [author's copy on Yun Song's page](https://people.eecs.berkeley.edu/~yss/Pub/decomposition.pdf), made from a DVI file on 2008-01-19, has the symbols and the same words apart from moved floats, footnotes and hyphenation, so it was used to check them. E49, E51 and E54 turn on symbols, and each is in that copy too, so none is an artifact of the PMC text. That copy isn't the journal's typeset text, which wasn't reachable, so whether the journal has them is unknown. The matrices and networks of the figures were compared with the PMC manuscript's images.

Neither Theorem 1, the paper's main result, nor the refutation of the conjectures is affected by anything here. Theorems 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 11 and Corollaries 1, 2, 3 hold as printed, under the paper's standing assumption that no column is constant. What fails is Theorem 4 as stated, and some sentences around it.

**E42. Theorem 4: the definition of "efficient" omits the node where a blob is entered (an error).** Lines 326–328: "we say that a node v in blob b is an external node if there is an edge from v to some node outside of b. We say a phylogenetic network is efficient if [it] does not contain two external nodes with the same node labels, and for each external node v (in a blob b), there is exactly one edge from v to a node off of b." Theorem 4 (lines 334–336): for "any efficient fully-decomposed network for M", contracting the blobs gives a tree that "is necessarily the tree T̄".

The definition says nothing about the edge entering a blob, and neither does the proof. An exit can sit at the entry node of a blob while rows lie above the blob. Then line 342, "in the other subnetwork, no leaf label contains S_v(C)", is false: the rows above the blob contain the super-character of its top. Line 341, "each super-character derived from C labels exactly one external node", needs the entry node too: in Figure 2 made efficient, the super-character `00` of the component `{2, 5}` labels no external node ([`Gusfield2007.line341_false_fig2`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2080)). There `T′` is `T̄`, so that one is a gap in the proof, not a further failure of the theorem.

The counterexample is [`Gusfield2007.topRows`](../src/Gusfield2007/Examples.lean#L100), with rows `00000, 01000, 10000, 11001, 11011, 11101, 11111`. The network has a blob on `00000, 01000, 10000, 11000` with the sites 1, 2; an arc with the site 5 from `11000` to `11001`; a blob on `11001, 11011, 11101, 11111` with the sites 3, 4; and a pendant arc at each node of the two blobs other than `11000`. It is efficient in the printed sense and fully decomposed, in any crossover model, with no recombination leaf, and with a site on every arc that has both ends off the blobs and isn't pendant. The split `{00000, 01000, 10000, 11001} | {11011, 11101, 11111}` of `T̄` is missing from `T′`: [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_false_top`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1943). The split is the witness to quote, since the number of edges depends on how line 264 is read. Moving the row `11001` up to a new node on the entering arc gives another efficient fully decomposed network, whose `T′` is `T̄`, so `T′` isn't invariant, against line 346: [`Gusfield2007.topRows_not_invariant`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1952). A smaller instance, with one blob and a root of degree 3, is on `{0000, 0001, 0010, 0110, 1010, 1110}`: [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_false_small`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1974).

The result stands in the following form. With the entry node counted, the theorem holds up to the two conventions of E44 and the site-free arcs of E43: for a fully decomposed network, `T′` is `T̄` exactly when the network has [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsEfficientAtEntry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1721), [`Gusfield2007.Network.RegularArcs`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L456) and [`Gusfield2007.Network.DegreeCond`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L467) ([`Gusfield2007.theorem4_entry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1843)). That definition reads "external" as having an edge to or from a node outside the blob, compares labels on one blob at a time, and counts the entering edge among the edges of its node. Lines 341 and 342 are then true: [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsEfficientAtEntry.existsUnique_port`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1803), [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsEfficientAtEntry.line342Claim`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1798). With the printed definition, [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1699) is exact: `T′` is `T̄` exactly when the network is efficient and has [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsTreeNormalStructural`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L888) and the degree condition. [`Gusfield2007.theorem4`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1310) and [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_splits`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L929) are its forward direction. These hypotheses can be met: for every `M` with at least three rows there is such a network, with an interior root ([`Gusfield2007.exists_realization`](../src/Gusfield2007/Realizability.lean#L327)). So `T̄` is the invariant structure of the networks the paper has in mind, and we read the omission as a slip in a definition: the proof's own line 341 needs the entry node. The use of Theorem 4 at line 418 is E53.

This repair is in print, and it isn't ours. Gusfield's book *ReCombinatorics* (MIT Press, 2014), Section 7.3.5, says "A node v in a blob B is an external node of B if v is adjacent (without regard to edge direction) to some node v′ not in B". It calls an ARG canonical if "No blob contains two nodes labeled with the same sequence", "Each external node v (in a blob B) is incident with exactly one cut edge", "If the root node v of a blob B is an external node, then the cut edge incident with v is directed into v", and "Every blob contains at least one incompatible pair of sites". Its Theorem 7.3.3 says "All canonical, fully decomposed ARGs for M have the same undirected backbone tree", and its Corollary 7.3.4 is line 341 repaired: the number of external nodes of a blob "is exactly the number of distinct sequences in M(C)". We read the book only through short snippets from Open Library's public search inside, and its table of contents, and not its proofs; someone should read Sections 7.3.5 and 7.4 in print. The snippets show no statement that the 2007 definition fails, and the section's introduction says "(with some technical qualification)". What the Lean adds is the failure under the printed definition, by explicit networks ([`Gusfield2007.theorem4_false_top`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1943), [`Gusfield2007.topRows_not_invariant`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1952)); the exact characterization in both directions, where the snippets show one direction, for `IsEfficientAtEntry`, which is weaker than canonical (a canonical ARG is efficient at the entry, but not conversely: a blob can repeat a label away from its external nodes, and [`Gusfield2007.fourRootNet`](../src/Gusfield2007/CounterexampleNets.lean#L439) has an exit at the root of its blob, against the third condition); and the check in the kernel.

**E43. Theorem 4: site-free arcs (a case line 97 allows and the statement must exclude).** Line 97: "There may be additional edges that are assigned no integers." The proof at lines 341–344 looks only at edges leaving a blob, and never meets such an arc with both ends off the blobs, on which the theorem fails as printed. Take the rows `10000, 01000, 00100, 00010, 00001`, with arcs from the root to the first two and a site-free arc to a node with the other three: a perfect phylogeny, so efficient and fully decomposed under either definition, with no blob at all, and with every node of `T′` of degree at most two carrying a leaf. `T̄` is a star, and `T′` has one more edge: [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_false_siteFree`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2028), [`Gusfield2007.fiveNet_split`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2045). Counting the entry node doesn't help, since there is no blob. The theorem holds when no arc with both ends off the blobs, other than a pendant one, is site-free: the field `arc_labelled` of [`Gusfield2007.Network.RegularArcs`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L456). [`theorem4_entry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1843) shows that this is necessary. Contracting such arcs repairs a given network, but the paper doesn't say to. Whether the ARGs of Gusfield's 2014 book (E42) allow such arcs wasn't visible in the snippets we read.

**E44. Theorem 4: two conventions (implicit, not errors).** Read literally, the printed statement also fails on a root of degree two and on a recombination leaf. For `M = {0, 1}`, the network with a root and two leaves has a `T′` with three nodes, where `T̄` has two ([`Gusfield2007.theorem4Printed_false_rootDegreeTwo`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L439)); line 418 itself places a root on an edge, so read `T′` with the root suppressed. For `M = {00, 01, 10, 11}`, with `11` a recombination leaf, `T′` has three edges and `T̄` four ([`Gusfield2007.theorem4Printed_false_recombLeaf`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1920)); Figure 1's caption speaks of "the recombinant sequence exiting a recombination node", and line 341 needs it, so give a recombination node a pendant arc. We don't count these as errors. They are [`Gusfield2007.Network.DegreeCond`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L467) and the field `recomb_not_leaf` of [`Gusfield2007.Network.RegularArcs`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L456).

**E45. §4.2, line 373 (an error).** "With the above definition of 'treeness', it is clear that a phylogenetic network is 'the most tree-like' if and only if T̄ is the resulting undirected tree". Treeness is the number of edges of `T′`, among networks whose blobs share no node and with no node of in-degree and out-degree one (lines 364–368). Site-free arcs add edges: on [`Gusfield2007.starRows`](../src/Gusfield2007/Examples.lean#L105), the star with four edges has `T′ = T̄`, and resolving it with two site-free arcs gives six ([`Gusfield2007.mostTreeLikePrinted_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2137)). A reading that doesn't count a site-free arc with both ends off the blobs escapes that example, but not the next two. They are efficient and fully decomposed, with no such arc: their site-free arcs that aren't pendant are at the blob, from the root to its top, and in the second also from its top to a node with two rows. On the rows `0000 0001 0010 0100 1000 1100` the treeness is 7, the number of edges of `T̄`, yet `T′` isn't `T̄` ([`Gusfield2007.treeness_example₁`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2151)). On `00000 00001 00010 00100 01000 10000 11000` it is 9, more than the 8 edges of `T̄` ([`Gusfield2007.treeness_example₂`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2173)). Two narrower counts aren't in the paper, and we computed them by hand on these networks, not in Lean. Counting only the edges with a site doesn't escape them: the counts tie, at 2 = 2 and 3 = 3, while `T′` isn't `T̄`. Counting only the edges that carry a character of `B`, as line 264 labels `T̄`, does: 5 < 6 and 6 < 7. So these two examples refute the definition as printed, and that last reading isn't refuted by them, though on [`starRows`](../src/Gusfield2007/Examples.lean#L105) it ties at 4 = 4 unless the site-free arcs are also contracted. Line 373 is a remark, and no theorem of the paper rests on it. Theorem 7.4.1 of Gusfield's 2014 book (E42) restates it for canonical ARGs ("the most treelike"); we didn't see that book's definition of treelike, so these examples aren't compared with it. What's true is [`Gusfield2007.treeness_le_and_eq_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2333): for networks with node-disjoint blobs, each with the sites of one component, and with `RegularArcs`, [`Gusfield2007.Network.NonPendantPortsDistinct`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2255) and the degree condition, the treeness is at most the number of edges of `T̄`, with equality exactly when `T′` is `T̄`. These needn't be fully decomposed or efficient ([`Gusfield2007.treeness_nonFD_example`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2354)), and the inequality can be strict ([`Gusfield2007.treeness_strict_example`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2390)). The sentence of line 375 that all of a component lies on one blob is right. Blobs merging components are left open (Section 9).

**E46. Line 156 (a case line 97 allows and the statement must exclude).** "The undirected tree created by ignoring the directions of the edges of T is invariant over all perfect phylogenies for M. Similarly, there is a unique phylogenetic network that derives M, with ancestral sequence S and without recombination cycles". The paper's networks may have site-free arcs (line 97), and with them neither holds: [`Gusfield2007.fiveNet`](../src/Gusfield2007/CounterexampleNets.lean#L494) and [`Gusfield2007.fiveStarNet`](../src/Gusfield2007/CounterexampleNets.lean#L500) derive one matrix with no recombination, and their trees have six and five edges ([`Gusfield2007.perfectPhylogeny_not_invariant`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2480)). The classical result that line 156 recalls can only be about trees without such arcs; the expositions it points to weren't read. Formalized: [`Gusfield2007.perfectPhylogeny_invariant`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2465), under `RegularArcs` and the degree condition, and [`Gusfield1991.network_rooted_unique`](../src/External/Gusfield1991/PerfectPhylogeny.lean#L885), among the reduced trees [`Gusfield1991.IsReducedTree`](../src/External/Gusfield1991/PerfectPhylogeny.lean#L230).

**E47. Line 180: the paraphrase of a cited definition is too weak (an error of wording).** "In the case of galled-trees, every reduced galled-tree for M must be fully-decomposed. A galled-tree is 'reduced' if every recombination cycle contains some incompatible sites." With this wording the claim fails: on the rows `000, 101, 010, 110` there is a single-crossover galled tree with one cycle, holding two incompatible sites and also a compatible one, which isn't on a tree edge ([`GusfieldEtAl2004.line180_printed_false`](../src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004/GalledTree.lean#L520)). The source's Definition 2.2 says more: "every gall contains at least one conflicted pair of sites, and contains no unconflicted sites". With it the claim holds, in the source's rooted form and in the unrooted form of 2007: [`GusfieldEtAl2004.IsReducedGalledTree.isFullyDecomposedFor_conflictGraph`](../src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004/GalledTree.lean#L449), [`GusfieldEtAl2004.IsReducedGalledTree.isFullyDecomposedFor_incompatGraph`](../src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004/GalledTree.lean#L444). The cited theorem stands, and no proof of the paper uses this sentence.

**E48. Lines 403 and 425: a step that fails on one `M` (an error; no result affected).** Line 403: "the rooted conjectures imply the original unrooted conjectures, by letting S be the ancestral sequence that appears in the optimal phylogenetic network using R(M) (respectively R¹(M)) recombination nodes." An ancestral sequence can merge components: the network that S-min decomposability gives is fully decomposed for `G_S(M)`, not for `G(M)`. On Figure 6, Figure 7's topology with the root relabelled `001100` gives `R¹_S(M) = 3 = R¹(M)` with `G_S(M)` connected, so `M` is S-min-1 decomposable at that `S`; yet `F¹(M) = 4`, so `M` isn't min-1 decomposable ([`Gusfield2007.fig6_hG_necessary`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L252); the example isn't in the paper). Formalized: [`Gusfield2007.minDecomposable_of_sMinDecomposable`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L110), with the hypothesis that `G_S(M)` has the components of `G(M)`. As an implication between the conjectures, the sentence holds only because the rooted conjectures are false ([`Gusfield2007.rootedConjecture_imp_unrootedConjecture`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L554), Section 7). Line 425, "when M is S-min (respectively S-min-1) decomposable for all S, then M is min-decomposable", is a stronger hypothesis on one `M`, and it stays open: [`Gusfield2007.MinDecomposableOfForallS`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L118) is a definition, neither proved nor refuted. Line 590 uses the step: "which also implies that the rooted versions are false" is the paper's only argument for the rooted multiple-crossover case. That conclusion holds directly, because the ancestral sequence of Figure 9 is a row of Figure 8, where `G_S(M) = G(M)` ([`Gusfield2007.rootedConjecture_multiple_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L479)).
