The single-crossover case is shown directly at line 608 ([`Gusfield2007.rootedConjecture_single_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L224)). The positive results of §5.2 are rooted, and don't use the step.

**E49. Lines 554–555 (false as printed; probably `M` for `M + S`, as at line 552).** "It is easy to prove that for any non-degenerate galled-network [N] for M, a pair of sites is incompatible in L if and only if it is incompatible in M, and therefore G(L) and G(M) are identical." The root's sequence can supply a fourth pair. On [`Gusfield2007.galledNet`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L221), with root `0000` and `M = {1000, 1010, 0100, 0101, 1100}`, the sites 1 and 2 are compatible in `M` and incompatible in `L` ([`Gusfield2007.galledIncompatPrinted_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L309)). True with `M + S`: [`Gusfield2007.incompatible_labelSet_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L175), so `G_S(L) = G_S(M)` ([`Gusfield2007.conflictGraph_labelSet_eq`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L190)). That is what the application of Theorem 5 needs, and it is proved: [`Gusfield2007.sMinDecomposable_of_galled`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L198). The printed form holds when `S ∈ M`: [`Gusfield2007.incompatible_labelSet_iff_of_mem`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L205). Huson and Klöpper 2007 wasn't read (Section 2), so this finding rests on the definition of non-degenerate in their errata, and the definition of galled in Huson et al. 2009; the 2007 wording may differ.

**E50. Line 560 (an error).** From the haplotype bound "it follows that" equality holds "only when every node in [the network] is labeled by a sequence in M + S. Hence, when the haplotype bound is tight for the appropriate crossover model, every node in [the network] must be visible" (lines 560–564). This fails on identical columns. For `M = {00, 11}` and `S = 00`, `H = 2 - 1 - 1 = 0`, and [`Gusfield2007.chainNet`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L448), the path `00 → 10 → 11` with a leaf `00` at the root, has no recombination and an invisible node: [`Gusfield2007.visibleOfTightPrinted_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L525). Line 580's count allows for this: the labelled edges are at least the distinct sites. With distinct columns the sentence holds: [`MyersGriffiths2003.allVisible_of_eq`](../src/External/MyersGriffiths2003/Bound.lean#L97). For Theorem 6 this does no harm, since its hypotheses give distinct columns. For Theorem 10 it does: see E51.

**E51. Theorem 10 as printed: a notation slip, and the printed statement stays unproved.** Line 568: "if H(M + S) = R¹(M + S) then M is S-min-1 decomposable, and if H(M + S) = R(M + S) then M is S-min decomposable". The proof reasons on an optimal network for `M` with ancestral sequence `S`, using `R_S(M)` nodes, and treats that number as `R(M + S)`, the minimum over all ancestral sequences. Line 525 has the same slip. The two can differ under the hypothesis, and then the tight network has another root and the proof doesn't apply. For `{0000, 0101, 1000, 1010, 1110, 1111}`, `S = 0101`, with multiple crossovers, `H = R(M + S) = 1` and `R_S(M) = 2`; for `{0001, 0011, 0100, 0101, 1000, 1011, 1100}`, `S = 1000`, with single crossovers, `H = R¹(M + S) = 2` and `R¹_S(M) = 3`. The values of `R` in these two examples were computed by a separate exhaustive search, and aren't in Lean. Both examples have one component, so the conclusion holds on them. Theorem 10 as printed is unproved here, and not false as far as we know: [`Gusfield2007.Theorem10Printed`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L562) is a definition. A search found no failure, within these limits: rows of the form `(x, 0)` or `(0, y)`, two non-trivial components, `S` a row, and no compatible sites; all the classes at the smallest sizes (3 + 3 and 4 + 2 sites for multiple crossovers, 3 + 2 and 3 + 3 for single), and only samples at larger ones. Its programs aren't in this repository, and it proves nothing. The natural reading has the rooted count, and that is what's proved: [`Gusfield2007.theorem10`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L756), [`Gusfield2007.theorem10_multiple`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L575). For multiple crossovers no more is needed, by restricting to one site per class of identical columns. For recombinations modelling mutations it is Corollary 3. For single crossovers [`Gusfield2007.theorem10`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L756) needs distinct columns in `M + S`, because of E50, and whether that can be dropped is open: re-inserting a duplicate site can break the prefix-suffix form of a crossover. Theorem 6 uses this form, so it is unaffected.

**E52. Lines 329–330 (a gap).** "If [the network] is not efficient, it can be easily modified to become efficient without increasing the number of recombinations used. Also, if [it] is fully-decomposed, then the efficient network derived from it is also fully-decomposed." The paper gives no modification, and the obvious one hides a case: moving the exits of a node to another node with its label can leave a recombination node as a new leaf. That node is then wasted, and removing it from a fully decomposed network takes Lemma 5 and Theorem 3. The statement holds for the printed definition: [`Gusfield2007.exists_isEfficient`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L260), by induction on recombination nodes and then exits, and [`Gusfield2007.exists_isEfficient_of_any`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L285) for any network. Gusfield's 2014 book (E42) gives a modification in the proof of its Theorem 7.3.2, of which the snippets show only the start. For `IsEfficientAtEntry` only existence is proved ([`Gusfield2007.exists_isEfficientAtEntry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Realizability.lean#L365); Section 9).

**E53. Gaps in the proofs (all the results hold).**

- **Line 135.** That a cycle sharing no edge can be made node-disjoint needs that a node shared by blobs has arcs in from at most one of them, which is unstated: two arcs into a node lie on one cycle. [`Gusfield2007.Network.exists_isCycle_of_tgt_eq`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L344), [`Gusfield2007.Network.exists_blobsNodeDisjoint`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L1180).
- **Line 141.** If the contracted graph had a cycle, "that cycle would correspond to a recombination cycle in" the network. A cycle of the underlying graph isn't a recombination cycle: it can change direction several times. Each of its arcs is on one, since all nodes are reachable from the root: [`Gusfield2007.Network.isTreeArc_iff_isBridge`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L397), [`Gusfield2007.Network.blobbedTree_isTree`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L465).
- **Lines 187–190.** The rooted analogue "follows from Theorem 1". The statement of Theorem 1 for `M + S` puts `S` at a leaf, and a network can't be re-rooted through a blob. What gives the result is the proof of Theorem 1, as line 312 says for Theorem 3: [`Gusfield2007.theorem1_rooted`](../src/Gusfield2007/Decomposition.lean#L291).
- **Line 264.** "Applying the Perfect Phylogeny Theorem", there is a tree "where each character of B labels one edge in T̄, and each edge is labeled by one or more characters of B". That theorem gives a tree with possible unlabelled edges and nodes of degree two, and "each edge" can't hold with rows at leaves on `{00, 01, 10}`. Line 289 says "every internal edge", which is how [`Gusfield2007.IsTBar`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L433) reads it; [`Gusfield2007.IsPerfectPhylogeny.exists_normal`](../src/Gusfield2007/TreeContract.lean#L321) contracts what isn't needed.
- **Line 271.** "From the facts that all columns in B are distinct" is false: a trivial component gives two columns with one bipartition, as line 245 says, and columns from different components can have one bipartition, as in [`topRows`](../src/Gusfield2007/Examples.lean#L100), where the column of super-character `11` and that of the site 5 coincide. What's used is that the splits of edges are distinct: [`Gusfield2007.IsTBar`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L433).
- **Line 295.** "The three other cases are similar and omitted." The two where `e₁` has a non-dominant super-character take another argument: all the rows beyond `e` have the dominant super-character, which must be that of `e₂`, so that `e` and `e₂` would have one split ([`Gusfield2007.IsTBar.edges_incident`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L392)).
- **Line 299.** The sentence on connecting edges uses what line 277 declines to assert ([`Gusfield2007.adj_center_labelled`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L750)), and "each central-star node" uses that distinct non-trivial components have distinct centres ([`Gusfield2007.IsTBar.center_ne`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L780)). When the root is a central node `v_C`, its label is 0 on every character of `B` from `C`, by Lemma 4, so by our reading it gives no sequence on the sites of `C`, and `S(C)` takes a choice. This observation is ours: line 416 says of "the situation in the proof of Theorem 1" that "the label on v can be used for the ancestral sequence of bv". Lean takes the root at a row, one of the choices that line 299 leaves arbitrary, which avoids the question.
- **Lines 312–316, Theorem 3.** "Establishes" (line 312) omits three steps: if `S(C) ∈ M(C)`, a leaf labelled `S(C)` has no edge of `T̄` to carry ([`Gusfield2007.RS_erase_eq`](../src/Gusfield2007/Decomposition.lean#L300)); an optimal network has no site-free blob ([`Gusfield2007.Decomp.exists_copy_of_siteFree`](../src/Gusfield2007/DecompNet.lean#L518)); and if `S ∈ M`, the row needs its own leaf below the root ([`Gusfield2007.Decomp.addLeaf`](../src/Gusfield2007/DecompNet.lean#L240)).
- **Lines 341–342.** See E42.
- **Line 411.** That every `M` is min decomposable for recombinations modelling mutations "follows from results on the maximum parsimony problem using median networks, as established by Bandelt et al. (1995)". Those results are there: "all most parsimonious trees for a set V of 0-1 vectors can be realized in the median network" (p. 745), and at a cut node of that network the characters split into two sets, and the most parsimonious trees "are exactly the trees composed by the MP trees" of the two parts (p. 747; "decomposition along cut nodes", p. 748). Neither paper writes the link from cut nodes to the components of `G(M)`, which the statement needs; for Buneman graphs it appeared later, as Theorem 5.1 of Dress et al. 2011 (Section 10). The statement holds: [`Gusfield2007.minDecomposable_pointwise`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L168), by another route, which isn't forced (Section 7).
- **Line 418.** "The correctness of the procedure follows from Theorem 4 and the fact that placing the root of T̄ at a leaf or on the interior of an edge will not reduce the number of required recombinations". Theorem 4 is false as printed, and an optimal fully decomposed network needn't satisfy its corrected form. The conclusion holds: [`Gusfield2007.F_eq_sInf_interior`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L696), and the second fact is [`Gusfield2007.exists_interior_sum_rootCost_le`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L651) (Section 7).
- **Line 576.** The two conditions of the modification don't exclude a recombination node labelled `S`, and the count is then off by one ([`Gusfield2007.exists_reattach_of_card_lt`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L774)). And "does not label any other node" asks too much: a node reached by a site-free arc can share the label. Only recombination nodes need to move: [`Gusfield2007.exists_tight_of_allVisible`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L826).

**E54. Misprints (all in both the PMC text and the author's copy).**

- Line 210: "i and k′ can't be incompatible" should be `i′` and `k′`, and "W ∩ Y̅ = ∅" should be `W ∩ Y = ∅` (page 10 of the author's copy, read by eye).
- Line 561: "ℛ^∞(ℳ + 𝒮)", in another font and with `∞`, should be `R¹(M + S)`, given the "(or R(M + S))" after it (page 27 of that copy).
- Line 299: "M(G)" for `G(M)`.
- Line 525: "R¹(M + S)" for `R¹_S(M)` (E51).
- Line 552: the dominant sequences are in `(M + S)(C)` and `(M + S)(C′)`, not `M(C)` and `M(C′)`, since the components are those of `G_S(M)`.
- Line 644: "R¹(M_d(C₁)) + R¹(M_d(C₁)) = 4d" for `C₁` and `C₂`.
- Line 656: "R(M_d) ≤ 3d" for `R¹(M_d)`.
- Line 666: "F¹(M) is always R(M) + 1" for `R¹(M) + 1`.
- The reference list gives Gusfield 1991 as "inferring evolutionary history"; the article is "Efficient algorithms for inferring evolutionary trees".

**What wasn't a problem.** Line 277, "Lemma 4 does not assert that v_C is only incident with edges labeled by characters that originate from C", is a caution: the stronger fact is true ([`Gusfield2007.adj_center_labelled`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L750)), and line 299 needs it. The second clause of Lemma 4 (line 275) fails for a trivial component only because Lean keeps both of its columns, where lines 245 and 281 keep one, so [`Gusfield2007.lemma4`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L672) states it for non-trivial components and the third clause covers the rest. The haplotype bound's arithmetic at line 559 is right: `H` can be negative as an integer, and is at most 0 on a perfect phylogeny. "Some appropriate sequence" at line 358, which line 510 relies on, needs a partner that already exists, and one does: the first change at a site is a mutation, and every later one has a partner earlier in the order. The numbers of the figures are confirmed in Lean: Figure 5 ([`Gusfield2007.fig5_counts`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L109), [`Gusfield2007.fig5_counts_multiple`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L115)); Figure 6 ([`Gusfield2007.fig6_R_single`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure6.lean#L250), [`Gusfield2007.fig6_F_single`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L194)); Figure 8 ([`Gusfield2007.fig8_R_multiple`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure8.lean#L206), [`Gusfield2007.fig8_four_le_F`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L442)), Figure 9's crossovers ([`Gusfield2007.figure9_crossovers`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure8.lean#L191)), Figure 10 ([`Gusfield2007.figure10`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure10.lean#L258)) and Footnote 2 ([`Gusfield2007.footnote2`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure1.lean#L188)).

**On record, and not from the paper.** The refutation is on record elsewhere: Huson et al. 2009, Figure 3, credits to Yun Song an example of the same 2-plus-2-into-3 count for clusters, and van Iersel et al. 2010 and Kelk et al. 2012 state the failure (Section 10). Line 590 says the refutation also establishes that Conjecture 1 of Huson and Klöpper 2007 is false; we didn't read that conjecture, so that it applies as stated is unverified. A search for this formalization found a counterexample to the multiple-crossover conjecture smaller than Figure 8, with 7 rows and 6 sites against 13 and 10: the matrix [`Gusfield2007.evenRows`](../src/Gusfield2007/Examples.lean#L81), `{000000, 000011, 000101, 000110, 011000, 101000, 110000}`, has `F = 4` and `R = 3` ([`Gusfield2007.even_unrootedConjecture_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L542), and rooted at `000000`, [`Gusfield2007.even_rootedConjecture_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L546)). We don't claim that it is new: it has the same count. The paper's two counterexamples are each for one model only: Figure 8 has `R¹ = F¹ = 6` ([`Gusfield2007.fig8_R_single`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure8.lean#L229), [`Gusfield2007.fig8_F_single`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L458)), and Figure 6 has `R = F = 2` ([`Gusfield2007.fig6_R_multiple`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure6.lean#L261), [`Gusfield2007.fig6_F_multiple`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L203)).

### Allman, Baños, Mitchell, Rhodes 2023

"Line N" here is a line of the plain text of the PMC page of the author manuscript NIHMS1884071, as in [`docs/CONFIGURATION.md`](CONFIGURATION.md) ("the PMC text, line N"). This manuscript and the arXiv TeX were both read, and the journal's text wasn't reachable, so nothing here was checked against it. The items in the manuscript only are marked; the rest are in the TeX as well. The later papers cite this one, and none of their uses is affected (Section 2). No numbered result of the paper fails, reading a subnetwork as connected; read literally, Lemma 2 does (E55). E57 and E64 record sentences that fail as read literally, and the rest are gaps, loose sentences and misprints.

**E55. Lemma 2 (a missing word; the statement needs it).** Line 184: "Consider a network N with degree-1 nodes bijectively labelled by X, and a subnetwork M of N with the restricted labelling of some degree-1 nodes by Y ⊆ X. If a set S ⊆ Y determines a blob on M, then S determines a blob on N." Lemma 2 needs `M` connected, or at least each `s` joined to `ℬ₀` in `M`. Its proof assumes this and all its uses satisfy it, but the word is missing. For disconnected `M`, Definition 8 can hold vacuously and the conclusion fails. No result of the paper is affected. The paper writes "connected subnetwork" where it means that (line 207), so the literal reading is available. The counterexample is the caterpillar `(((a, b), c), d)` with `M` the pendant edges: [`AllmanEtAl2023.lemma2_needs_connected`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Lemma2Counterexample.lean#L163). [`AllmanEtAl2023.lemma2`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Lemma2.lean#L146) takes `M` connected, which is what its uses have ([`AllmanEtAl2023.isConnectedSubnetwork_ancestralArcs`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Corollary2.lean#L43)).

**E56. Lemma 2: the degree-one hypothesis, and blobs of three taxa (gaps).** "With degree-1 nodes bijectively labelled by X" is more than the uses satisfy: at line 215 Lemma 3 applies Lemma 2 with `M` in the role of `N`, and the root of `M` can have degree 1 there, with no label. The proof doesn't use it: [`AllmanEtAl2023.IsLabelledNetwork`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Lemma2.lean#L30) asks for distinct labels on nodes of degree 1. In the proof, "it has a subpath from ℬ to s entirely outside of ℬ" (line 185) needs `s ∉ ℬ`, which holds since a degree-one node is alone in its blob, and such a blob isn't determined by three or more labels. And line 73, "on a network every subset of 3 taxa determines a blob", has no proof: [`AllmanEtAl2023.BlobStructure.existsUnique_isDeterminedBy_three`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobStructure.lean#L305), through [`AllmanEtAl2023.exists_median`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/SubgraphBlobs.lean#L588).

**E57. Proposition 1 (four small points; the proposition holds as printed).**

- **Line 165: the lengths (a misprint, manuscript only).** "the pendant edges to a, b, c, d to be of lengths 1, 1, 1 + (k + 1)ϵ + (2^k − 1)M, respectively" gives three lengths for four edges. The TeX has `1, 1, 1 + (k+1)ε, 2 + (k+1)ε + (2^k − 1)M`, and Figure 6 agrees. Lean uses those ([`AllmanEtAl2023.NetworkNk.metricNk`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/NetworkNk.lean#L135)), which make the network ultrametric as the sentence says ([`AllmanEtAl2023.NetworkNk.heightNk_source`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/NetworkNkGraph.lean#L791)).
- **Line 166: "Note that by Theorem 1, CF_abcd is strictly (ab|cd)-cut for ϵ > 0" (false as a claim on all `ε > 0`; unused).** Theorem 1 is generic, and the proposition's own intermediate-value argument reaches `(1/3, 1/3, 1/3)`: [`AllmanEtAl2023.NetworkNk.exists_not_isStrictlyCutAtFirst`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Proposition1.lean#L229). No step uses the sentence.
- **Lines 163, 167, 173: `p` means two things (notation).** It is an entry of the triple in the statement, and `exp(-ϵ)` in the proof; the last paragraph writes `(q, p, p)`, `q + 2p = 1`, for the statement's `(p, q, q)`.
- **Line 164: "By statement 1 of Theorem 1" (a loose citation that holds).** That statement is for generic parameters, and the proposition is for all valid ones. Generic parameters are dense and CFs are continuous, so the citation is good: [`AllmanEtAl2023.ForGenericParams.eq_zero_of_continuous`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/GenericDense.lean#L24), [`AllmanEtAl2023.proposition1`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Proposition1.lean#L62).

**E58. Theorem 1, statement (b) (gaps; the result holds).**

- **Lines 147–148: "suppressing any degree-2 nodes" isn't enough.** A root can lose a child, and then it has out-degree 1, which Definition 1 excludes. On the 4-cycle with arcs `r→v, r→x, x→b, x→y, y→c, y→z, z→d, z→v, v→a`, deleting `r→v` does this. Step 3 of Definition 6 is what's needed, and the CFs are unaffected ([`AllmanEtAl2023.nmscCF_removeStem`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/InducedCF.lean#L470)).
- **Line 149: "Now e must be a cut edge in both N1 and N2" has no reason.** It's true: a cut edge of `Nᵢ` with split `ab|cd` lies in `N₃` between `ℬ` and `b`, so the attachments of `a` lie beyond it, and hence beyond `e` ([`AllmanEtAl2023.IsLowPair.ends_mem_far`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Reduction.lean#L277), [`AllmanEtAl2023.IsLowPair.onlyCrosses_far_insert`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Reduction.lean#L337)).
- **Line 146: "Doing so, we have effectively removed these blobs"** has no reason ([`AllmanEtAl2023.nmscCF_contracted`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/ContractBlob.lean#L724), [`AllmanEtAl2023.exists_isContractible_of_cutDegree_two`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/ContractBlob.lean#L1080)). **Line 148: "since 𝒩− has no 3-blobs"** leaves unproved the 3-blob at the parent of a cherry ([`AllmanEtAl2023.exists_cutDegree_three_of_cherry`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem1b.lean#L289)).
- **Implicit:** that a lowest hybrid node exists, and how generic parameters on `𝒩⁺` reach the `𝒩ᵢ⁺` at line 152, which states the choice with no reason ([`AllmanEtAl2023.measure_deleted_preimage`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem1b.lean#L82)).

**E59. Corollary 1 (a gap; the result holds).** Line 157, "they give rise to a generic condition on numerical parameter values on the n-taxon network", needs that the map of parameters to an induced network sends valid parameters to valid ones, and that preimages of null sets are null: [`AllmanEtAl2023.isMetric_inducedParams`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/ClaimsTheorem1.lean#L52), [`AllmanEtAl2023.isGenericNull_inducedParams`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/ClaimsTheorem1.lean#L73). It also needs the identity of the CFs, which line 181 states "by the structure of the NMSC model": [`AllmanEtAl2023.nmscCF_induced`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/ClaimsTheorem1.lean#L36).

**E60. Corollary 2 and Lemma 3: the stem (a gap, manuscript only).** Line 186 says that induced networks "are obtained from subnetworks by suppressing degree-2 nodes", and line 207 likewise. The manuscript's Definition 6 has a third step that removes a stem from the root, and neither passage mentions it. (The TeX's Definition 6 has only the first two steps.) Blobs and cut edges pass through it: [`AllmanEtAl2023.Bridges.isBlobQuartetOnInduced_iff`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Bridges.lean#L636), [`AllmanEtAl2023.Bridges.induced_hasCutSplit_iff`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Bridges.lean#L210).

**E61. Lemma 3 (omitted steps; the result holds).**

- Line 208: "Thus e0 also separates a, b, from c, d" has no reason: the side containing `c, d` avoids `ℬ′` and is connected.
- Line 213: "Thus there is a path P3 from e0 to z in K_cd": the reason gives connectivity in `N′` only, and the path has to be kept in `K_cd`.
- Line 214: "This cycle also lies in M, as none of its edges are ancestral only to the taxon d. Since ℬ′ also lies in M" has no argument for `K_ab`, and doesn't follow from `ℬ′` being a blob of `N′` alone. It holds because `ℬ′ ⊆ K_ab` and `d ∈ K_cd`: a directed path from `K_ab` to `d` must use `e₀`, whose head is above all of `K_cd`, so it can also lead to `c` ([`AllmanEtAl2023.CutEdgeData.near_reaches`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CycleLemma.lean#L422)).
- Line 214: that `C` "passes through A, Kab, e0, and Kcd" after removing self-intersections, and that the path from `z` to `y` avoids `e₀`, so that `e₀` is used once, are unstated.
- Line 215: "and hence in M ∩ N′" goes from a larger network to a smaller one, the direction Lemma 2 doesn't give. It holds because the edges above `d` alone lie in `K_cd`, so `ℬ′` is untouched ([`AllmanEtAl2023.CutEdgeData.determines_off`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CycleLemma.lean#L870)).
- Line 215: "the initial segment of P1 gives a path from α to C" needs `P₁` to meet `C` after the removal. It does: take the last vertex of `P₁` on the directed path from `y` to `α` ([`AllmanEtAl2023.CutEdgeData.Paths.exists_meet`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CycleLemma.lean#L692)).

**E62. Theorem 3 (omitted steps; the result holds).** Line 222, "Passing to the induced network on those taxa, all edges in ℬ are retained", has no proof ([`AllmanEtAl2023.exists_representatives_induced`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Claims.lean#L129)). The induction at lines 222 and 225 speaks of the induced 4-taxon networks of an induced network, and needs these to have the quartet topologies of the 4-taxon networks induced from `𝒩⁺`, which the paper doesn't say: [`AllmanEtAl2023.inducedHasQuartetTopology_induced`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Claims.lean#L106), [`AllmanEtAl2023.inducedHasStarTopology_induced`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Claims.lean#L117).

**E63. The caption of Figure 5 (a labelling slip, manuscript only).** The caption at line 293 has `𝒩₁⁺` "the result of further removing edge h₂", and `𝒩₂⁺` from removing `h₁`. The formula at line 151 is `CF(𝒩⁺) = γ₁ CF(𝒩₂⁺) + γ₂ CF(𝒩₁⁺)`, with `γ₁, γ₂` the parameters of `h₁, h₂`, which fits `𝒩ᵢ⁺` removing `hᵢ`, as line 148 has it. Only the labels are affected. The formula is in the TeX too, and the figure and its caption are not. In Lean the two networks are named by the arc deleted ([`AllmanEtAl2023.InPassing.cf_mixture`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/InPassing.lean#L550)).

**E64. Line 252: edges between trivial blobs (an implicit qualification).** "For edges between trivial blobs, it is straightforward to see that edge lengths are identifiable." Read literally, this fails for pendant edges: a taxon and its parent can both be trivial blobs, and a single lineage on the edge never coalesces, so nothing depends on its length ([`AllmanEtAl2023.FourCycle.pendant_not_identifiable`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/FourCycle.lean#L817), [`AllmanEtAl2023.FourCycle.isTrivialBlob_root_taxon`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/FourCycle.lean#L823)). At the root of `𝒩⊕`, only the sum of the two lengths counts. Probably a missing word. The proved form is for internal edges of `𝒩⁻`: [`AllmanEtAl2023.TrivialEdges.semidirected_length_eq`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/TrivialEdges.lean#L696), with [`AllmanEtAl2023.TrivialEdges.edge_length_eq`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/TrivialEdges.lean#L487) and [`AllmanEtAl2023.TrivialEdges.root_length_eq`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/TrivialEdges.lean#L549) on `𝒩⁺`.

**E65. Line 252: "k or M varied" (loose).** "If the length of the edge between the blobs is held fixed but k or M varied, the CF varies over a line segment." Since `k` is discrete, only `M` gives a segment. The conclusion is proved on one `𝒩(k)⁺`, for `k ≥ 2`, small `ε₀` and all `ε′` near it: two nearby values share a nondegenerate segment of CFs ([`AllmanEtAl2023.InPassing.blobDistance_not_identifiable`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobDistance.lean#L563)). `k = 1` isn't formalized (Section 9).

**E66. Misprints in the proof of Lemma 3.** Line 208: "K_bc" for `K_cd`, in the TeX as well (its line 1087). In the manuscript only: line 208, "N \ {e0}" for `N′ \ {e₀}`, and line 207, "𝒩⁺, ℳ⁺" for `𝒩′, ℳ⁺`.

### Typos

**E34.**

- ECToBlob, proof of Corollary 4.3: `∪_{j′≠i} B_{j′}` should be `∪_{j′≠j} B_{j′}`.
- ECToBlob, §7: "Theorem 3.6" for Corollary 3.6; §10: "Theorem 4.2 and Theorem 4.3" for Corollaries.
- TOB-QMC, line 449: `QS^tree(T|N)` for `QS^tree(T_N)`. Lines 455, 459: `Pᵢ ≠ Pⱼ ≠ Pₛ ≠ Pₜ` must mean pairwise distinct. Line 462: `∪∪ Y` gives taxa, not 4-subsets: should be the union of `f_cover(P)`.
- TOB-QMC, line 535: `Z|Y` for `Z|W`.
- TINNiK, Appendix C: `Λ̃_n` for Proposition 5's `Λ_n`.
- Mitchell et al. 2019: "Proposition 16.7" for van der Vaart's Theorem 16.7, in one place.
- Rhodes 2020, the display before (2), and (2), (3): the sums over `C_v^i` run to `k` or `k(v)`, though there are `k - 2` blocks; (2) prints `v on p` for `P`.

## 5. Redundant hypotheses

The Lean statements leave these out, except where the last column says otherwise and in [`src/MainResults.lean`](../src/MainResults.lean), which keeps the papers' hypotheses.

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ECToBlob Theorem 4.1 | `A₁ ∩ A₂ = ∅`, `B₁ ∩ B₂ = ∅` | Only `A ∩ B = ∅` and the cover are used | [`ECToBlob.theorem4_1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section4.lean#L55) |
| ECToBlob Proposition 5.1, forward clauses | Degree 2 or 3 in blobs | Exchangeability holds on every network | [`ECToBlob.proposition5_1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section5.lean#L184), where the condition enters only through the generic clause |
| ECToBlob (3.3) | Nonempty blocks | | [`ECToBlob.equation3_3`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L86) |
| TOB-QMC Lemma 4 | That `B` is a tree, refines `T_N`, that `A` is an internal side and is not in `T_N` | Only [`RefinesBlob`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L48) is used | [`TOBQMC.lemma4`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L560) |
| TOB-QMC Corollary 1 | `B` binary (line 558) | Theorem 2 prints no "binary"; [`TOBQMC.theorem2`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L712) reads the quadripartition as the multipartition (Section 6) | [`TOBQMC.corollary1`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L591) keeps it and uses only [`IsUnrootedTree`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Defs.lean#L49) |

## 6. How the formalization reads the papers

### ECToBlob, TOB-QMC, TINNiK

- **Networks** are directed multigraphs with named arcs ([`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Defs.lean#L30)), rooted, with parallel arcs allowed. The papers' semi-directed networks are handled through the rooted networks they come from; TOB-QMC's `N⁻` is `N` with a root.
- **Unrooted trees** are families of sides of splits ([`Phylogenetics.IsUnrootedTree`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Defs.lean#L49): [`unroot`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Hierarchy/Defs.lean#L43) of a hierarchy). The tree of blobs `T_B(N)` is the family of nonempty proper sides of cut edges ([`Phylogenetics.treeOfBlobs`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Quartets.lean#L40)).
  Bridges to the graphs: [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.blobTree`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/TreeOfBlobs.lean#L127) is the graph with blobs contracted; [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.mem_treeOfBlobs_iff_blobTree`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/TreeOfBlobs.lean#L213) says the two agree on splits, and [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.blobTree_isTree`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/TreeOfBlobs.lean#L134) that it is a tree. The root of `blobTree` is not deleted. Allman et al.'s [`AllmanEtAl2023.Trd`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobDefinitions.lean#L422) is the reduced graph, and [`AllmanEtAl2023.Bridges.splits_Trd`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Bridges.lean#L353) says its splits are this family.
- **Induced networks**: `HasCutSplitOn Y A` says that one arc of the subgraph of ancestors of `Y` is the only arc of it crossing a vertex set that meets `Y` in `A`; [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.hasCutSplitOn_iff_separates`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Cuts.lean#L253) gives the domination form, and [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.hasCutSplitOn_iff_inducedNetwork_hasCutSplit`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Induced.lean#L857) proves it equal to the cut splits of the graph `inducedNetwork` (LSA restriction, then suppression). The later papers use this network, whose CFs are those of `N` on four taxa ([`Phylogenetics.NMSC.nmscCF_inducedNetwork`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Induced.lean#L76)). Allman et al.'s own results use their Definition 6, [`AllmanEtAl2023.induced`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/InducedNetwork.lean#L296), which keeps what lies above the LSA; the two have the same cut-edge splits ([`AllmanEtAl2023.Bridges.induced_hasCutSplit_iff`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Bridges.lean#L210)).
- **Quartet trees** are unordered pairs of cherries; [`Phylogenetics.Displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Defs.lean#L84).
- **The NMSC** on arbitrary networks is an order-free sum over histories ([`Phylogenetics.geneTreeDist`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Defs.lean#L94)), the product over vertices and arcs of the joint law of the forests at the bottom and top of each arc. One lineage per taxon. `ρ` is the inheritance correlation of TOB-QMC's supplement, in closed form as an exchangeable Pólya law ([`Phylogenetics.allocationWeight`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Defs.lean#L54), proved equal to the urn in [`Phylogenetics.allocationWeight_eq_urn`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Inheritance.lean#L232)). [`Phylogenetics.sum_geneTreeDist`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Basic.lean#L51) and [`Phylogenetics.geneTreeDist_support`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Basic.lean#L64) validate it: a law on binary rooted trees.
- **Sampling**: `Phylogenetics.iidProb dist n Ev` is the finite sum over samples, proved equal to the product measure ([`Phylogenetics.iidProb_eq_pi`](../src/Basic/Testing/IID.lean#L264)). TOB-QMC's almost-sure statement uses any probability space (`TOBQMC.IsGeneTreeSample`).
- **Test statistics** follow the implementation (MSCquartets, Mitchell et al.). The T1 p-value uses the plug-in `min(1, φ̂)` at all counts; small-count procedures of software are not modelled. Star: likelihood ratio by default, Pearson as `StarTest.pearson`.
- **Gene trees in ECToBlob's Theorem 8.2** are binary ([`ECToBlob.BinaryGeneTree`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L216)), which is what the proof and the NMSC give; ECToBlob's unrooted topological gene trees need not be. So the theorem covers the NMSC at any `ρ`, and, for Bon and BBC, the Displayed Tree model when all displayed trees are binary: on a cut split that model's CF is `(1,0,0)`, so the hypothesis `hpos` fails for Cauchy and CBC. It does not cover the Displayed Tree model on nonbinary networks (E37).
- **TOB-QMC's disjoint cover around an edge of a non-binary tree** (Definition 2) is read with the multipartition at the edge ([`Phylogenetics.multipartitionQuartets`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Defs.lean#L107)), since Theorem 2 is stated there.
- **[`CutExchangeable`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Quartets.lean#L67)** is the form in which ECToBlob's Theorem 3.1 uses its condition 2; [`ECToBlob.pendantChainInvariant_cutExchangeable`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L1363) derives it from the literal form, and [`ECToBlob.pendantChainInvariant_nmscCF`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L1395), [`ECToBlob.pendantChainInvariant_displayedCF`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L1436) prove the literal form for the models of Remark 3.3.
- **Parameters**: `MetricData` has lengths on all arcs and hybridization parameters; `IsMetric` requires positivity on tree edges, and `γ` strictly between 0 and 1 where a reticulation has two parents.
- **Assumption 6a** of TOB-QMC is the hypothesis `hBlob` of [`TOBQMC.consistency`](../src/TOBQMC/Consistency.lean#L131).
- **TOB-QMC's restriction `N⁻|Y`**: Lemma 4 is proved on `wedgeArcs Y`, the union of the wedge paths of `N` between taxa of `Y`, which lies below the LSA ([`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.wedgeArcs_subset_belowArcs`](../src/TOBQMC/Wedge.lean#L774)). Its cut edges with both sides nonempty are those of `HasCutSplitOn` ([`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.hasWedgeCutSplit_iff_hasCutSplitOn`](../src/TOBQMC/Wedge.lean#L900)). Parallel arcs and degree-two blobs are not suppressed: in a binary network this changes no split of a cut edge, which is not formalized. "Contains a 4-blob" is read as "no cut edge with a `2|2` split", the paper's equivalence at lines 379–382.
- **ECToBlob's Lemma 6.1** gives no domain for its bound. Lean states it on `0 ≤ η ≤ D < 1`, the values `(min P, max P)` reach, with the printed bound (E8).
- **TINNiK Theorem 4**: [`TINNiK.theorem4`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L661) uses the paper's levels ([`TINNiK.exists_levels`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L614)), which depend on unknown limits. [`TINNiK.theorem4_explicit`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L689) also proves it for one sequence `α_m = P(χ²₁ > log m)`, independent of the network and parameters, as ECToBlob's Remark 8.3 suggests. [`ECToBlob.remark83_tinnik`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark83.lean#L244) makes `β` explicit too.
- **`4 ≤ |X|`** in [`TINNiK.theorem4`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L661) is explicit in Lean and implicit in the paper: at `|X| = 2` the quartet distance is 0, and [`IsExactBuilder`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Metric.lean#L51) says nothing about that input.
- **Lemma 8.1** of ECToBlob is proved for functions of iid finite samples, with `δ` chosen first. This is the form its use needs, and is stronger in the order of quantifiers.
- **The T3 test**: [`Phylogenetics.t3Stat`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L55) is the minimum over topologies of [`t1Stat`](../src/Basic/Testing/Defs.lean#L63), and [`Phylogenetics.t3Var`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L62) the random variable of Mitchell et al.'s Proposition T3approx, whose squared distance to the three half-lines is [`Phylogenetics.QuartetTests.t3Var_eq_min_sqDistHalfLine`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L173) (a step they leave as "it can be shown"); at `Z = 0` their formula gives 0, harmless since `Z = 0` has probability zero. The sources differ at the centroid: Mitchell et al.'s null includes it, TINNiK's Fig. 2 caption excludes it, and its anomalous condition `q ≥ 1/3` counts it as anomalous. Lean follows each: [`Phylogenetics.IsT3Null`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/T3Test.lean#L50) follows Mitchell et al., `TINNiK.NoAnomalousQuartets` TINNiK. TINNiK's Theorem 4 says "using the cut test", so [`TINNiK.theorem4T3`](../src/TINNiK/T3.lean#L523) has no printed proof to follow, and takes the structure of the proof for the cut test.
- **TOB-QMC's combined search**: [`TOBQMC.tobqmcSearch`](../src/TOBQMC/BipartitionSearch.lean#L726) reads the tests as [`TOBQMC.tobqmc`](../src/Basic/Defs.lean#L258) does. This is with the cut test ([`TOBQMC.consistency_search`](../src/TOBQMC/BipartitionSearch.lean#L796)) and with T3 ([`TOBQMC.consistencyT3_search_of_noAnomalous`](../src/TOBQMC/BipartitionSearchT3.lean#L202)). It does not model the implementation at lines 133–137, which runs the star test on the minimizer only.
- **Counts of operations** are counts of what the papers' claims are about, tied to the definitions by `Finset.card` of what a step enumerates or by an instrumented run ([`Phylogenetics.NJ.ops_run`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/NeighborJoining.lean#L1976), [`TINNiK.count_bQuartetInference`](../src/TINNiK/Counts.lean#L709)), and are not running times on a machine model. [`TOBQMC.threeFixOneAlterOperations_le`](../src/TOBQMC/Counts.lean#L273) takes the LCA cost and the precomputation as parameters.
- **The displayed tree model** of ECToBlob is [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.displayedSides`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/DisplayedTreeModel.lean#L183) with [`ECToBlob.displayedTreeDist`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L406). Remark 3.2's "no impact on the score" holds for the raw probabilities of resolved topologies ([`ECToBlob.remark3_2_no_impact`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L606)), and under the convention of lines 289–293 each new quartet adds `1/3` ([`ECToBlob.quartetScore_displayedCF_of_resolution`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L619)), the reason for the remark's last sentence, [`ECToBlob.isBinaryTree_of_displayedCF_maximizer`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L541).

### Gusfield et al. 2007

- **Sites and rows.** Sites are a linearly ordered type, a row is a function to `Bool`, and `M` is a finite set of rows, since the paper assumes no duplicate rows (line 112). `M(C)` is the set of distinct restrictions, as the super-characters of line 245 are. The standing assumption that no column is constant is [`Gusfield2007.NoConstantColumn`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L74), a hypothesis of the results that use it.
- **Networks** ([`Gusfield2007.Network`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L129)) have no parallel arcs. [`Gusfield2007.Network.Derives`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L180) is a bijection from the leaves to the rows, since the network has "exactly n" leaves. A recombination node may be a leaf, as nothing at line 94 excludes it; this is what E44 is about. There is no bridge from these networks, which carry sites and sequences, to the rooted networks of the other four papers, and the modules import Mathlib only.
- **Crossovers.** A single crossover is a prefix of one parent followed by a suffix of the other, the prefix being a lower set, possibly empty or everything; a recombination that copies a parent can be dropped, so no minimum changes. `Gusfield2007.Crossover.pointwise` is a third model, the recombinations of Section 4.1 that model one back or recurrent mutation, which Corollary 3 needs: "S-min decomposable" can't mean multiple crossovers there, since Figure 8 refutes that.
- **Blobs.** A blob is the arc set of a class of recombination cycles under sharing an arc. "No blob shares a node with any other blob" (lines 135, 140) is [`Gusfield2007.Network.BlobsNodeDisjoint`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L224), a conjunct of [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsFullyDecomposedFor`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L257); [`Gusfield2007.Network.exists_blobsNodeDisjoint`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L1180) shows that no count depends on the convention. `T′` is the quotient of the nodes by the components of the arcs on cycles, a tree with no assumption on blobs ([`Gusfield2007.Network.blobbedTree_isTree`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L465)).
- **`T̄`** ([`Gusfield2007.IsTBar`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L433)) has every row at a leaf, every internal edge labelled, and no node of degree two, as lines 271–289 use it. Line 264's "each edge is labeled" can't hold with rows at leaves. It agrees with the split families of the library, which contain the singletons. With line 264's reading, `T̄` of [`topRows`](../src/Gusfield2007/Examples.lean#L100) has eight edges, which is why E42 quotes the split.
- **Theorem 4's form.** [`Gusfield2007.Theorem4Form`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L49) asks for an isomorphism of graphs from `T′` to `T̄` that respects the rows at the leaves, which is what "is necessarily the tree" means.
- **`R`, `F`.** [`Gusfield2007.R`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L291), [`Gusfield2007.RS`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L295), [`Gusfield2007.F`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L300), [`Gusfield2007.FS`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L305) are minima over networks on finite types, in `ℕ`. `sInf ∅ = 0` occurs only on an empty `M`, which is excluded. The haplotype bound [`Gusfield2007.haplotypeBound`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L114) is an integer, as printed, and is negative on `{10, 01}`. `d_oSPR` is in `ℕ∞`, as unreachable pairs would otherwise cost 0.
- **Trivial components.** Lean keeps both columns of `B` that a trivial component gives, where lines 245 and 281 drop one, so the second clause of Lemma 4 is for non-trivial components ([`Gusfield2007.lemma4`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L672)).
- **Theorem 10** is [`Gusfield2007.theorem10`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L756) for the rooted count; [`Gusfield2007.Theorem10Printed`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L562) is the literal statement, as a definition (E51).
- **"Efficient, counting the entry node"** ([`Gusfield2007.Network.IsEfficientAtEntry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1721)) isn't in the paper. It is the closest reading of lines 326–328 that makes lines 341, 342 true. The idea is in Gusfield's 2014 book, whose canonical ARGs are a stricter class (E42).
- **Galled networks.** [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsGalled`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L41) and [`Gusfield2007.Network.IsNonDegenerate`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L45) come from what the authors published around Huson and Klöpper 2007 (Section 2), and "tree edge" there means an arc into a node that isn't a recombination node.
- **Footnote 2** speaks of "the two edges labeled with those sites" in `T̄`, whose edges carry super-characters. [`Gusfield2007.footnote2`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure1.lean#L188) reads it as the edges of super-characters where the site has state 1, which matches `T`'s labels and the root `00000` of Figure 1.
- **"The haplotype of one is not tight"** at line 608 is unclear, and [`Gusfield2007.fig6_line608_theorem10`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L357) proves it for the whole matrix and for each component.

### Allman et al. 2023

- **The paper's objects.** The results as printed are on [`AllmanEtAl2023.IsBinaryNetwork`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Definitions.lean#L61), its Definition 1 verbatim, tied to the library's definitions by [`AllmanEtAl2023.isBinaryNetwork_iff`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Definitions.lean#L139). [`AllmanEtAl2023.Trd`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobDefinitions.lean#L422) is `T_rd(𝒩⁻)` as a labelled graph, and [`AllmanEtAl2023.Bridges.splits_Trd`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Bridges.lean#L353) says its splits are the library's [`Phylogenetics.treeOfBlobs`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Quartets.lean#L40). The topologies of Figure 4 are listed literally by `HasQuartetTopology` and `HasStarTopology` on [`AllmanEtAl2023.LabelledGraph`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobDefinitions.lean#L385).
- **The manuscript and the TeX.** Definition 6 follows the manuscript, with its third step that removes a stem ([`AllmanEtAl2023.induced`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/InducedNetwork.lean#L296)). The lengths of `𝒩(k)⁺` follow the TeX and Figure 6, and the manuscript's lengths are a misprint (E57). The two versions also differ in Definition 8's wording, with the same meaning; the manuscript's fuller proof of Theorem 1 (b), with `w`, `k₁`, `k₂`, Figure 5 and "only a single edge below v"; its misprints in the proof of Lemma 3 (E66); and more detail on the algorithm in Section 5. A comparison word by word found nothing else mathematical.
- **"Identifiable from `CF_Q`" (Corollary 1).** [`AllmanEtAl2023.readTopology`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Concordance.lean#L293), a fixed function of the triple, returns the topology of `T_rd(𝒩⁻_Q)` for generic parameters, with one exceptional set per network for all `Q`.
- **"Can be identified" (Theorem 3).** [`AllmanEtAl2023.CFInferred`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Concordance.lean#L319) is a predicate on the CFs alone: what [`readTopology`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Concordance.lean#L293) reads as unresolved, closed under the printed rule, whose third hypothesis is read off the CF of the third set. [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem3`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem3.lean#L510) says that it holds exactly on the B-quartets, for generic parameters; the direction from [`CFInferred`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Concordance.lean#L319) to B-quartet, which "identified" needs, is Theorem 2.
- **"Identifiable" (Theorem 4).** There is one function `F` of the CFs, chosen before the network, with `F(CF)` the splits of `T_rd(𝒩⁻)` off a null set of each network's parameters ([`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem4`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem4.lean#L103)). A function that may depend on the network would say nothing. [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem4_treeFromCF`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem4.lean#L86) names the function, [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem4_two_networks`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem4.lean#L111) is the pairwise form, where the null set may depend on the other network, and [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem4_iso`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem4.lean#L134) the labelled-graph form.
- **"Arises" (Proposition 1).** A network of Definition 1 with parameters of Definition 3. The proof gives more, `𝒩(k)⁺` for all large `k`: [`AllmanEtAl2023.NetworkNk.exists_params`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Proposition1.lean#L193).
- **Generic parameters.** [`AllmanEtAl2023.ForGenericParams`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Concordance.lean#L282) is the library's [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.IsGenericNull`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Defs.lean#L122), Lebesgue-null in the paper's coordinates, and it isn't vacuous ([`AllmanEtAl2023.not_isGenericNull_isMetric`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/GenericParams.lean#L70)). One exceptional set per network serves all namings of the taxa and all 4-sets, which is stronger than Theorem 1's statement and equivalent by a finite union.
- **Lemma 2** names the blob on `N` as the one containing the blob on `M`. **Corollary 2** has `Q ⊆ Y` explicit. **Theorem 2** has `n ≥ 5` as five distinct taxa. **Lemma 3** writes out that `α` is not one of `a, b, c, d`.
- **In passing.** `IsAboveLSA` and `IsBelowLSA` (in [`AllmanEtAl2023.BlobStructure`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobStructure.lean)) and `LiesIn` for "the blob gives a node" are ours. "6-blob when unrooted" in Figure 9R is read as six taxa determining a blob of the edges above them.
- **The forms for the later papers** are stated in another language, on subgraphs of a fixed network and by domination: [`AllmanEtAl2023.determines_of_subnetwork`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/SubgraphBlobs.lean#L479), [`AllmanEtAl2023.rule_core`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Basic.lean#L614), [`AllmanEtAl2023.lemma3_resolves`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Basic.lean#L240), [`AllmanEtAl2023.closure_complete`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Basic.lean#L724), [`AllmanEtAl2023.cfDetectable_generic`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CFDetectable.lean#L24). [`AllmanEtAl2023.corollary1`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Corollary1.lean#L67) doesn't imply [`AllmanEtAl2023.cfDetectable_generic`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CFDetectable.lean#L24) by its statement alone: [`readTopology`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Concordance.lean#L293) reads an all-equal CF as unresolved, while [`AllmanEtAl2023.CFDetectable`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CFDetectable.lean) asks for a non-cut CF there. So that one comes from the step of the proof on one 4-taxon network, [`AllmanEtAl2023.forGenericParams_inducedDetectable`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Corollary1.lean#L39).

### Corrections of statements

Listed here for reference: E2 (3.3); E10 and E11, Theorem 8.2, and its binary gene trees (E37); E22, the margin of supplement Lemma 4; E23, E26, supplement results; E31, TINNiK's MLE. Of results stated in passing or only cited: E35, Remark 8.4; E36, Remark 3.3; NANUQ's Theorem 36. Of Gusfield et al. 2007: E42–E44, Theorem 4; E45, line 373; E46, line 156; E47, line 180; E48, line 403; E49, line 554; E50, line 560; E51, Theorem 10. Of Allman et al. 2023: E55, Lemma 2; E64, the remark at line 252.

## 7. Departures from the papers' proofs

The last column but one says why, and says so plainly when a departure isn't forced. Mechanics and representation with the same content aren't listed: paths as sets of arcs, for instance, and a concrete value for "sufficiently long".

| Result | The paper's argument | The formalization's | Why | E-item |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ECToBlob Theorem 3.1 | Local objective `f̃_m`; (3.3) | Weighted by the block sizes; the claims on all maximizers hold in that form ([`ECToBlob.innerSum_le_of_isBlockAttachment`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L219), [`ECToBlob.quartetScore_le_pullTree_of_starPart_maximizer`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L287), [`ECToBlob.exists_splice_of_maximizer`](../src/ECToBlob/Section3.lean#L517)) | Gap: (3.3) is false, and so is the side claim at lines 497–500 for the unweighted objective | E2 |
| ECToBlob Remark 3.2 | "Arbitrarily resolved" increases the score | One chosen split of positive weight | Gap: a resolution of weight zero doesn't increase it | E1 |
| ECToBlob Corollary 3.6 | Equal sets of optima | Containment, by the gap to non-maximizers | Gap | E3 |
| ECToBlob Theorem 4.1 | The induced subnetwork has two 3-blobs, joined by chains of 2-blobs and cut edges | The same on `G̃₄`, without suppression, with degree counted by cut edges with a taxon beyond them ([`ECToBlob.IsTwoBlobs.exists_three_cuts`](../src/ECToBlob/Section4Funnels.lean#L1007), [`ECToBlob.IsTwoBlobs.exists_two_blob`](../src/ECToBlob/Section4Funnels.lean#L1109)) | Not forced. On `G̃₄` the arc from the root to the LSA can be a third cut edge | E4 |
| ECToBlob Proposition 5.1 | Quarnet by quarnet, "checking the argument" of Allman et al. | On `N` directly, for each 4-set, with the witness on the face where hybrid lengths are 0; "no anomalous quartets" read strictly | The first isn't forced: it avoids pushing this measure, with its atoms, through the map of parameters. The second is forced | E5, E36 |
| ECToBlob Theorem 8.2 | Per-edge bounds, Lemma 8.1 per edge, Corollaries 4.2, 4.3; `B ∼ β_n`; T1 threshold "exactly when"; Step 3 "precisely"; bounds `(α_n, 1-δ)` | The same structure. Repairs: one test per pair (`T ⊆ T₀`, edge of `T`) and `K` tests; [`falseEdge_witness`](../src/ECToBlob/FalseEdge.lean#L1044) in the trivial 4-blob case; a constant `c₀`; tightness at the centroid; the `χ²₂` bound for T1; a weaker Step 3 claim; only the lower bounds; degenerate nulls apart; `2k · min` for Bon and BBC. The statement is on binary gene trees, a narrowing and not a repair (E37). `quad` on a non-binary `T₀` uses the multipartition, and the late rule fixes a value when no `p_k < α` (E9); these fill what the paper leaves undefined | Gaps, except the count: `K` counts sides of edges, and shares of the failure probability differ, which is immaterial and not forced | E10–E17, E37 |
| TOB-QMC Lemma 1 (cited there, from Ané et al.; v5 prints no proof) | Their proof, from "two 3-blobs" (Allman et al.'s Lemma 1) to a cut edge | [`IsTreelike`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Quartets.lean#L49) says this directly | Not forced | — |
| TOB-QMC Lemma 4 | Attachments distinct, then suppressed `N⁻\|Y`, "thus" a 4-blob | On the subnetwork `wedgeArcs Y` without suppression; a 2\|2 cut edge would make two taxa attach through it | The "thus" is a gap. Skipping suppression isn't forced | E19 |
| TOB-QMC Corollary 1 | "Each side has two blocks" from `B` binary; the blob "by the setting" | From the edge not being in `T_N`; the blob is proved ([`TOBQMC.exists_blob_of_false_positive`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1.lean#L507)) | Gap: the printed reason is wrong | E20 |
| TOB-QMC supplement Lemma 4 | Margin from all three differences; strictness dropped, then claimed; `B*` a maximizer | Margin against the displayed quartet; strict bounds; `B*` the tree of Lemma 3, through a hypothesis | Gap for the first two. The hypothesis isn't forced | E22, E24 |
| TOB-QMC supplement Lemma 8 | Two disjoint paths from the blob root, a sequence of reticulations | A lowest reticulation, by a sink of the blob below `u` ([`TOBQMC.exists_reticulation_below`](../src/TOBQMC/SupplementLemma8.lean#L43)) | Not forced in the sense of the rule, though the printed route has gaps and needs Menger's theorem, which the library lacks | E26, E39 |
| TOB-QMC consistency | Theorem 1, Theorem 2, and TINNiK's Theorem 4 for correct tests on all four-taxon subsets | The same combination, with the levels of TINNiK's proof. Correctness is the proof step [`TINNiK.tests_correct`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L538), at parameters where [`TOBQMC.Level1.cfDetectable`](../src/TOBQMC/Level1CF.lean#L197) holds; on subsets treelike but not resolved by the tree of blobs this needs the new [`Phylogenetics.NMSC.nmscCF_lt_of_fork`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/TreeCF.lean#L451) | Gap: the cited statement concerns the output at generic parameters and `ρ = 0`, and the paper does not treat the third class | E21 |
| TINNiK Proposition 5 | From Mitchell et al. Theorem 3.1 (van der Vaart 16.7) | Direct CLT for this model; projections and minimum as the paper | Lean lacks differentiability in quadratic mean, local asymptotic normality, MLE consistency | — |
| TINNiK Theorem 4 | Under the alternative, the test law's limit `χ²₁` | A pointwise upper bound by `Z²`, and the least limit over the three lines. The centroid step follows the paper ([`TINNiK.isGenericNull_centroid`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L443)), though CF-detectability already gives it (Section 11). The null follows the paper, with E33's corrections | Gap: the limit gives no comparison at growing cutoffs, and the fitted line is random | E33 |

Proposition 12 (cited, for Remark 3.3) | Average over displayed trees, then "greater than or equal to" on a species tree; it prints no formula | The same average, with a strict quantitative bound by conditioning at the arc; hybrid arcs of length 0 by a limit. Its class-2 half isn't proved, since Remark 3.3 doesn't use it | Not forced. `TreeCF.lean` asks for positive lengths | — |
| NANUQ Proposition 9 (cited) | The source prints no proof and cites others | Our own proof, placing lineages at vertices and moving them up by the Markov property, stated on four taxa of a whole network with the CF as a marginal ([`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.proposition9`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/Proposition9.lean#L1471)). The sources it cites weren't read | No printed argument to follow | — |

| Neighbor joining (Studier, Keppler 1988) | A Lemma on rows of `S`, and a contradiction from two nodes | `Q = -∑ w_S g_S` over edges, and a side with at most `n/2` clusters, whose neighbours beat the pair ([`Phylogenetics.NJ.exists_QC_lt_of_not_nbr`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/NeighborJoining.lean#L785)) | Gap: their Lemma fails at polytomies | Section 2 |
| TOB-QMC Theorem 2 | "Following the proof of Corollary 1" | The steps of that proof, Lemmas 2 and 4, and not the corollary's statement, which concerns all subsets around an edge and says nothing on a cover | Not a departure from the argument: the route check records it | — |

Proposition 6 isn't used: it is the density of a law that [`cutTail`](../src/Basic/Testing/Defs.lean#L113) defines directly | Not a departure: the proof the paper cites, one level down | — |

The unrooted form points to a reference not read | E47 |

Theorem 1 (line 299) | Selects any node as root, and inflates central nodes with "other nodes if needed" | The root at a row of `M + S` ([`Gusfield2007.exists_network_of_pieces`](../src/Gusfield2007/Decomposition.lean#L90)), one of the choices the paper leaves arbitrary; the inflation is ours ([`Gusfield2007.Decomp.exists_inflate_net`](../src/Gusfield2007/InflateNet.lean#L637)), as the paper gives no construction | A specialization, not forced. It avoids a central root, whose label on the characters of `B` gives, by our reading, no sequence on the sites of `C`; line 416 says of this proof that "the label on v can be used". An arbitrary root isn't proved (Section 9) | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. line 135 | One gall, then "repeating this as needed" | At all shared tops of blobs at once, with one new node and edge each and the same labels ([`Gusfield2007.Network.separate`](../src/Gusfield2007/Blobs.lean#L672)) | Mechanics, with the same content | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. lines 187–190 | "Follows from Theorem 1" | From the proof of Theorem 1, with `T̄` for `M + S` rooted at `S` ([`Gusfield2007.theorem1_rooted`](../src/Gusfield2007/Decomposition.lean#L291)) | Forced: the statement of Theorem 1 puts `S` at a leaf, and a network can't be re-rooted through a blob | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. line 264 | "Applying the Perfect Phylogeny Theorem" | The theorem, then contracting the edges that aren't needed ([`Gusfield2007.IsPerfectPhylogeny.exists_normal`](../src/Gusfield2007/TreeContract.lean#L321)) | Forced: the theorem gives neither what line 264 says nor what lines 271 and 289 use | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. Lemma 4 (line 295) | Case 1, and "the three other cases are similar" | Two of the three take another argument ([`Gusfield2007.IsTBar.edges_incident`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L392)). "Pairwise incident implies a star" is [`Gusfield2007.exists_common_of_pairwise_incident`](../src/Gusfield2007/TreeCuts.lean#L199), and the lemma takes [`Gusfield2007.IsTBar`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L433) as a hypothesis, where the paper takes the tree it just built | Forced: the argument of Case 1 gives no contradiction there | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. Theorem 4 (lines 340–344) | The printed argument, on the splits of edges leaving a blob | The same, with hypotheses added ([`Gusfield2007.theorem4_splits`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L929), [`Gusfield2007.theorem4`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1310)), handling the entering edge too, which the proof doesn't mention. [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1699), [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_entry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1843) add the converse, which the paper doesn't state. Counting the entry node is the repair of Gusfield's 2014 book, in a weaker form (E42). `IsEfficientPerBlob` replaces `IsEfficient` in [`theorem4_splits`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L929) | Forced: the printed statement is false | E42–E44 |
| Gusfield et al. line 373 | "It is clear" | [`Gusfield2007.treeness_le_and_eq_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2333), with conditions. Its reasoning, counting edges with `T̄` having the most, is [`Gusfield2007.treeness_le_of_charSplits`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2192) once the splits are known | Forced: the printed claim is false | E45 |
| Gusfield et al. line 156 | "Invariant over all perfect phylogenies" | [`Gusfield2007.perfectPhylogeny_invariant`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2465), from [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1699), under `RegularArcs` and the degree condition; [`Gusfield1991.network_rooted_unique`](../src/External/Gusfield1991/PerfectPhylogeny.lean#L885) among reduced trees | Forced: the printed claim is false for site-free arcs | E46 |

| Gusfield et al. line 403, as an implication between conjectures | "Letting `S` be the ancestral sequence that appears in the optimal phylogenetic network" | [`Gusfield2007.rootedConjecture_imp_unrootedConjecture`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L554), by `absurd`: the rooted conjectures are false. On one `M`, [`Gusfield2007.minDecomposable_of_sMinDecomposable`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L110) adds a hypothesis | Forced: the step is false on one `(M, S)` | E48 |
| Gusfield et al. line 590, the rooted multiple-crossover conjecture | From the unrooted one, through line 403 | [`Gusfield2007.rootedConjecture_multiple_false`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L479) reads Figures 8 and 9 directly, with `S` a row | Forced, by the same step | E48 |
| Gusfield et al. line 411 | "Follows from results on the maximum parsimony problem using median networks", of Bandelt et al.: the decomposition at cut nodes of the median network (pp. 745–748) | From Corollary 3: the parsimony score doesn't change on re-rooting at a row of an optimal tree, so an optimal `S` can be taken in `M`, where `G_S(M) = G(M)` ([`Gusfield2007.minDecomposable_pointwise`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L168)) | Not forced. A route by cut nodes exists and wasn't built, for its cost: it needs the median network, its retract property, which that source cites, and the link from cut nodes to the components of `G(M)`, which neither paper writes, and which Dress et al. 2011 state for Buneman graphs (Section 11) | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. line 418, computing `F` | "Follows from Theorem 4", and the fact on the root | From the ancestral sequence of an optimal fully decomposed network ([`Gusfield2007.F_eq_sInf_roots`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L146)), then [`Gusfield2007.exists_interior_sum_rootCost_le`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L651), whose proof uses [`Gusfield2007.exists_sink`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L464); the other direction is the paper's construction ([`Gusfield2007.F_le_sum_rootCost`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L639)). Theorem 4 isn't used ([`Gusfield2007.F_eq_sInf_interior`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L696)) | Forced: Theorem 4 is false as printed, and its corrected form applies only to networks that an optimal fully decomposed network needn't be | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. Theorem 10 (lines 560–568) | With `R(M + S)`, through line 560's equality, then Corollary 2 | [`Gusfield2007.theorem10`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L756), with `R_S(M)`. For multiple crossovers, restricting to one site per class of identical columns ([`Gusfield2007.theorem10_multiple`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L575)); for `pointwise`, Corollary 3; for single crossovers, distinct columns | Forced: `R(M + S)` is a notation slip, and line 560 is false for identical columns | E50, E51 |
| Gusfield et al. Theorem 6 (lines 574–584) | Applies Theorem 10 to a network in which a row can be at an internal node, after the modification of line 576 | The modification in the form of its count ([`Gusfield2007.exists_tight_of_allVisible`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L826)), and trimming ([`Gusfield2007.Network.exists_derives_of_displays`](../src/Gusfield2007/NetworkOps.lean#L352)), since `Derives` has rows exactly at the leaves | The modification as printed has a hole (E53); the trimming is mechanics | E53 |
| Gusfield et al. line 554 | "Easy to prove", with `M` | With `M + S`: our own proof, as the paper gives none ([`Gusfield2007.incompatible_labelSet_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/GalledNetworks.lean#L175)) | Forced: the printed claim is false | E49 |
| Gusfield et al., lower bounds on `R` (Figures 5, 8, lines 404, 612) | By programs: beagle, multicross.pl | A verified search in the kernel ([`Gusfield2007.LabelSearch.lt_of_check`](../src/Gusfield2007/LabelSearch.lean#L277)), through [`Gusfield2007.Gen.card_le`](../src/Gusfield2007/Histories.lean#L71); [`Gusfield2007.Gen.split`](../src/Gusfield2007/Histories.lean#L313) for `R¹` of Figure 8, the two-region case of Myers and Griffiths' equations (1), (2), proved directly on a history rather than through their `r_l` | A program's run isn't a proof here. The arguments the paper gives by hand, Hudson–Kaplan and Song–Hein, are used where it uses them | — |
| Song, Hein 2005 (line 654) | Not read | The standard construction of a recombination graph from marginal trees ([`SongHein2005.R_le_sprLength`](../src/External/SongHein2005/Bound.lean#L1315)) | The source couldn't be read, so this can't be compared with its proof | — |
| Gusfield 1991, Semple and Steel 2003 | Not read | Standard arguments: a laminar family, and orientations of the splits | The sources weren't read, so these can't be compared with their proofs | — |

The proofs of Buneman's four-point theorem ([`Phylogenetics.FourPoint.exists_tree_of_isFourPoint`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/FourPoint.lean#L802), by induction on a pair maximizing the Gromov product) and of ASTRAL's dynamic program weren't compared with the published ones, which weren't read in full. The T3 theorems have no printed proof. TINNiK's estimation on the closed simplex ([`src/TINNiK/Statistics/Definitions.lean`](../src/TINNiK/Statistics/Definitions.lean)) is a correction of statement, E31.

[`docs/route_differences.tsv`](route_differences.tsv) lists every change of route, each with its reason. Those not in the table above aren't changes of argument. The `*` rows are definitions and basic facts that the papers use throughout without citing them: Allman et al.'s definitions, and that `T_rd` is a tree; Gusfield et al.'s line 139 and `G_S(M) = G(M + S)`. Some rows are for a Lean statement that takes as a hypothesis what the paper has at hand, or that is more general (`g:dom_unique`, `g:lem4`, `g:blobtree`). Some are for a statement kept as a definition, since it is false or open (`g:thm4_printed`, `g:treeness`, `g:thm10_printed`). And some are for what the proof uses silently, and Lean names (`g:thm1`, `g:thm4`, `g:thm4_splits`, `g:compute_F`, `g:thm6`), or for a label on two theorems (`a:claim:part-a`: [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem3_core_partA`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem3.lean#L358) and [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem3_partA`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem3.lean#L519)). `ecto:prop5_1` reaches the last step of statement (b) of Allman et al.'s Theorem 1, which ECToBlob doesn't cite by name, since it says only "checking the argument".
