This table is the output of [`scripts/Audit.lean`](../scripts/Audit.lean), whose last column of axioms is left out here: every row has only standard axioms. Three things to read it by. It lists 304 labelled declarations, some of them definitions, such as a network used in a counterexample, and results stated in passing as well as numbered ones. Allman et al. 2023 is both a paper and prior work, so its own rows list its results as prior work, as they do when a later paper's proof reaches them. And a row stops at the first result of prior work on a path, so it doesn't list what that result depends on.

The route check (`python3 scripts/route_check.py check docs/paper_routes.tsv --accept docs/route_differences.tsv`) confirms that every proof uses the results that the paper's proof cites, except the differences recorded in [`docs/route_differences.tsv`](route_differences.tsv), each with its reason: 236 results, 192 route differences, all recorded. It merges all the declarations that share a label, so `tinnik:theorem4`, for instance, is the union of [`TINNiK.theorem4`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L661), [`TINNiK.theorem4_of_rejects`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L574) and [`TINNiK.theorem4_nj`](../src/TINNiK/NJ.lean#L35); and its `*` rows also accept later papers reaching Allman et al.'s definitions. Its `paper_routes` rows for Allman et al. 2023 are checked against `route_check.py extract` on the arXiv TeX. The others are written by hand, since their sources have no TeX.

## 9. Not formalized

**ECToBlob, TOB-QMC, TINNiK and what they cite.**

- **Empirical claims**: the simulations and data analyses of these three papers, and measured running times. These aren't mathematical claims.
- **Running times on a machine.** What's proved are counts of operations (Section 6): [`Phylogenetics.NJ.ops_run_le`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/NeighborJoining.lean#L2024), [`TINNiK.tinnikOperations_nj_le`](../src/TINNiK/NJ.lean#L65), [`TOBQMC.card_threeFixOneAlter_total_le`](../src/TOBQMC/Counts.lean#L240), [`TOBQMC.threeFixOneAlterOperations_le`](../src/TOBQMC/Counts.lean#L273), [`TOBQMC.search_total_le_of_limit_le`](../src/TOBQMC/Counts.lean#L401), [`ECToBlob.Counts.ectoblobOperations_le`](../src/ECToBlob/Counts.lean#L827). LCA data structures aren't formalized, nor ASTRAL's running time.
- **That a heuristic finds an optimum.** No source claims this of TREE-QMC, and nothing here does. For ASTRAL, what's provable is proved: its dynamic program is exact on its search space ([`Phylogenetics.IsTraceback.isAstralOptimal`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/AstralDP.lean#L1103), [`TOBQMC.astral_refines_treeOfBlobs`](../src/TOBQMC/Astral.lean#L159)).
- **ECToBlob's Remark 3.4**: it cites results on duplication and loss models and states an open question. No such model is in the library.
- **ECToBlob's §10, beyond what's proved**: post-selection p-values, which it calls future work; the analogue of Assumption 1 for sum-of-pairs distances, which it cites, and a test for that data type (only the four-point facts on trees are proved: [`Phylogenetics.FourPoint.sumOfPairs_of_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/FourPoint.lean#L121)); and the Log-det distance it says one "might" use. Combining edge p-values under CBC should fail, by our sketch: only the combination step is in Lean ([`ECToBlob.Remarks.combineP_cbc_eq_one`](../src/ECToBlob/Remarks.lean#L804)).
- **ECToBlob's Theorem 8.2 for the displayed tree model on non-binary networks** (E37).
- **TOB-QMC's supplement Lemma 7 and Theorem 1 at arbitrary level**, which are false (E25); at level 1 they are Lemma 4 and Theorem 2.
- **TOB-QMC's implementation of the tests at lines 133–137**, the star test on the minimizer only (Section 6).
- **Ané et al.'s class-2 half** of Proposition 12, which nothing here uses.
- **TINNiK's extension to gene trees with missing taxa**, and NANUQ's.
- **The T3 test**: the limit law of its statistic at the singularity, which only tightness replaces, and Mitchell et al.'s density (checked numerically only, by a script not kept in the repository).
- **NANUQ beyond its distance**: NeighborNet, circular networks and splits graphs; its Theorems 15, 23, 34, Corollaries 16, 24, 31, 35, Lemmas 19, 21, 25–28, 32, 33, Proposition 30, and gene trees on subsets of taxa. [`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.theorem36_output`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/Proposition9.lean#L1623) has the output step as a visible hypothesis. [`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.networkClass`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/NANUQ.lean#L1238) and [`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.HasThreeTwoCycle`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/NANUQ.lean#L1196) aren't shown equal to the source's definitions by contraction, and the bound `CF ≥ 1/6` of part 2 of its Proposition 9, which nothing uses, isn't proved.

**Allman et al. 2023.**

- **`k = 1` in the remark on the distance between blobs** (E65): the bounds used need `k ≥ 2` and small `ε₀`, and the finer computation for `k = 1` isn't formalized.
- **The outline of an algorithm in its Section 5** (lines 240–251), and its claim of consistency, aren't formalized as such. That algorithm is TINNiK's, whose consistency is [`TINNiK.theorem4`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L661). Nor are the heuristics for edge lengths it cites at line 252.
- **Small remarks**: "leaves are the only 1-blobs" and the 3-blobs of a tree, and the counts of blobs in the caption of Figure 1. No proof uses them.
- The rest of what it says in passing is proved: the table in the [README of `src/AllmanEtAl2023/`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/README.md) lists it.

**Gusfield et al. 2007.**

- **Empirical claims**: Table 1, the simulation study of §5.4 and the eight counterexamples it reports (lines 660–670).
- **Line 381, the optimality count for galled trees**: that a reduced galled tree minimizes the number of recombinations, even against multiple crossovers. It rests on the source's Theorem 2.7 and its Section 8, which aren't formalized.
- **Line 590, Huson and Klöpper's Conjecture 1.** Their paper wasn't read, so whether the refuted conjectures are that one, as line 590 says, isn't checked.
- **Line 329 for `IsEfficientAtEntry`, without increasing recombinations.** [`Gusfield2007.exists_isEfficientAtEntry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Realizability.lean#L365) is existence only: a network with the fewest recombination nodes can have tree arcs in the networks at its central nodes. For the printed definition it is proved (E52). Theorem 7.3.2 of Gusfield's 2014 book may give it for canonical ARGs; we saw only the start of its proof.
- **The treeness inequality with merged blobs.** [`Gusfield2007.treeness_le_and_eq_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2333) asks that each blob have the sites of one component. For a blob merging two components, or with no site, the inequality isn't proved or refuted.
- **Line 299's root at an arbitrary node.** The construction of Theorem 1 has the root at a row ([`Gusfield2007.exists_network_of_pieces`](../src/Gusfield2007/Decomposition.lean#L90)). For the counts, a root at any node of `T̄` is covered ([`Gusfield2007.F_le_sum_rootCost`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L639)), through the sequence of [`Gusfield2007.exists_root_of_node`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L552) and Theorem 3. That the network's `T′` is `T̄` with the root at that node is proved only for the neighbour of a row ([`Gusfield2007.exists_realization`](../src/Gusfield2007/Realizability.lean#L327)).
- **`M_d`**: only `R¹(M_d) ≤ 3d` is proved ([`Gusfield2007.Md_R_le`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure10.lean#L394)), which is all that the paper claims and Theorem 11 needs.
- **The source's stronger forms**: one cycle in place of one blob (Gusfield et al. 2004), and the integer program and Algorithm 1 of Myers and Griffiths, and Hudson and Kaplan's algorithm for `R_M`, which Gusfield et al. don't use.

**Open: stated in Lean, and neither proved nor refuted here.**

- [`Gusfield2007.MinDecomposableOfForallS`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L118), line 425: if `M` is S-min decomposable for all `S`, then `M` is min decomposable (E48).
- [`Gusfield2007.Theorem10Printed`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L562), Theorem 10 with the unrooted count `R(M + S)` (E51).
- Whether [`Gusfield2007.theorem10`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L756) needs distinct columns for single crossovers: re-inserting a duplicate site can break the prefix-suffix form of a crossover (E51).

## 10. The papers in the literature

Two searches are reported here. The first, of 2026-10-31, is about ECToBlob, TOB-QMC and TINNiK, and "the paper" and "the three papers" in the subsections "Before the paper", "Concurrent work" and "Since the paper" mean those. The second, of 2026-11-01, is about Gusfield et al. 2007 and Allman et al. 2023 as primary papers, and has its own subsection, "The two earlier papers", before "Formalizations".

The search for the first three papers was made on 2026-10-31 in OpenAlex (works citing TINNiK, TOB-QMC, ECToBlob, Allman et al. 2023, Ané et al. 2024, Huber et al. 2025, "Beyond level-1", Rhodes et al. 2025, Xu and Ané 2023, NANUQ+, and the WABI paper of Dai and Molloy; keyword searches), Crossref (title searches; its `updates` filter for corrections), Europe PMC and PubMed, the bioRxiv API (versions, journal links), the arXiv API, GitHub's search API, the Archive of Formal Proofs, and web search. We kept every correction or counterexample found, and otherwise the works that state or claim a result about the tree of blobs, about which four-taxon information decides it, or about a method that starts from a tree of blobs with a stated guarantee. Empirical studies, reviews and work on other problems were screened by abstract and left out. Each statement below comes from the cited work's abstract or numbered statements, and we say where only the abstract was read. We could not read the full text of the Genome Research version of TOB-QMC (subscription), of BROOQS, DIMPLE and Justison et al. (bioRxiv answered HTTP 429), or of Rasschaert's preprint (SSRN answered HTTP 403). Citation indexes lag: OpenAlex lists one work citing ECToBlob and two citing TOB-QMC, so recent citing work may be missing. Crossref's deposited reference lists add BROOQS and Justison et al. for TOB-QMC, and Justison et al. for ECToBlob. A preprint's results are reported as claimed.

### Before the paper

- Gusfield, Bansal, Bafna, Song, [J. Comput. Biol. 14 (2007)](https://doi.org/10.1089/cmb.2006.0137): a decomposition theorem for phylogenetic networks that exposes "a maximal embedded tree structure". TOB-QMC cites it for the tree of blobs. It is a primary paper here: see Section 3.
- Solís-Lemus, Ané, [PLoS Genet. 12 (2016)](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pgen.1005896) (SNaQ, pseudolikelihood on quartets); Baños, [Bull. Math. Biol. 81 (2019)](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11538-018-0485-4) ([arXiv 2017-11-28](https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.10545); abstract only): "every cycle of size at least 4 and every hybrid node in a cycle of size at least 5" of a level-1 network are identifiable from gene tree quartets; Allman, Baños, Rhodes, [NANUQ, Algorithms Mol. Biol. 14 (2019)](https://doi.org/10.1186/s13015-019-0159-2) ([arXiv 2019-05-16](https://arxiv.org/abs/1905.07050); abstract only): level-1 inference from quartet hypothesis tests and a quartet intertaxon distance. TINNiK's introduction sets its algorithm against these level-1 methods.
- Allman, Baños, Mitchell, Rhodes, [J. Math. Biol. 86 (2023), 10](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00285-022-01838-9) ([arXiv 2022-05-06](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.03466)): "most features of the unrooted tree of blobs" are determined by the distribution of gene quartet topologies, with no assumption on the level, through a B-quartet inference rule. It names the difficulty the three papers meet in different ways: a B-quartet of the network can have a cut-edge split on its induced 4-taxon network, "the main obstacle". It is a primary paper here: see Section 3.
- Xu, Ané, [J. Math. Biol. 86 (2023), 12](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00285-022-01847-8) ([arXiv 2021-10-22](https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.11814)): the "distance split tree" built from average pairwise distances refines the tree of blobs, and at level 1 equals the tree of blobs refined to separate polytomies from blobs (Theorem 10). Its Conjecture 9 is taken up by a preprint under Since the paper. ECToBlob's §10 suggests applying its framework to distances.
- Solís-Lemus, Yang, Ané, [Syst. Biol. 65 (2016)](https://doi.org/10.1093/sysbio/syw030): species tree methods can be inconsistent under gene flow. Dinh, Baños, [Mol. Biol. Evol. 42 (2025), msaf049](https://doi.org/10.1093/molbev/msaf049) (published 2025-03-07; abstract only): under the NMSC, "including nonanomalous scenarios", ASTRAL can produce a tree "that does not correspond to any topology displayed by the true underlying network". ECToBlob cites both as the reason a statement about quartet optima on networks is needed (its [14, 37]); TOB-QMC cites Dinh and Baños, with Molloy, Durvasula, Sankararaman 2021 (its [12], [27]).
- Ané, Fogg, Allman, Baños, Rhodes, [J. Math. Biol. 88 (2024), 29](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00285-024-02050-7) ([bioRxiv 2023-08-21](https://doi.org/10.1101/2023.08.18.553582); abstract only): under common inheritance "4-taxon networks are never anomalous"; under independent and correlated inheritance simulations find "truly anomalous networks are rare" and many networks close to the anomaly zone. Source of the no-anomaly hypotheses of TOB-QMC and ECToBlob, and of the exchangeability of Section 2.
- Rhodes, Baños, Xu, Ané, [Adv. Appl. Math. 163 (2025), 102804](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aam.2024.102804) ([arXiv 2024-02-18](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11693)): circular orders of outer-labeled planar blobs are identified from 4-taxon subnetworks. Its Corollary 6.6 gives the tree of blobs under the displayed tree model, common inheritance, and independent inheritance with no anomalous quartets. Its Lemma A.1 classifies 4-taxon networks, and ECToBlob says of its Corollary 4.2 that it extends part of that lemma to more taxa.
- Huber, van Iersel, Jones, Moulton, Veenema-Nipius, [Bull. Math. Biol. 87 (2025), 136](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11538-025-01510-5) ([arXiv 2024-08-23](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12997)). Theorem 5.1, for binary semi-directed networks, which "does not put any restriction on the level": a nontrivial `A|B` is a cut-edge split exactly when `a₁a₂|b₁b₂` is a cut-edge split of the quarnet for all `a₁, a₂ ∈ A`, `b₁, b₂ ∈ B`. Corollary 5.6: the quarnets determine the blob tree. TOB-QMC's Lemma 2 is the forward direction, as TOB-QMC says.
- Frohn, Holtgrefe, van Iersel, Jones, Kelk, [J. Comput. Syst. Sci. 152 (2025), 103655](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jcss.2025.103655) ([arXiv 2024-09-09](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06034)). Lemma 1 sharpens Huber et al.'s theorem by fixing `a₁` and `b₁`. Theorem 5 reconstructs the tree of blobs of a binary network of any level in `O(n³)` time from `O(n³)` quarnet-splits, and Proposition 6 gives a lower bound of `Ω(n log n + ℓn)` quarnet-splits at level `ℓ`. For binary networks ECToBlob's Corollary 4.2 is this Lemma 1, as ECToBlob says: it "follows from Lemma 1 of [17], which depends on results in [22]. Our proof is considerably briefer and we relax the binary condition". ECToBlob's Theorem 4.1 goes further in two ways: the network need not be binary, and the partitions `A₁, A₂` and `B₁, B₂` are arbitrary.

Huber et al. and Frohn et al. work from exact quarnet information, with no statistical model. Both appeared after the TINNiK preprint and do not build on its algorithm.

What the papers add. TINNiK ([bioRxiv 2024-04-24](https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.04.20.590418)) turns the sketch of Allman et al. 2023 into an algorithm with a consistency theorem (Theorem 4) and writes "We know of no other proposed algorithm for tree of blobs inference from biological data"; ECToBlob and TOB-QMC both describe it as the first. TOB-QMC and ECToBlob add a different outline: an optimum of the quartet criterion refines the tree of blobs, and edges are then contracted by quartet tests. We found no earlier statement of that refinement result, and no earlier proof of TINNiK's Theorem 4. Section 2 lists the cited results the proofs use, including Mitchell et al. 2019 and Rhodes 2020.

### Concurrent work

- TOB-QMC and ECToBlob. First public versions: TOB-QMC [bioRxiv v1, 2025-11-06](https://doi.org/10.1101/2025.11.05.686850), under the title "Fast and accurate tree of blobs reconstruction under the network multispecies coalescent"; ECToBlob [bioRxiv v1, 2026-05-31](https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.05.28.728501). ECToBlob's statement: "When this work was well underway, we learned a different group was independently pursuing the same outline for tree of blobs inference", with its research dated to a residence at ICERM in Fall 2024, and thanks to Dai, Han and Molloy "for sharing their pre-publication work". TOB-QMC v5 does not mention ECToBlob; it thanks Ané, Allman, Baños and Rhodes for feedback.
  - Same result: an optimum of the quartet criterion refines the tree of blobs. TOB-QMC's Theorem 1 assumes the binary NMSC model and T-quartet-nonanomalous networks. ECToBlob's Theorem 3.1 and Corollary 3.6 assume only two conditions on the gene tree distribution, which its Remark 3.3 says hold for the NMSC with no anomalous quartets, for common inheritance and for the displayed tree model.
  - Same question by another method: the contraction step. TOB-QMC applies TINNiK's tests (ECToBlob compares with the T3 option) to `O(n)` four-taxon subsets around an edge and keeps the smallest p-value, with guarantees at level 1 (its Corollary 1, Theorem 2). ECToBlob applies the T1 test to the bipartition, quadripartition or multipartition sets of an edge, combines p-values under dependence, and contracts one step at a time; its Theorem 4.1 and Theorem 8.2 hold at any level. ECToBlob's §10 gives the authors' comparison of the two.
- Holtgrefe, Huber, van Iersel, Jones, Martin, Moulton, Squirrel, [Mol. Biol. Evol. 42 (2025), msaf067](https://doi.org/10.1093/molbev/msaf067) ([bioRxiv 2024-11-03](https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.11.01.621567); abstract only): level-1 networks from quarnets or alignments, "combinatorially consistent" on full triangle-free quarnet sets. TOB-QMC says it was released during its study and "creates candidate TOBs, through edge contractions, which is similar to TOB-QMC", from sequence data and with no statistical guarantee under the NMSC.
- Englander, Frohn, Gross, Holtgrefe, van Iersel, Jones, Sullivant, [bioRxiv 2025-04-24](https://doi.org/10.1101/2025.04.18.649493) (v4, 2026-07-04; preprint, no journal link in the bioRxiv API; abstract only): claims that under the Jukes-Cantor and Kimura 2-parameter models any two binary semi-directed networks displaying different quartets are distinguishable, so that the tree of blobs is identifiable at any level. Same question on sequence data. ECToBlob cites it.

### Since the paper

We found no erratum, retraction or published counterexample to a statement of the first three papers: Crossref's `updates` filter returned nothing for TINNiK, the Genome Research paper, or the cited papers we checked (Allman et al. 2023, Ané et al. 2024, Huber et al., Frohn et al., "Beyond level-1", NANUQ+, Rhodes et al., Xu and Ané, Dinh and Baños); Europe PMC lists no correction for TINNiK, Allman et al. 2023, Ané et al. 2024, Huber et al., "Beyond level-1" or NANUQ+, and PubMed lists none for the Genome Research paper. Among the works cited in this section, the only correction we found is that to Holtgrefe et al. 2025, for an omitted figure. The errors recorded in Section 3 are this audit's own.

Versions of the papers:

- TOB-QMC: bioRxiv v2 (2025-12-09) changed the title; v3 (2026-02-26), v4 (2026-04-06), and v5 (2026-05-15), the version audited. The supplement's internal date, February 26, 2026, is v3's posting date. Published as [Genome Research, doi 10.1101/gr.282203.126](https://doi.org/10.1101/gr.282203.126), accepted 2026-08-20, online 2026-08-28, [ahead of print on PubMed](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/42665444/). Its abstract keeps the restriction ("statistically consistent TOB reconstruction when the underlying network is level-1"). We have not read its text or supplement (subscription; the NSF Public Access Repository copy is embargoed to 2027-08-28), so we cannot say how its statements differ from v5.
- ECToBlob: the bioRxiv API lists v1 only and no journal link. Crossref's [listing of the SIAM Journal on Life Sciences](https://api.crossref.org/journals/3066-7410/works) (25 works, to 2026-10-14) does not contain it. No version newer than the one audited was found.
- TINNiK: no later version found. It is implemented, with ECToBlob and NANUQ+, in MSCquartets 3.3 (Rhodes, Allman, Ané, Baños, Mitchell, Wicke, [Bioinformatics, 2026-10-20](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btag775); [EcoEvoRxiv 2026-06-09](https://doi.org/10.32942/x24d5w); abstract only).

Work building on TINNiK:

- Allman, Baños, Rhodes, Wicke, NANUQ⁺, [Algorithms Mol. Biol. 20 (2025), 14](https://doi.org/10.1186/s13015-025-00274-w) ([bioRxiv 2024-11-03](https://doi.org/10.1101/2024.10.30.621146); abstract only): given a tree of blobs from TINNiK, resolves its multifurcations to level-1 structure.
- Kolbow, Kong, Solís-Lemus, InPhyNet, [bioRxiv 2025-05-07](https://doi.org/10.1101/2025.05.05.652278) (preprint; abstract only): merges independently inferred level-1 networks and claims that a statistically consistent pipeline can be built with it. TOB-QMC says its guarantee comes from partitioning taxa by the tree of blobs.
- Allman, Ané, Baños, Rhodes, "Beyond level-1", [Bull. Math. Biol. 87 (2025), 166](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11538-025-01545-8) ([arXiv 2025-04-29](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21116)): identifiability of classes of galled tree-child networks of any level, by first identifying the tree of blobs. Its Proposition 5.1 states tree of blobs identifiability for binary networks with generic parameters under the displayed tree model and both independent and common inheritance, by "straightforward modifications" of Allman et al. 2023. On the analogue of its Assumption 1 under common or correlated inheritance, ECToBlob's §5 says "We strongly suspect" it holds. Nevins, Claudio, Nguyen, SEQUoIA, [SIAM Undergrad. Res. Online 19 (2026)](https://doi.org/10.1137/26S1843342), turn that result into an algorithm using the NANUQ⁺ and TINNiK functions.
- Holtgrefe, Allman, Baños, van Iersel, Moulton, Rhodes, Wicke, [Bull. Math. Biol. 87 (2025), 168](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11538-025-01549-4) ([arXiv 2025-07-23](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.17308); [correction, 88 (2026), 26](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11538-025-01564-5): a production error omitted Fig. 12b, results unaffected): features of outer-labeled planar galled level-2 networks distinguishable from displayed quartets, and circular decomposability of an inter-taxon quartet distance (Theorem 4.7). Its outline for an inference method starts from TINNiK's tree of blobs.

Work citing TOB-QMC:

- Dai, Molloy, *Is level-1 blob reconstruction under the network multispecies coalescent easy?*, [WABI 2026, LIPIcs 390, 1:1–1:24](https://doi.org/10.4230/LIPIcs.WABI.2026.1) (published 2026-08-27, revised 2026-09-28; [bioRxiv v1 2026-06-10, v2 2026-09-21](https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.06.06.730607)). "Blob reconstruction" here means the inside of a level-1 blob, with the tree of blobs given as input. For binary semi-directed level-1 networks, NetCS finds the hybrid block and circular order of each blob of degree at least 5 (Lemma 1, Theorems 1–3); Corollary 1 states statistical consistency with `O(n log(n) k)` time and `O(n log n)` quarnet queries in its fast mode, matching Frohn et al.'s lower bound. Every statement assumes level 1, and the paper says nothing about treelikeness tests beyond level 1. Its proofs use TOB-QMC's Theorem 2 and the result it numbers Lemma 5 (v5's Lemma 4), and cite TINNiK's Theorem 4 for correctness of the tests on all 4-taxon subsets, the same reading recorded here as E21. The revised version's notice says the trees of blobs estimated in its experiment 3 "did not satisfy the properties of the protocol", that "a minor bug in the TOB reconstruction code" did not explain this, and that after a rerun the trees were "much more accurate" with overall conclusions unchanged. The notice does not say whether the bug touched TOB-QMC's own experiments, which are outside this formalization (Section 9). Its Appendix B.4 reruns TOB-QMC at TREE-QMC commit `eb3e140` (2026-09-18), whose merged commits change `src/tree_of_blobs.cpp`; the v5 and Genome Research experiments predate it. The theorems of TOB-QMC are not affected.
- Guang, Sapoval, Yan, Kessler, Nakhleh, DIMPLE, [bioRxiv 2026-10-18](https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.12.758494) (preprint; abstract only): divide-and-conquer inference "without an explicit restriction on network level", using a tree of blobs. No theorem is claimed in the abstract. Its README says "The workflow uses TOB-QMC, implemented in TREE-QMC v4.0.1".
- Arasti, Mirarab, BROOQS, [bioRxiv 2026-09-04, v3 2026-10-26](https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.08.31.748319) (preprint; abstract only): resolves a tree of blobs into a level-1 network; claims a proof of consistency under the NMSC for blobs of size 5 to 15 in its PQ-tree variant, and conjectures it for its spectral variant.
- Justison et al., [bioRxiv 2026-09-04](https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.09.01.748505) (preprint; abstract only): reports by simulation that SNaQ on non-level-1 networks "frequently recovers a tree of blobs compatible with the true network".

Other related work:

- Frohn, Holtgrefe, van Iersel, Jones, Kelk, [Ann. Comb. (2026)](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00026-026-00830-0) ([arXiv 2025-11-27](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22736); abstract only): sequence length sufficient to reconstruct binary level-1 networks under the CFN model. This is a finite-sample result on sequences at level 1. We found no such bound for the tests of the three papers.
- Holtgrefe, Huber, van Iersel, Moulton, [arXiv 2026-10-06](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07855) (preprint): claims level-3 semi-directed networks are encoded by their quinnets and that one known counterexample is the only obstruction for quarnets; restates Huber et al.'s Corollary 5.6.
- Cummings, Curiel, Currie, Kagy, Ranasinghe, Rhodes, [arXiv 2026-08-04](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03544) (preprint): claims features of level-1 networks identifiable from quintet concordance factors and not from quartets; its Proposition 4.1 claims a limit that holds for `m`-tet concordance factors of every `m`.
- Brits, Holtgrefe, van Iersel, Martin, [arXiv 2026-07-14, v3 2026-08-25](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12919) (preprint; abstract and statements scanned): claims that under Jukes-Cantor no network and tree induce the same leaf-pattern distribution unless the network is a tree with 2-blobs.
- Rasschaert, [SSRN 7292396 (2026)](https://doi.org/10.2139/ssrn.7292396) (preprint, DOI registered 2026-08-16; abstract read through Crossref, text not read). Claims a six-taxon binary level-5 counterexample to Xu and Ané's Conjecture 9: a distance split tree with an internal split that "no average-distance-equivalent metric semidirected network of any level" realizes by a cut edge. This is a claimed counterexample to a conjecture, not to a result of the three papers.

Treelikeness beyond level 1. TOB-QMC's oracle asks only whether the network induced on four taxa has a 4-blob. No work we read proves or refutes that this suffices at higher level, on all four-taxon subsets around an edge.

- TOB-QMC v5 proves it at level 1 (Corollary 1, Theorem 2), and from level-2 simulations suggests "a practical heuristic for higher-level networks". Its supplement of February 26, 2026 claims it for disjoint covers at any level (its Lemma 7, Theorem 1); that claim is false at level 2 (E25).
- ECToBlob's §10: "we know of no proof of a stronger result that would justify using the T3 test. Thus the formal justification for TOB-QMC is currently limited to level-1 networks [13, Theorem 2], although simulations suggest it may perform well beyond that case."
- Huber et al.'s Theorem 5.1, Frohn et al.'s Lemma 1 and ECToBlob's Theorem 4.1 hold at any level, but they ask whether a named split `a₁a₂|b₁b₂` is a cut-edge split of the quarnet, which is more than treelikeness: Frohn et al.'s count of a quarnet-split includes identifying "the specific split".
- Allman et al. 2023 and TINNiK work at any level and use resolved topologies with the inference rule, since "the induced network on a B-quartet may or may not have a 4-blob, and can even be a tree". They do not ask about four-taxon sets around an edge of a refinement.
- Dai and Molloy's WABI paper, "Beyond level-1" and Holtgrefe et al. do not address the question. The works read only by abstract, among them BROOQS, DIMPLE and Justison et al., do not mention it there; their texts were not read.

### The two earlier papers

The search was made on 2026-11-01 in OpenAlex (the works citing each paper, 60 and 22, screened by title and, where the title left it open, by abstract; keyword searches), Europe PMC (citing records, and its lists of corrections), Crossref (its `updates` filter for both DOIs, which returned nothing), arXiv, Open Library's search inside the book below, GitHub's repository search, the Archive of Formal Proofs' sitemap, and web search. We kept the works that state or claim something about the decomposition, its optimality, `T̄` or the lengths of cut edges. Citing works that use "blob" or the incompatibility graph only as a definition, empirical studies and surveys were left out. Each statement below comes from the cited work's abstract or quoted statements, and we say where less was read.

What we couldn't reach: SAGE's page for Gusfield et al. (HTTP 403) and Springer's for Allman et al. (a login redirect), so we saw neither journal's text, nor any notice of change on them. Huson and Klöpper 2007 is closed, and we read only the authors' errata. Bafna and Bansal's 2006 preprint wasn't found. The PDF of Gusfield and Bansal's RECOMB 2005 paper has an unreadable text layer. Gusfield's 2014 book was read only in snippets, and Baños 2019 not at all. Semantic Scholar and Google Books answered HTTP 429, so neither was used.

**Gusfield et al. 2007, before the paper.** From its own account, and the sources named.

- Hudson, Kaplan, [Genetics 111 (1985)](https://doi.org/10.1093/genetics/111.1.147) and Myers, Griffiths, [Genetics 163 (2003)](https://doi.org/10.1093/genetics/163.1.375): the lower bounds on recombinations that Theorems 10 and 11 use (Section 2).
- Wang, Zhang, Zhang, [J. Comput. Biol. 8 (2001)](https://doi.org/10.1089/106652701300099119), on perfect phylogenetic networks with recombination; Gusfield, Eddhu, Langley, [J. Bioinform. Comput. Biol. 2 (2004)](https://doi.org/10.1142/s0219720004000521), on galled trees, where two conflicting sites lie on a common cycle (Theorem 2.2) and reduced galled trees have the structure that Theorem 1 generalizes; Gusfield, J. Comput. Syst. Sci. 70 (2005), the root-unknown case; Bafna, Bansal, [IEEE/ACM TCBB 1 (2004)](https://doi.org/10.1109/TCBB.2004.23), on the conflict graph and lower bounds. Wang et al. and Bafna and Bansal were read only by title and the paper's description.
- Gusfield, Bansal, RECOMB 2005, LNBI 3500: the first statement of the decomposition theorem and of the Full-Decomposition Optimality Conjecture (lines 350, 393). Gusfield, [technical report, January 2005](http://csiflabs.cs.ucdavis.edu/~gusfield/fullydecomp.pdf): "That conjecture is still open". Its Theorem 3.1 treats a network in which every node is visible and no edge has more than one site, and line 554 says Corollary 2 "was proven with a more complex proof" there.
- Song, Hein, [J. Comput. Biol. 12 (2005)](https://doi.org/10.1089/cmb.2005.12.147), and Song, Ann. Comb. 10 (2006): ordered SPR, the upper bound of Theorem 11. The first paper's abstract, the only part we read, says its method "always gives the minimum number of recombination events if the right kind of rooted trees are used".
- Line 350 says that Steel (personal communication, 2005) pointed out a proof of Theorem 1 by Buneman graphs, that Wu (personal communication, 2005) verified it, and that a 2006 preprint of Bafna and Bansal has a shorter direct proof that "does not establish or emphasize the role of super-characters".
- Bandelt, Forster, Sykes, Richards, [Genetics 141 (1995)](https://doi.org/10.1093/genetics/141.2.743): median networks, and decomposition at their cut nodes, for line 411 (E53).

What the paper adds: the proof of Theorem 1 by super-characters with `T̄`, its polynomial construction, sufficient conditions for the conjecture (Theorems 5–10), and the refutation of the conjecture (Section 5.3, Theorem 11).

**Concurrent.** Huson, Klöpper, Lockhart, Steel, RECOMB 2005, LNBI 3500, 233–249: line 350 calls it "a related decomposition theorem", where the input is a set of trees, "subsequent to the development of Theorem 1". Not read. Huson, Klöpper, [RECOMB 2007](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-540-71681-5_15): galled networks and their decomposition, with a Conjecture 1 that line 590 says is false. Not read; the authors' errata corrects "non-degenerate", crediting Gusfield. Line 590 is the only account of that conjecture we have, so that the refutation applies to it as stated is unverified.

**Since: Gusfield's 2014 book.** Gusfield, [*ReCombinatorics*, MIT Press 2014](https://doi.org/10.7551/mitpress/9432.001.0001), read only in snippets from Open Library's search inside, and its [front matter](http://csiflabs.cs.ucdavis.edu/~gusfield/afewgoodpages.pdf). Chapter 7 is the full-decomposition theory, with "canonical" ARGs in place of efficient ones (Section 3, E42). Section 7.4.3 asks "Is There Always a Fully Decomposed MinARG?", and Chapter 11 is "Conditions for a Fully Decomposed MinARG" (spatial disjointness, component respect, visibility and tight lower bounds), with "Corollary 11.2.2 There is no fully decomposed MinARG for M, only if every MinARG for M has at least one Steiner node". Theorem 7.4.1 restates line 373 for canonical ARGs. Nothing we could see states the points of E45 or E49, and we found no other work that says Theorem 4 fails as printed, or that line 554 needs `M + S`. The book's chapters should be read in print before more is said.

**Since: the refutation, elsewhere.**

- Huson, Rupp, Berry, Gambette, Paul, [Bioinformatics 25 (2009), i85–i93](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btp217): "an example due to Yun Song shows that this property does not hold for more general rooted phylogenetic networks (Gusfield et al.,). As we illustrate in Figure 3, it is sometimes possible to save one reticulation by replacing two completely unlinked and unrelated configurations, each containing two reticulations, by one configuration containing only three reticulations." Their Figure 3 is an instance on clusters. They take it as a reason to restrict to decomposable networks, since this "may link together totally unrelated and arbitrarily distant parts of a phylogeny".
- van Iersel, Kelk, Rupp, Huson, [Bioinformatics 26 (2010)](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btq202) ([arXiv 0910.3082](https://arxiv.org/abs/0910.3082)): a decomposition theorem for level-`k` networks representing a cluster set, whose analogue for "network with k reticulations" fails, "as shown in [12], based on [7]", that is, Huson et al. 2009, on this paper.
- Kelk, Scornavacca, van Iersel, [IEEE/ACM TCBB 9 (2012)](https://doi.org/10.1109/TCBB.2011.128) ([arXiv 1103.1834](https://arxiv.org/abs/1103.1834)): "one cannot minimize reticulation number by optimizing independently over the connected components of the incompatibility graph", though for clusters from two trees the divide-and-conquer algorithm reaches the minimum (Theorem 6). Kelk, Scornavacca, [Algorithmica 68, online 2012](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00453-012-9708-5) ([arXiv 1108.3653](https://arxiv.org/abs/1108.3653)): "reticulation number is a global optimality criterion".
- In the same spirit, from trees rather than sequences: Baroni, Semple, Steel, [Syst. Biol. 55 (2006)](https://doi.org/10.1080/10635150500431197), as Weller and Zeh describe it: cluster reduction is safe for two trees. Albrecht, [arXiv 1408.3044](https://arxiv.org/abs/1408.3044) (preprint), claims a form of it for any number of rooted binary trees. Weller, Zeh, [arXiv 2508.21749](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.21749) (preprint), citing a manuscript in preparation by van Iersel, Jones, Landry, Linz, Weller, Zeh and Zhang, say it fails for four or more trees. These preprints disagree, we haven't read the manuscript, and we report them as claimed.

**Since: generalizations.** Dress, Huber, Koolen, Moulton, [SIAM J. Discrete Math. 25 (2011), 1902–1919](https://doi.org/10.1137/090764360) ([arXiv 1101.4927](https://arxiv.org/abs/1101.4927)): the blocks of the Buneman graph of a split system correspond one to one to the connected components of its incompatibility graph (Theorem 5.1), and a graph built on them is a tree (Theorem 6.1), generalizing that the Buneman graph is a tree exactly when the splits are compatible. They say the blocks "are also closely related to the 'blobs' defined in terms of directed graphs described by D. Gusfield et al.". This is the route by Buneman graphs that line 350 mentions. Read side by side, their reduced tree `T_Σ` looks like `T̄`; neither paper says so, and we didn't check it further. Lam, Gusfield, Sridhar, [SIAM J. Discrete Math. 25 (2011)](https://doi.org/10.1137/090776305), generalize the Splits Equivalence Theorem and the four-gamete condition to three-state characters; title and abstract only. Allman et al. 2023 cite this paper for the tree of blobs, and the later papers take it from there or from this paper. We found no later study that repeats the empirical claim of Table 1.

**Allman et al. 2023, versions.** arXiv [2205.03466](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.03466) has v1 only (2022-05-06), and differs from the PMC manuscript as Section 6 says. Crossref and Europe PMC list no correction. We couldn't reach Springer's text, so whether the journal has the misprints of E57 and E66 is unknown. Nothing we read cites those, the connectivity that Lemma 2 needs, or the unused sentence of E57.

**Allman et al. 2023, before.** Its own account: NANUQ, Baños 2019 and Solís-Lemus, Ané 2016 on level 1; identifiability from induced 4-taxon networks (Huber et al. 2018); and that the tree of blobs comes from Gusfield et al. 2007, with "a closely related notion" in Murakami et al., [Bull. Math. Biol. 81 (2019)](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11538-019-00641-w).

**Allman et al. 2023, since, on distances between blobs.** Line 252 ends "This identifiability question for larger networks will also be studied in a future work."

- Allman, Baños, Garrote-López, Rhodes, [Bull. Math. Biol. 86 (2024)](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11538-024-01339-4) ([arXiv 2401.06290](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06290), statements read): for level-1 networks with no 2-cycles, Theorem 5.12 lists what is identifiable. Not identifiable: pendant lengths, and "for 3-cycles, hybridization parameters and the lengths of the six edges in and adjacent to the cycle", among others. Proposition 5.5 covers `k = 1` of Figure 6's family as a general fact, and Propositions 5.7–5.11 give the lengths of cut edges at larger cycles, by cases. So the metric question is settled at level 1, in their setting.
- Allman, Ané, Baños, Rhodes, "Beyond level-1" (above; [arXiv 2504.21116](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.21116)): "the theorems do not claim identifiability of the lengths of cut edges", though "A-4len can be applied to any cut edge uv whose endpoints are not incident to a blob". Ané et al. 2024's Theorem 4 replaces any 2-sub-blob by a single edge, at any level, leaving the quartet CFs unchanged. We found nothing else on the lengths of cut edges above level 1.

We found no work that restates Lemma 2 or the proof of Theorem 3, so nothing later corrects them. The citing works we screened otherwise cite Theorem 4 or the definitions.

### Formalizations

We found no other formalization of any of the five papers, or of Huber et al. 2025, in any system, nor of `T̄`, the Buneman graph, perfect phylogeny, the four-gamete test or ancestral recombination graphs. Searches covered GitHub (repository and code search, by language), the [Archive of Formal Proofs](https://www.isa-afp.org/) sitemap, OpenAlex and web search; they see public work only.

- [vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics) (Lean 4, public, Apache-2.0, created 2026-10-10, by the user who requested this formalization): the multispecies coalescent, Kingman's coalescent on forests with Tavaré's formula, rooted metric level-1 networks and their network coalescent, and the concordance factors of a cycle (Baños 2019). This project imports it, pinned at commit `9948de9`, for its definitions and general results. Before that, it adapted the library's hierarchy kernel and Kingman modules from commit `06f684e` ([`docs/ADAPTED_SOURCES.md`](ADAPTED_SOURCES.md), [`docs/COALESCENT_SOURCES.md`](COALESCENT_SOURCES.md)). Its term-based level-1 network coalescent isn't used, and the network coalescent on arbitrary networks, first written for this project, has moved there.
- [vltanh/lean4-camus](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-camus) (Lean 4, public, created 2026-10-10, same owner): the CAMUS dynamic program for level-1 networks (Willson, Warnow, [Bioinformatics 2026](https://doi.org/10.1093/bioinformatics/btag245)) and the statistical steps of that paper. Surveyed for this project ([`docs/CONFIGURATION.md`](CONFIGURATION.md)), not imported.
- [vltanh/lean4-adr11](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-adr11) (Lean 4, public, created 2026-10-04, same owner): Allman, Degnan, Rhodes 2011, identifying the rooted species tree from unrooted gene trees under the multispecies coalescent. Not used here.
- [SauersML/Descent](https://github.com/SauersML/Descent) (Lean 4, public, created 2026-08-05): "A Lean 4 formalization of genetic theory", with coalescent modules (`Descent/Coalescent/`); a code search of it for "multispecies" returns nothing. Not used here.
- Poindexter, [Zenodo 2026-08-04](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21802297) (preprint; abstract only), claims a Lean 4 proof of confluence of a saturation procedure for rooted triples. [Paureel/rooted-forest-frontier](https://github.com/Paureel/rooted-forest-frontier) (created 2026-10-16) concerns rooted maximum agreement forests of two trees, and its README says its argument "is not an end-to-end machine-checked runtime theorem". Neither treats networks or the coalescent.
- Coq/Rocq, Isabelle, Agda: GitHub's language-filtered repository searches for "phylogenetic", "phylogeny" and "coalescent" returned nothing. Code search found only incidental uses, of which the closest is [CharlesCNorton/sciuridae-verified](https://github.com/CharlesCNorton/sciuridae-verified), a Rocq formalization of squirrel taxonomy. No entry name in the [Archive of Formal Proofs](https://www.isa-afp.org/) sitemap contains "phylogen" or "coalesc"; we did not search the entries' contents.

## 11. What's next

### Open directions

- **Treelikeness beyond level 1.** ECToBlob's §10 says of the T3 test: "we know of no proof of a stronger result that would justify using the T3 test", and that the formal justification for TOB-QMC stops at level 1; Section 10 finds no published work that settles this. The question: for a network of any level and a refinement `B` of its tree of blobs, does every edge of `B` not in the tree of blobs have a non-treelike 4-taxon set among those around it?
- **Assumption 1 under correlated inheritance.** ECToBlob's §5 says "We strongly suspect Assumption 1 also holds for the NMSC model with common or correlated inheritance". For binary networks at generic parameters, "Beyond level-1" (Proposition 5.1, Section 10) extends the arguments of Allman et al. 2023 to common inheritance, `ρ = 1`, for tree of blobs identifiability, and leaves other `ρ` aside. So what is open is `0 < ρ < 1`, and `ρ = 1` on non-binary networks with ECToBlob's measure. [`AllmanEtAl2023.cfDetectable_generic`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CFDetectable.lean#L24) here covers only `ρ = 0`. [`Phylogenetics.geneTreeDist`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Defs.lean#L94) includes `ρ`, and the equalities of the forward clauses are proved for all `ρ` ([`Phylogenetics.NMSC.cut_alternatives`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Exchangeability.lean#L550), [`Phylogenetics.NMSC.trivial_uniform`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Exchangeability.lean#L771)); the strict inequality of (5.2) is the no-anomaly hypothesis. What is needed is the generic clause: a nonvanishing witness for each 4-taxon network, for each `ρ`.
- **Rates.** All consistency statements here are asymptotic, with no rate. The proof of Theorem 8.2 runs on bounds per test, and could run on one event per quartet (below), so a non-asymptotic statement would follow from a concentration bound on each test, with constants depending on the distance of the CF from the null. What stands in the way is the non-regularity at the centroid and the boundary (E11, E13).
- **Theorem 8.2 for Cauchy and CBC on the boundary.** E11 shows these two variants need positive alternatives on null quartets. The T1 test's own null excludes the boundary in Mitchell et al., while (5.2) does not. A test that behaves at `(1,0,0)` would remove the hypothesis, as would a finite value of the Cauchy transform at `p = 1` (E11). An assumption that excludes the boundary would too, but it would drop the Displayed Tree model from these two variants, since a quartet with a cut-edge split has CF `(1,0,0)` there.
- **Coarsening, when Remark 8.4 fails.** E35 settles the remark, and [`ECToBlob.Coarsening.limitTree`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark84.lean#L822) is what the algorithm tends to. What should the algorithm do on a non-binary network when `T₀` isn't a resolution? A test that sees the false edge in E35 would have to use quartets beyond those around it.
- **The no-anomaly assumption.** ECToBlob's §10 would like to drop it. For its T1 test this is done, as it says, by the test of the line alone ([`ECToBlob.Remarks.consistency_line_nmsc`](../src/ECToBlob/Remarks.lean#L566)); it is easier than T1, since the centroid is interior to the line, so E13 disappears. The step that remains is the resolution: without the assumption, an optimum of the quartet criterion need not refine the tree of blobs, and ECToBlob asks for an algorithm that uses the CF entry that differs from the others: "Developing an algorithm to give a resolution of the tree of blobs based on that fact would be valuable". We found no work that does this.

- **How much does full decomposition cost?** Theorem 11 says `F¹ − R¹` is unbounded for single crossovers. Line 666 says "we do not know how robust these empirical results are in other ranges of data, or to multiple-crossover recombination, or when there is a fixed ancestral sequence". We found nothing on this in the works of Section 10, and Gusfield's 2014 book, whose Section 7.4.3 and Chapter 11 are about this, should be read first.
- **[`Gusfield2007.MinDecomposableOfForallS`](../src/Gusfield2007/Conjectures.lean#L118).** Line 425's "when M is S-min decomposable for all S, then M is min-decomposable" is unproved here. [`Gusfield2007.fig6_hG_necessary`](../src/Gusfield2007/Counterexamples.lean#L252) shows why it can't be got one root at a time.
- **[`Gusfield2007.Theorem10Printed`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L562), and distinct columns for single crossovers.** [`Gusfield2007.theorem10_multiple`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L575) drops distinct columns. What stays open is `H(M + S) = R¹(M + S)` as printed, and the single-crossover form with duplicates, where interleaved duplicate sites raise `R¹` of a component.
- **Theorem 4 at the entry node.** [`Gusfield2007.exists_isEfficientAtEntry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Realizability.lean#L365) is existence only, and line 329's "without increasing the number of recombinations" for this definition is open here. The proof of Theorem 7.3.2 of Gusfield's 2014 book should be compared. Also open: the root at an arbitrary node of `T̄` in line 299, with `T′` equal to `T̄` (Section 9).
- **Treeness when a blob merges two components.** [`Gusfield2007.treeness_le_and_eq_iff`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L2333) covers networks in which every blob is one component's. Whether treeness is still at most the number of edges of `T̄` when a blob holds two components, under the same structural hypotheses, isn't known here.
- **One cycle.** Gusfield et al. 2004's Theorem 2.2 says two conflicting sites lie on one recombination cycle. 2007 needs only one blob, which is what's proved, and the source's argument deletes arcs without saying why the result is still a network for `M`. Is the stronger claim true?
- **`T̄` and the tree of blobs.** A remark that is unverified: on paper, not in Lean, and read by no independent checker. For a binary network, `T̄` of all the splits of its displayed trees should be its tree of blobs, at any level. A split `A|B` of `T̄` is compatible with every displayed split, so every displayed tree shows `a₁a₂|b₁b₂` across it, so by [`AllmanEtAl2023.InPassing.isTreelike_iff_forall_displays`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/InPassing.lean#L513) and [`ECToBlob.theorem4_1`](../src/ECToBlob/Section4.lean#L55) (or Huber et al.'s Theorem 5.1) it is a cut-edge split. Conversely a cut-edge split is in every displayed tree and is a trivial component. A script tried it on 3,000 random binary networks with at most four reticulations; the script isn't kept in the repository. Under the displayed tree model `T̄` would then be an exact estimator. Under the coalescent, where every pair of gene-tree splits is eventually incompatible, is anything left of this?
- **Distances between blobs above level 1.** Level 1 is settled in Allman et al. 2024 (Section 10). Above it: which cut edges have identifiable lengths, and which functions of the lengths are identifiable? [`AllmanEtAl2023.InPassing.blobDistance_not_identifiable`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobDistance.lean#L563) is the 4-taxon case for `k ≥ 2`.

### Simpler proofs

Each of these is the argument of the formal proof, so it is checked in Lean. We found none of them published, in the search of Section 10.

- **ECToBlob Theorem 4.1, in domination language.** An arc `e` induces the split `a₁a₂|b₁b₂` on a quartet exactly when `e` dominates `a₁, a₂` (every root path to them uses `e`) and `b₁, b₂` are not below it, or the reverse ([`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.hasCutSplitOn_iff_separates`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Cuts.lean#L253)). So a cut edge of a quartet is a statement about each taxon separately. The paper's minimal choice of quadruple stays; the funnel step becomes the comparability that E4 finds unstated: replacing one taxon by another of its block gives comparable sets of cut arcs, by four cases on how two arcs are oriented, ordered when they dominate a common taxon. This saves the funnels, the graph `G̃₄`, Figure 2 and the "two 3-blobs" remark: about 300 lines against about 1,070. An earlier version of the formal proof did this (in the repository's history); the formal proof now follows the paper.
- **Allman et al. Lemma 3, likewise.** Since `e₀` is a cut on `Y \ {α}` and not on `Y`, the taxon `α` is below `e₀` and not dominated by it, and an arc resolving `{a,b,c,α}` on `Y \ {d}` compares with `e₀` by [`AllmanEtAl2023.order_of_isBelow_of_dominates`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/CutOrder.lean#L61), in four cases. Components and the cycle through `A, K_ab, e₀, K_cd` do not appear. It takes about 126 lines against about 1,600 for the source's route. An earlier version of the formal proof did this (in the repository's history); the formal proof now follows the source. Smaller changes also remain possible within the source's route: `C` as a minimal subset of all of `P₁ ∪ P₂ ∪ P₃ ∪ P₄` closing up `e₀` must leave `K_ab` at `x`, so `P₁` itself is the path from `α` to `C`; and `y` reaches `α ≠ d`, so `P₃` can run straight to `y`, with no `z`.
- **TINNiK's Theorem 4: the centroid.** The paper proves `CF ≠ (1/3,1/3,1/3)` for generic parameters by a separate analyticity argument. It follows from CF-detectability: strictly cut and non-cut CFs each have two different entries. This removes that paragraph of the proof, and also the need for a separate generic set. The formal proof of Theorem 4 takes the paper's step; [`TINNiK.tests_correct`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L538) takes the shortcut, [`TINNiK.noCentroidCF_of_cfDetectable`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L384).
- **Allman et al. Theorem 1, part (b).** Instead of one parameter with three distinct CFs, take each pair of quartet trees separately. The reduction shows that the display trees of a non-treelike 4-set show at least two different quartets, so at a display tree with long arcs one of the pair has CF near 1. Non-cut then holds generically with no mixture in `γ`. This is a sketch; the formal proof follows the source's mixture.
- **Allman et al. Theorem 1, the choice of `γ`.** One explicit point suffices: all lengths 3 and `γ = 1/3`. Each endpoint CF is within `exp(-3) < 1/4` of a vertex, so the mixture has entries near `1/3`, `2/3`, `0`, with no genericity at all. An earlier version of the formal proof did this; it now follows the source.
- **Allman et al. Theorem 1, the contradiction.** With no blobs: `e₁` and `e₂` dominate a common taxon, so they are ordered, and in four cases by direction the arc nearer the blob has `a` on the `b` side in both deletions. An earlier version of the formal proof did this.
- **Allman et al. Corollary 1.** On `N` directly: statements (a) and (b) hold for a 4-set of any network, since domination off `D` sees the four taxa alone and CFs are analytic in `N`'s coordinates. This needs neither the induced network, nor the map of parameters, nor the identity of CFs. An earlier version of the formal proof did this (in the repository's history); the formal proof now follows the source, through [`AllmanEtAl2023.induced`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/InducedNetwork.lean#L296).
- **Allman et al. Theorem 2.** Take a side `A` of the tree of blobs with two taxa of the set: the two B-quartets constrain `A` on `a, b, c, d, e`, and 32 cases leave only `A` separating `a, e` from `b, c, d`, which is the contradiction. No node of the tree of blobs is needed. An earlier version of the formal proof did this.
- **ECToBlob Theorem 8.2, one event.** One event per quartet, Lemma 8.1 applied once to all null quartets, a joint tightness bound at the centroid and growth under the alternative give one `δ` and `c₀` that make every combined test on every possible tree come out right together. So E16 disappears, with no count `K` and no bound `C(s,4)`. And [`ECToBlob.falseEdge_witness`](../src/ECToBlob/FalseEdge.lean#L1044) alone gives a quartet in the multipartition of `T₀`, which serves all types, so Corollary 4.2 isn't needed. An earlier version of the formal proof did this; it now follows the paper.
- **ECToBlob Theorem 3.1.** Only the splice is needed of the attachments at lines 495–497: [`Phylogenetics.displays_splice_iff`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Splice.lean#L159) gives its inner sums directly.
- **Rhodes Theorem 2.** `{x,y,u,v} ∈ Q` holds exactly when no side contains `u, v` and avoids `x, y`, so count such pairs directly and split them by node, with no case split on the shape of the quartet.
- **TOB-QMC's ASTRAL corollary.** Every binary gene tree occurs almost surely, so all binary trees are in the search space; there's no need for the probability of a bipartition.
- **TOB-QMC's supplement Lemma 8.** A lowest reticulation of the blob has only bridges below it, in any phylogenetic network.
- **The T3 test at the singularity.** Non-collapse needs no limit law: the T3 statistic is at most the T1 statistic, which is tight on the null, and the p-value is at least `½ χ²₁(10 t)`, since `sin² α₀ ≥ 1/10` on `[0,1]`.
- **NANUQ's Theorem 36.** Explicit levels prove it directly ([`ECToBlob.remark83_nanuq_fixed_beta`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark83.lean#L156)), with no levels built from the unknown `d`s. The formal [`External.AllmanBanosRhodes2019.theorem36`](../src/External/AllmanBanosRhodes2019/Proposition9.lean#L1607) keeps the source's route.
- **Neighbor joining.** The side with at most `n/2` clusters is shorter than Studier and Keppler's two-node contradiction, and it covers polytomies.
- **Remark 3.3's displayed tree model.** It is the limit of `ρ = 1` as all lengths grow ([`ECToBlob.abs_nmscCF_sub_displayedCF_le`](../src/ECToBlob/Remark33.lean#L461)), which also justifies the star convention, since a polytomy gives exactly `1/3` at all lengths.
- **TOB-QMC Lemma 3, the second observation.** Lines 489–492 restrict to one taxon per block and say a refinement's gain "would be amplified after reattaching". No restriction is needed: a maximizer resolves every 4-set in distinct blocks, since adding one split that resolves one more gains a positive CF on `X` directly, and that the result is binary then follows from the standard fact that a tree is binary exactly when it resolves every 4-set ([`Phylogenetics.isBinaryTree_of_forall_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Detach.lean#L177), [`Phylogenetics.IsBinaryTree.exists_displays`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Tree/Splits.lean#L589)). An earlier version of the formal proof did this (in the repository's history); the formal proof now follows the paper. Similarly, the contradiction at lines 472–486 adds nothing to the direct transformation it contains.
- **TOB-QMC Lemma 4.** Read on the rooted induced subgraph, with no wedge paths: for ⇐, a blob arc does not dominate the taxon below the reticulation, and an arc away from the blob has the whole blob below it or two taxa in distinct blocks joined outside it; for ⇒, descend from the top of the blob to an arc with exactly two taxa below it. An earlier version of the formal proof did this (in the repository's history); the formal proof now follows the paper, through wedge paths and the bridge [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork.hasWedgeCutSplit_iff_hasCutSplitOn`](../src/TOBQMC/Wedge.lean#L900).
- **TINNiK Proposition 6.** Computing the strip probabilities in rotated Cartesian coordinates, with Fubini in place of differentiating under the integral, and taking `G₄, G₅` as reflections of `G₃, G₂` at `-μ`, gives the same `d/dx Gᵢ` and needs no domination argument. An earlier version of the formal proof did this (in the repository's history); the formal proof now follows the paper.
- **Allman et al. Theorem 3.** The first pass to one taxon per component isn't needed: removing a taxon or applying Lemma 3 covers every case. Checked in a scratch Lean file, outside the library.
- **Wilks-type limits for a trinomial.** The papers cite these limits; for a trinomial there is an elementary proof. One estimate, `|(1+v) log(1+v) - v - v²/2| ≤ 4|v|³` for `|v| ≤ 1/2` ([`Real.abs_entropyExcess_sub_le`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/EntropyExcess.lean#L50)), gives each likelihood ratio statistic as a sum of such terms, so each is uniformly close on bounded sets to a continuous function of the whitened frequencies, and an extended continuous mapping theorem, proved from the portmanteau theorem on open sets (`Phylogenetics.Trinomial.tendstoInDistribution_of_closeOnBalls`), finishes. This does not use van der Vaart's Theorem 16.7. At the centroid, Pearson's statistic is exactly `Z₁² + Z₂²` ([`Phylogenetics.Trinomial.pearsonS_centroid`](../src/Basic/Testing/Trinomial/Whiten.lean#L425)); the T1 test at the centroid is handled separately (E13).

- **Gusfield et al., the lower bounds of Figure 8.** The paper runs a program to show that a component needs two multiple-crossover recombinations, since one recombination node is a galled tree. A shorter argument: with one recombination node `x`, the incompatibility graph is bipartite. Colour a site on one side of the cycle by its state at `x`, and a site on the other side by the opposite: two incompatible sites then get different colours, and sites off the cycle are compatible with all. So a triangle of incompatible sites forces two recombination nodes, and each component of Figure 8 has one, as do those of [`Gusfield2007.evenRows`](../src/Gusfield2007/Examples.lean#L81). Checked on paper, and the triangles by script; the formal proof uses a verified search ([`Gusfield2007.LabelSearch.lt_of_check`](../src/Gusfield2007/LabelSearch.lean#L277)).
- **Gusfield et al., line 411.** Re-rooting at a row of an optimal tree, and Corollary 3, give that every `M` is min decomposable for recombinations that model mutations, with no median network ([`Gusfield2007.minDecomposable_pointwise`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L168)). Checked in Lean. It isn't the route line 411 points to, which stays to be built (below).
- **Gusfield et al., existence of `T̄`.** The Splits Equivalence Theorem applied to the character splits with the singleton splits gives `T̄` directly, with no contraction of the Perfect Phylogeny Theorem's tree. A sketch; the formal proof contracts, as line 264 suggests ([`Gusfield2007.IsPerfectPhylogeny.exists_normal`](../src/Gusfield2007/TreeContract.lean#L321)).
- **Allman et al. Theorem 1 (b), the 2-blob step and Lemma 1.** Neither is needed: a reticulation in a 2-blob passes the same deletion test as any other, since domination sees through 2-blobs, and "a single edge below `v`" follows from the `2|2` split against an unresolved `T`. Checked in Lean, in [`AllmanEtAl2023.exists_reduction_aux`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Reduction.lean#L673), which the later papers use; the numbered result [`AllmanEtAl2023.theorem1_b`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Theorem1b.lean#L716) follows the paper.
- **Allman et al., blobs through suppression.** "Determines" is equivalent to "no side of a cut edge away from the blob has two of the taxa" ([`AllmanEtAl2023.BlobsPass.determines_iff_noTwoInSide`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobsPass.lean#L38)), which makes the passage of blobs through Definition 6 short. Checked in Lean.
- **Allman et al. Proposition 1, the bound on `CF_{ab|cd}`.** The bound alone has a shorter proof, by a potential on the event that the lineages diverge, with no conditional masses or species trees. An earlier version of the formal proof did this (in the repository's history); the formal proof now follows the paper.

- **Gusfield et al. 2004, Theorem 2.2, in the form 2007 cites.** No contraction or deletion: if no recombination node breaks `{i, j}`, only three pairs occur; if one does, its parents differ at both sites, and tracing states up gives a cycle through each site's arc and both arcs into that node, and these cycles share an arc. This avoids the source's unproved claim that an arc can be deleted. It is the Lean proof, [`GusfieldEtAl2004.exists_isBlob_of_incompatible`](../src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004/SameBlob.lean#L123).
- **Reduced galled trees.** Lemma 2.3 and Theorems 2.4, 2.5 of the source go by the arrays `W1, W2, W3`, one root and single crossovers. A threshold on a line through the gall's nodes covers the three arrays at once, for any root and crossover model ([`GusfieldEtAl2004.exists_threshold`](../src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004/GalledTree.lean#L327)). It is the Lean proof.
- **The remark on the distance between blobs at `k = 1`.** It has a closed form. With `q = e^{−ε}` and `r = e^{−M}`, on `𝒩(1)⁺`, `CF_{ab|cd} = 1 − q/2 − q²/6 + q(1−q) r/6`: cases on the lineages of `a, b` at the hybrid node, and three lineages always give `1/3`. So the values for fixed `ε` fill an open segment of length `q(1−q)/6`, and it moves continuously with `q`, so segments for nearby `ε` overlap. On paper, not in Lean, and it is what [`AllmanEtAl2023.InPassing.blobDistance_not_identifiable`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/BlobDistance.lean#L563) lacks at `k = 1`. Allman et al. 2024's Proposition 5.5 gives non-identifiability around any 3-cycle in general.

### The formalization

- **Reading the 2014 book.** [`Gusfield2007.theorem4_entry`](../src/Gusfield2007/Uniqueness.lean#L1843) and the book's Theorem 7.3.3 should be compared in print, with the book's definition of "most treelike" against E45, and whether its ARGs allow the arcs of E43.
- **`T̄` and the Buneman graph.** A proof that `T_Σ` of Dress et al. is `T̄` would connect [`src/Gusfield2007/`](../src/Gusfield2007) to a published canonical construction, and would give another proof of Theorem 1 and of [`Gusfield2007.isTBar_unique`](../src/Gusfield2007/SuperCharacters.lean#L264).
- **`R¹(M_d) = 3d`.** Only `≤` is proved ([`Gusfield2007.Md_R_le`](../src/Gusfield2007/Figure10.lean#L394)). On paper, the composite bound of Myers and Griffiths gives `≥`: restricted to one copy of the six sites, a single-crossover network keeps only the recombination nodes with their crossover inside that copy, and `R¹ = 3` on each of the `d` disjoint copies. In Lean, [`Gusfield2007.Gen.split`](../src/Gusfield2007/Histories.lean#L313) would need to keep the crossover model.
- **[`src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004/`](../src/External/GusfieldEtAl2004).** Theorem 2.7 of the source, the recombination count of a galled tree, which line 381 uses for optimality, isn't formalized.

- **Line 411 of Gusfield et al. by cut nodes.** [`Gusfield2007.minDecomposable_pointwise`](../src/Gusfield2007/SufficientConditions.lean#L168) goes by re-rooting and Corollary 3, which isn't the route line 411 points to (Section 7). That route would build the median network of `M`, with its retract property (which Bandelt et al. cite from Bandelt 1984 and van de Vel 1993), and Bandelt et al.'s decomposition at cut nodes, and would add what neither paper writes: that the cut nodes separate the components of `G(M)`. Another route, also unbuilt, goes through Theorem 9: a majority of three rows creates no new incompatible pair, so that labels in the median network respect `G(M)`. Both are sketches. Neither has been built, because of their cost.
- **A bridge between the two kinds of network.** [`Gusfield2007.Network`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean#L129) carries sites and sequences, and the library's [`Phylogenetics.RootedNetwork`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/Network/Defs.lean#L30) carries lengths and inheritance, and nothing connects them. The blobs and trees of blobs of the two could be compared, by forgetting the labels. [`Gusfield2007`](../src/Gusfield2007) imports Mathlib only, so this would be a new module.

- **Other inheritance.** [`AllmanEtAl2023.corollary1`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Corollary1.lean#L67) is at `ρ = 0`. [`Phylogenetics.NMSC.one_sub_exp_le_nmscCF`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics/blob/9948de9b3cba330596424efd2dca9617be2efb0c/Phylogenetics/NMSC/Witness.lean#L687) and the analytic parametrization hold for all `ρ`, and the mixture does too, so the same proof may reach the generic clause at other `ρ`: see the open direction above.
- **Non-binary networks.** [`TINNiK.theorem4`](../src/TINNiK/Basic.lean#L661) takes binary networks. [`AllmanEtAl2023.exists_reduction_of_blobDegree`](../src/AllmanEtAl2023/Reduction.lean#L951) should allow blob vertices of degree at most 3 and no trivial 4-blob on any 4-set; a trivial 4-blob has the centroid CF at every parameter, so some such condition is needed.
- **Generality to reach.** Non-binary gene trees in Theorem 8.2 (Section 6, E37).
- **Contributions to other libraries.** [`src/Basic/ForMathlib/`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib) holds what could move to Mathlib: the error function ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Erf.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/Erf.lean)), the second-order expansion of `x log x` ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/EntropyExcess.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/EntropyExcess.lean)), the maximum of a multinomial likelihood ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/GibbsInequality.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/SpecialFunctions/GibbsInequality.lean); Mathlib has Gibbs' inequality for `klDiv`), zero sets of analytic functions ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/AnalyticZeroSet.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/AnalyticZeroSet.lean)), stability of maximizers under perturbation ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/FiniteOptimization.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Analysis/FiniteOptimization.lean)), strip probabilities and the orthonormal change of coordinates for two independent unit normals ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Probability/GaussianPlane.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Probability/GaussianPlane.lean); Mathlib has rotation invariance as `stdGaussian_map`), the `χ²₂` tail and Gaussian interval bounds ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Probability/GaussianTail.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Probability/GaussianTail.lean)), and an extended continuous mapping theorem ([`src/Basic/ForMathlib/Probability/Transfer.lean`](../src/Basic/ForMathlib/Probability/Transfer.lean)). What is general to phylogenetics is already in the library [`lean4-phylogenetics`](https://github.com/vltanh/lean4-phylogenetics), which keeps what is standard to the field; [`src/Basic/README.md`](../src/Basic/README.md) says what here could still go there.
- **Reuse of [`src/External/`](../src/External).** The rule and Lemma 3 of Allman et al., the forward direction of Huber et al.'s Theorem 5.1 and exchangeability under the NMSC are stated for arbitrary rooted networks, and the quartet metrization of Rhodes for arbitrary unrooted trees. [`src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019/`](../src/External/MitchellAllmanRhodes2019) has the limit law of the likelihood ratio at a cut model. [`src/External/Gusfield1991`](../src/External/Gusfield1991), [`src/External/SempleSteel2003`](../src/External/SempleSteel2003), [`src/External/MyersGriffiths2003`](../src/External/MyersGriffiths2003), [`src/External/HudsonKaplan1985`](../src/External/HudsonKaplan1985) and [`src/External/SongHein2005`](../src/External/SongHein2005) have the classical bounds and structure theorems for recombination networks, on the sequence-labelled networks of [`src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean`](../src/Gusfield2007/Defs.lean), which imports Mathlib only. These are for other formalizations citing the same sources.
